# Android implementation status

**Current Pixel data incident, October 27:** Running the newly added app instrumentation task against the main application caused Gradle/UTP cleanup to uninstall it. Reinstallation confirmed **0 points instead of the prior 13**, with zero visits/open sessions and recording idle. The 13 points have not been recovered; no backup was found among this task's saved artifacts. The owner confirmed that no existing history export is available, so there is no available recovery source. Earlier preservation statements below describe historical checks, not the current device state. The app's instrumentation target is now the separate `mapQa` build (`com.bontecou.whereday.mapqa`), and CI uses `connectedMapQaAndroidTest`. Rendering tests assert that isolated package identity. Never run application instrumentation against the owner's main package.


This checklist tracks the full [implementation plan](../docs/android-implementation-plan.md). A passing foundation test does not prove full phone parity. No phase is declared complete yet.

## Phone launch work still open — November 2

Most core phone features have implementations. The following work still prevents calling the app a release candidate:

- Finish adaptive Map/Timeline/visit-inspector acceptance, which the owner requires at launch. Map/place-directory, gallery, editor and replay state now have scoped configuration-recreation coverage. Full process-death, navigation, clear-data and accessibility acceptance remains required. Complete navigation, clear-data, process-death, live-window/hinge, keyboard and accessibility behavior need acceptance beyond the scoped recreation tests.
- Reconcile the remaining current iOS workflows against the frozen baseline. Selected reference fixtures and source hashes do not establish complete functionality parity.
- Configure and validate production maps/place search/road routing, including the approved Geoapify integrations, actual network behavior, consent, attribution and failure paths.
- Provision the individual lifetime Play product and purchase verification service; validate checkout, restore, pending/refunded/revoked ownership, reconciliation and every paid export/schedule/webhook path. Android myself.md claim eligibility and implementation remain open.
- Complete real calendar Save/Cancel, the installed Automate walkthrough, production webhook delivery and optional analytics deployment/retention/consent acceptance.
- Validate recording and Visits only against labeled physical traces, battery/Doze/OEM behavior, interrupted sessions, permission changes and reboot. Complete physical photo-grant/large-library and release performance/storage testing, including a second OEM and supported OS versions.
- Complete localization/accessibility/motion acceptance, privacy/legal and dependency review, protected release signing, remote CI, Play declarations/listing and a staged field beta on the production-configured candidate.

Wear OS is deferred until after the phone launch. Its independent recording and sync are not phone-launch blockers. Provider choices, analytics consent policy, Visits only and the adaptive-launch decision are settled; their implementation and acceptance requirements remain tracked below.

## Baseline and choices

The owner approved an individual lifetime purchase for Android on October 27, 2026. Do not build or advertise a family entitlement service for the phone launch. Client billing and the local verification service are implemented below; real Play setup, deployment, reconciliation and sandbox acceptance remain unfinished.

The starting iOS checkout is commit `65365fc02c8383d4f4f15e0785f6062564605b3b` plus the pre-existing working-tree edits present on October 26, 2026. Those edits were preserved; [ios-baseline.json](ios-baseline.json) records hashes for 115 source, string and configuration files. Kotlin/Compose, API 29 minimum, API 36 target and a Google-services fused location adapter are implemented. MapLibre with opt-in OpenFreeMap tiles is the initial renderer/provider candidate. Provider and product decisions requiring external configuration remain open.

## Evidence collected on October 26 2026

- `:domain:test`: 24 JVM tests passed for dwell/departure/gaps, outliers, corrections, inference, country-local days, midnight exclusion and interval/DST rules.
- `:export:test`: 10 JVM tests passed for supported import round trips, correction metadata, format field choices, OwnTracks units, XML/GPX structure and extension namespace/fields, GeoJSON coordinate order, CSV edge cases, overnight filters, filename safety and stable import identity.
- `:data:connectedDebugAndroidTest`: 19 isolated Room/repository/settings/analysis tests passed on the attached Pixel 7 running Android 17: concurrent starts, cached/poor/duplicate fixes, saved-place dwell transaction, interrupted session end time, full-history/equal-timestamp paging, session/inferred deletion protection, schema 1→2 migration, and country-day pagination/manual-stay updates.
- The recorded first-run e2e replay passed 22 steps on the Pixel, including normal precise-location and notification prompts and entry into the journal. Two idle checks warned about small changing pixels; screen identity and stable action controls passed.
- The latest build passed the saved navigation replay: map controls → Export → JSON preview → Timeline → Settings, with destination checks throughout. There were 15 passing checks/actions plus an echo, no failures, errors or skipped steps. Three idle gates warned about small changing pixels; destination-specific controls and the JSON result key passed. The native route renderer was also inspected visually. This is not full motion/performance or accessibility acceptance.
- A live Pixel recording remained a `location` foreground service with the screen locked. After owner unlock, 13 saved GPS points were visible; Stop returned to idle. This is basic field evidence, not proof of battery budget or unattended restart behavior.
- The latest native-map build passed Android assembly and lint with zero errors. Dependency-update and style warnings remain. ARM64 ELF load segments and APK native-library ZIP alignment pass 16 KB checks. The merged release manifest excludes the debug diagnostics provider.
- Aggregate-only diagnostics on the installed latest build report 13 points, no visits, no open session, requested recording false and runtime recording false. The Pixel was left idle with its history preserved.

Argent's live accessibility bridge sometimes returned a stale Compose snapshot. Resetting the Pixel-scoped inspection connection restored current descriptions; the replay runner successfully resolved the recorded first-run controls. Do not treat a stale live read as an app verdict.

Automatic approval review rejected copying the on-device location database to the Mac. That action was not performed. Aggregate-only debug diagnostics and synthetic instrumentation databases are the QA approach.

- Room schema 2 adds the timestamp/identity pagination index without changing historical rows. A separate disk-backed one-million-point Pixel instrumentation test passed: all 1,000,000 fixes contributed to the result, and only 2,500 sampled points reached the map window. The measured debug window read was 8,755 ms. This proves bounded point collection and complete counts, not release latency/memory/battery acceptance.
- The outing-details replay passed 28 actions/checks plus its prerequisite echo: open exact outing, rename, save, reopen to prove persistence, restore default name. All selectors are semantic, including `scroll-to` below the embedded map. Two small-pixel idle warnings remain; no coordinate or raw-gesture exceptions remain.
- Country Days domain coverage includes repeated points, country-local dates across travel, unresolved/outlier/future fixes, manual overlap, filtered date bounds, DST/midnight exclusion and iOS CSV provenance/CRLF. The installed app truthfully excludes its 13 geographically unresolved points; manual stays work offline. Automatic lookup was added in the following work; earlier evidence predates that build.

- Country-stay e2e replay passed 47 actions/checks plus an echo: open from Settings, create a stay, inspect manual date provenance, preview the CSV header, reopen for editing, confirm deletion, and return to the empty state. One small-pixel idle warning remained. No raw coordinates or raw-gesture exceptions remain in the saved test. The temporary stay was removed.
- A 64-second 1080×2400/30 fps recording of the country-stay replay is saved locally at `.argent/recordings/screen-recording-2C061FDH200CJN-1793060043462.mp4` (untracked evidence, not a release asset). Sampled frames were inspected for layout and modal transitions; this does not establish frame-time performance or a full-speed motion review.
- Moving a corrected visit or undoing a coordinate correction invalidates geographic country/time-zone metadata. Name-only edits retain valid metadata; a domain regression covers both paths.

- Room schema 3 adds attempt timestamps, exact-coordinate lookup cache and a persistent analysis/rate-limit record. Pixel regressions validate both schema 1→3 and 2→3 upgrades while preserving visits, corrections, country metadata and points. The analysis never holds a database transaction across a network wait.
- Seven synthetic country-analysis tests passed on the Pixel: multi-page batches/cache reuse, missing-result retries across runs, opt-out/resume, in-flight cancellation, edits while awaiting lookup, clear-data invalidation and a rate limit surviving a failed request/new engine. A separate concurrent-settings test proves unrelated preference updates cannot re-enable network geocoding. Repository tests total 11; with analysis and preferences the selected data suite totals 19.
- Both platform tests passed on the Pixel: five public coordinate/time-zone fixtures entirely offline, and a real Android Geocoder country lookup for the public New York fixture combined with its bundled geographic zone. No personal journal coordinates were sent for this QA. Boundary initialization measured 7,363 ms; the test process reported 198,233 KB total PSS and 205,497,056 Java heap bytes used with a 268,435,456-byte heap limit. The index is released after analysis. These measurements motivate further low-memory/release profiling; they do not prove a release budget.
- App assembly/lint and 24 domain/10 export regressions pass after resolver integration. ARM64 and x86_64 native LOAD/ZIP alignment checks pass for eight libraries, including Zstandard JNI. The reusable verifier is `scripts/verify_native_page_sizes.py`; CI now invokes it and defines an API 35 synthetic database emulator job. Remote CI has not run.
- The updated manual-stay replay again passed 47 actions/checks. The recorded analysis-disclosure fragment passed eight actions/checks after narrowing its ambiguous action label to an anchored selector. It cancels before any geocoder request and requires Country Days/network-enabled/idle state.
- Geographic boundaries and JNI licenses ship as assets and are accessible from Settings. [Third-party notices](THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) records the source, licenses and egress behavior; a complete dependency notice audit remains a release gate.

- The recorded geocoding opt-out replay passed 22 actions/checks plus its prerequisite echo. It disables geocoding, proves the Country Days disabled explanation, restores the original enabled state, and proves that explanation disappears. Three small-pixel idle warnings remained; selectors/scroll goals are semantic. No personal coordinate analysis was started.
- The shell-only, debug-only `/geography` probe loaded the boundary index in the actual app process with the map present, without calling any geocoder. It reported 5,176 ms loading, 245,932,720 / 268,435,456 Java heap bytes used, and 447,746 KB total PSS. This is **too close to the heap limit to accept for release**. The index is released afterward, but a smaller regional/on-demand loading strategy and low-memory validation are required before calling geographic lookup release-ready.

## October 27: bounded geographic loading

- Replaced the worldwide in-memory Timeshape index with a compact worldwide envelope index and individually loadable original polygons. The generated assets retain all 444 source zones, 1,320 polygons, 2,074 rings and 8,075,438 original coordinate pairs. Every source coordinate/ring is compared against the packaged data in a passing fidelity test. Another passing test compares complete match sets with the original engine at 160 worldwide fixtures, including borders, poles, date-line points and large geometries. Three loader tests cover holes/boundaries, ambiguity/cache accounting and corrupt data.
- The runtime widens the exact source float coordinates to double, as the original engine does. It uses the same Esri intersection predicate, caches polygons under a conservative 4 MiB estimate, and never assigns a time zone from an envelope alone. The build-only generator writes a reproducible source SHA-256 and provenance manifest. Timeshape/protobuf/compression/JNI dependencies are no longer on the Android runtime classpath. The source SHA-256 is `d3603ba598966015feb5f3cfb7b986f124e2abbb55a8a1929f5e3dbf37b68834`.
- Both updated Pixel platform tests pass, including an actual system lookup for the public New York fixture and eight offline fixtures spanning New York, Phoenix, Puerto Rico, Singapore, Sydney, Moscow, Alaska and Winnipeg. Index preparation measured 35 ms; cold per-fixture queries ranged from 12–572 ms. At the end of the isolated fixture run, Java heap used was 31,429,344 bytes and total PSS 62,749 KiB, versus the previous 205,497,056-byte heap / 198,233-KiB PSS sample. No forced GC was used.
- The actual app probe with the map loaded performed public New York/Moscow checks in 669 ms and reported 37,864,848 / 268,435,456 Java heap bytes used and 252,755 KiB total PSS. The prior worldwide-index probe reported 245,932,720 bytes used and 447,746 KiB PSS. This removes the observed near-heap-limit condition on the Pixel. Low-memory devices, API 29 and release profiling still need validation; these debug samples are not release budgets.
- Debug assembly and lint pass. Six ARM64/x86_64 native libraries pass ELF LOAD and APK ZIP 16 KiB alignment checks. APK inspection confirms all 1,320 polygon assets are packaged and the original full archive/Zstandard JNI library are absent. CI now runs both geography JVM suites and retains their reports/provenance; remote CI remains unrun.
- The installed build passed the 15-action/check navigation replay plus echo and a 25-step read-only geographic-license replay, including packaged Apache/ODbL documents and return to Settings. Two/four small-pixel idle warnings respectively remained; no raw-coordinate or gesture exceptions remain in the saved tests. A temporary recovery take was removed after the full license replay passed. The 13 historical points remain preserved; today’s map is empty because the date advanced.

## October 27: individual lifetime billing foundation

- Implemented a separate billing domain module and Play Billing 9.1 Java callback adapter (avoids an unrelated Kotlin upgrade required by the KTX artifact). Purchase/restore UI uses localized Play pricing only when configuration and a catalog product are available. Pending payments never unlock features. App resume/store callbacks refresh ownership. Unconfigured builds make no checkout offer and explain the unavailable purchase service; free recording/import/preview are independent of billing work.
- The client signs a fresh challenge with an app-private Android Keystore RSA key and obtains a Play Integrity Standard token bound to the canonical request. Only verified, signed leases grant paid capabilities. Cached leases are package/product/device/token-bound, limited to seven days, cleared by fresh empty/canceled ownership, and protected against simple clock rollback. The shared gate currently awaits integration into the unfinished paid file/schedule/webhook paths.
- Added `worker/android-purchases`: a local Cloudflare Worker with generated binding types, bounded JSON/network deadlines, single-use D1 challenges, short-lived rate buckets, Google OAuth/Integrity/Publisher verification, stable hash-only grants, server acknowledgement before lease delivery and RS256 signing. No consume operation exists. Google tokens, raw Play proofs, private keys and location data are not persisted in its ledger. Upstream tracing is disabled to avoid token-bearing URL traces. Production identifiers, signing certificates and credentials remain absent; no cloud deployment/provisioning occurred.
- Eight JVM billing tests pass, covering signature/product/device/package tampering, pending/unconfigured states, server-only access, cache expiry/clock rollback, refund/empty ownership, account/token changes, free access during blocked network verification and canonical message/token redaction. Both isolated Pixel instrumentation tests pass for actual Keystore signing/persistence and signed-cache save/load/clear; they use the separate library test app and preserve journal data.
- App debug assembly and lint pass after client integration. The installed build passed a recorded/polished 19-step Pixel replay for unavailable purchases, Restore, accessible import, hidden checkout, free JSON preview and return to idle recording/settings. Its initial authoring take encountered the known stale native inspection tree; resetting only the Pixel's Argent servers and replaying semantic selectors passed all 19 steps. The 13 historical points remain intact.
- The service passes TypeScript checking, five local tests (including an ephemeral real D1 concurrent replay test and mocked Google OAuth/query/ack transport), and a Wrangler deployment dry-run. This is local evidence only. Product provisioning, real Play sandbox acceptance, authenticated RTDN/voided-purchase reconciliation, refund monitoring, operational/key-rotation review, all paid entry points and customer-facing cache terms remain release gates. See the service README for the exact protocol and concrete setup checklist.

## October 27: streaming file delivery

- Added a cold, ordered export source and streaming renderers for all eight formats. Indexed 500-record keyset pages retain equal-time IDs for both visits and points. JSON arrays, CSV rows, Markdown tables, GeoJSON/OwnTracks/Overland features and GPX segments stream directly; KML performs repeatable timestamp/coordinate passes. Existing list-renderer compatibility is checked across all eight formats, data kinds, filters and field choices. Fourteen export JVM tests pass, including a million-point bounded sink, cancellation, Android-safe template syntax and byte-bounded Unicode filenames.
- The application now uses Room's bundled SQLite 2.6.2 driver and explicit writer transactions. Exports and map/country/outing reads use a separate deferred WAL reader connection from the same pool. A physical snapshot test proves a recording writer can commit an insertion/deletion while all 1,002 original equal-time points and visits remain stable across export pages and repeated reads. Twenty-one data instrumentation tests pass on the Pixel, including migrations from schemas 1/2 and a million-point map/statistics fixture. The latter retained exactly 2,500 displayed samples and all 1,000,000 statistics records; its all-history read took 7,297 ms in debug. This is not an accepted release latency budget.
- `ExportFiles` checks the paid capability before staging and before share, folder writes and picker-return writes. It prepares app-private UTF-8 files first, computes size/SHA-256, exposes only the export cache through a non-exported FileProvider with temporary read grants, and creates new SAF destination documents. It reads every destination back before reporting success; corrupt/failed new destinations are cleaned up where the provider permits deletion. Manual folder collisions retain both files; templates can create subfolders. Day splits use civil midnight in the selected zone, with unique names across days/formats. Clear-data invalidates active export generations, removes cache files and revokes prepared URI grants.
- Four isolated Pixel file tests pass for all 1,002 records, every paid delivery gate, free preview without access, FileProvider intent grants, destination verification/corruption cleanup, preserving a good existing document, folder collisions, DST day splits, canceled staging and invalidated prepared files. The synthetic DocumentsProvider and fixture classes are absent from the installed app APK. No purchase bypass or real journal destination write was used for these checks.
- A separate Pixel test staged a JSON file from 1,000,000 persisted synthetic points and parsed it back with a streaming reader, proving both the array and declared count contain all records. Preparation plus hashing took 34,041 ms for 208,884,982 bytes. Sampled Java heap use reached 230,273,760 bytes with a 268,435,456-byte limit; this includes uncollected allocation and fixture state, is not a retained-memory measurement, and needs release/map-loaded profiling before accepting a memory budget. The synthetic database/file were deleted afterward.
- The Export screen now includes date/time/quality filters, field switches, additional formats, civil-day splitting, filename/subfolder templates, Share, Save as and default-folder delivery. Errors appear next to delivery controls; switch rows have named toggle semantics. Preview uses the same snapshot/streaming path with 2,500 matching records of each kind and a 100,000-character cap. The final build passes lint/assembly and 24 domain/14 export/8 billing JVM tests. Eight packaged native libraries pass 16 KiB LOAD/ZIP alignment, including bundled SQLite.
- The installed build passes the semantic export-access/filter replay: 39 actions/checks plus its prerequisite echo, including denied Share/Save, accessible switch toggling, native time/date pickers, cancel/reset and free JSON preview. The existing billing/unconfigured replay passes all 19 steps, and navigation passes 16 actions/checks plus echo after adding an explicit scroll to the preview. These prove UI behavior under an unconfigured purchase service, not a purchased end-to-end transaction. Shell diagnostics still show the original 13 points, zero visits/open sessions and recording idle.
- Open export work: real purchased delivery, ActivityResult process-death acceptance and provider matrix; per-outing summaries/splits and Country Days file export; replacement/append transactions and schedule success/cursor bookkeeping; Swift-generated golden fixtures and cross-platform device round trips; low-storage, revoked-grant and provider deletion-failure UX; release memory/latency budgets. The owner approved matching iOS scheduling: latest due occurrence on the current local date, current-day export through actual execution time, no automatic missed-day backlog. No automatic schedule is enabled.

## October 27: saved export settings and picker recovery

- Export format, additional formats, filters, field choices, civil-day splitting and filename templates persist through an explicit versioned codec in DataStore. Application-owned writes coalesce rapid edits and preserve unrelated settings. Invalid or future saved configurations block delivery until an explicit reset instead of silently broadening filters. The Readable, Compact and Dated folders presets match the current iOS templates; new installations use its Readable default.
- Save as now journals its request before launching the system picker. Private atomic metadata records the prepared bundle, destination, write phase and completion. A cold callback can resolve the durable request without composition state. Recovery validates staged size/hash and compares an existing destination byte-for-byte against the prepared prefix before resuming. Changed destinations remain untouched; already complete matching documents become completed receipts without another write. Duplicate callbacks do not rewrite completed files. Cancellation commits a tombstone so a late callback cannot write, releases retained grants and removes staging while preserving external files. Cache eviction produces an expired-request error; journal metadata is outside the FileProvider share root.
- Six isolated Pixel recovery tests and two settings tests pass. They cover coordinator/controller recreation, partial/full destination checkpoints, duplicate and cold callbacks, paid reauthorization, cancellation, changed destinations/staging and path traversal; settings round trips preserve unrelated preferences and require explicit recovery from corrupt data. These are synthetic checkpoint tests, not a claim of real purchased ActivityResult process-kill acceptance. Three codec JVM tests pass; the export suite now has 17 tests. Debug assembly and lint pass, and all eight native libraries retain 16 KiB LOAD/ZIP alignment.
- The installed Pixel build passes the saved export-settings persistence replay: 32 actions/checks plus its prerequisite echo. It selects Compact, restarts the app, verifies the exact saved template, then restores Readable and returns to Settings. Every selector/scroll target is semantic; there are no raw gesture exceptions. ## October 27: iOS schedule occurrence and execution-plan parity

- Added latest/due occurrence selection with the owner-approved current-local-day policy. Already completed occurrences are skipped using successful execution instants; historical days are not enumerated. Interval tails crossing midnight and daily anchor resets remain supported. The separate once-daily implementation now mirrors ExportAutomationKit's resolve-today-then-add/subtract-day behavior, including a spring-gap anchor's adjusted clock time. Actual Swift fixtures exposed and verified this correction.
- Checked in 5,880 daily/interval cases generated by compiling the actual iOS interval implementation and the exact ExportAutomationKit revision pinned by the iOS project (`17993dc0361c41145dc6429738ed369d9976e550`). Seven zones cover US/European DST, Lord Howe half-hour DST, Puerto Rico, Kathmandu and Apia; anchors include ambiguous and nonexistent wall times. Both next and latest occurrence instants match every case. The generator requires a clean pinned checkout, stores local source hashes/package revision, and has a source-only CI check. Android CI consumes the goldens without requiring Swift; remote CI has not run.
- Added a captured scheduled execution plan matching the current iOS day/window/write policy. Once-daily runs ignore persisted append mode. Interval append clamps the cursor between current local midnight and actual execution time; GPX/KML always use a full-day snapshot without advancing the cursor. Filename patterns become day-stable. Other explicitly supplied filters/field choices survive date-window resolution.
- Scheduled staging now uses the captured execution timestamp for content and filenames, rather than the later wall clock when a retry prepares files. It checks both scheduled-export and file-export capabilities, rechecks after staging, and removes the prepared bundle if scheduled access expires before completion. The isolated Pixel scheduled-staging regression proves a March execution produces the March filename/window even when tested in October, excludes records outside the captured window, yields identical retry bytes, denies unverified access and cleans staging on mid-operation revocation. All five file-delivery tests passed; the strengthened revocation case passed again after its assertion was added. No personal journal data or external destination was used.
- The domain suite now passes 28 tests (including all generated comparisons), and export passes 22 tests. Debug assembly and lint pass; all eight native libraries still pass 16 KiB LOAD/ZIP checks. This is schedule planning/staging evidence. Verified replacement/append delivery, durable run/cursor receipts, WorkManager execution, scheduling UI and actual purchased/provider acceptance remain unfinished; automatic schedules remain disabled.

## October 27: streaming scheduled merge staging

- Added streaming scheduled CSV/JSON/Markdown merge strategies. JSON deduplicates top-level record identities independently per array, keeps the first version of each identity and refreshes scalar fields from the incoming payload, matching iOS. CSV preserves the current iOS header/line-trimming behavior. Markdown replaces matching managed sections while preserving other sections and merging frontmatter; section bodies stream from indexed file spans. GPX/KML reject delta merge and use the full-day plan. Malformed/incompatible JSON fails without touching the destination; full-day fallback orchestration remains to be connected.
- Twenty-two synthetic merge fixtures are generated by compiling the actual iOS CSV/JSON strategy declarations and Markdown strategy from the exact pinned ExportKit revision. JVM tests compare JSON semantically and CSV/Markdown byte-for-byte. Fixtures cover overlapping identities, per-array scope, missing old arrays, null/boolean/numerical identities, nested feature properties, malformed roots, multiline CSV, custom Markdown sections, duplicate/Unicode headings, multiline frontmatter, CRLF and missing closing delimiters. The numeric fixture exposed decimal/integer timestamp identity equivalence, now covered. Source hashes/package-pin checks run in CI; remote CI has not run.
- `ExportFiles.mergeScheduled` snapshots an existing SAF document into private staging, records its size/SHA-256 fingerprint, prepares the merged file with a disk-backed SQLite identity index, then deletes the scratch database and old staged bytes. It validates the delta, checks scheduled/file access before and after work, cleans failed staging and guards output against clear-data invalidation. It returns a new prepared bundle and expected existing fingerprint. It does not truncate or replace the external destination; the replacement writer must recheck that fingerprint before mutation.
- Both isolated Pixel merge tests pass. One proves inclusive cursor overlap produces three unique points, preserves the original destination bytes, checks access and leaves malformed input untouched with failed staging removed. The other merges two overlapping 100,000-record arrays into 150,000 correctly ordered records using the private disk index and verifies namespace separation and scratch cleanup. That debug run took 12,808 ms including streaming validation and produced 7,127,783 bytes; no release memory/latency budget is claimed. After final numeric/output-guard changes, the focused staging case passed again.
- Export now passes 28 JVM tests, including Swift merge goldens, a 100,000-row streaming CSV sink and cancellation. Final debug assembly/lint pass. Durable automatic destination replacement/backups, full-day fallback, occurrence success/cursor receipts and WorkManager/UI integration remain unfinished; no automatic schedule is enabled. Current iOS merge behavior also refreshes scalar totals from a delta, replaces matching Markdown sections, and does not deduplicate feature properties nested under `properties`; these are parity behaviors, not claims of cumulative totals or arbitrary-document merging.

## October 27: durable scheduled document replacement

- Added an application-private delivery journal under `filesDir/scheduled-delivery`, outside the FileProvider share root. It captures the new payload, original bytes, size/SHA-256 fingerprints, destination/backup URIs and prepared/backed-up/writing/verified/completed/canceled phases. Private payload/original files are synced before the first receipt; state updates use AtomicFile. A process mutex plus filesystem lock serializes writer instances/processes, and another active transaction for the same parent/name is rejected.
- Existing documents receive a separately created, verified sibling backup before truncation. The destination's captured fingerprint is rechecked immediately before replacement; every completed write is read back and verified. Recovery accepts the captured original, an exact new file, or a matching partial new prefix; changed destination/backup bytes stop recovery. A verified checkpoint commits without rewriting. Completed receipts suppress duplicate writes. Cleanup failure retains completion and its backup reference; a changed backup is never automatically deleted. Providers still cannot supply a compare-and-swap against an unrelated external writer, so rechecks are not a guarantee against simultaneous external edits.
- New-document creation persists its returned URI before entering the writing phase, and recovery reuses that URI if a process died before the phase commit. A provider process/app death exactly between createDocument returning and URI persistence can leave an unclaimed empty document; this platform boundary still needs actual provider/process-kill acceptance and cleanup UX. No false completion is recorded. The writer supports providers without rename/atomic-replace by using the verified backup; no atomic rename guarantee is made.
- `ExportFiles` now exposes start/resume/state/cancel delivery and a merge helper bound to its original destination/fingerprint. Clear-data immediately invalidates in-flight writer generations, clears private receipts/payloads and prevents late resume. Cancellation leaves external destination/backup files intact and commits a tombstone. The merge-to-replacement integration test proves inclusive cursor overlap is merged once into the stable day file, read back, and not rewritten by a duplicate callback. No schedule cursor advances yet; that requires the remaining occurrence coordinator.
- Ten isolated Pixel writer tests pass: durable payload after cache deletion, verified replacement/backup cleanup, coordinator recreation with a partial write, verified checkpoint recovery, changed destination and backup protection, cancellation, access loss, corrupt provider rejection, clear-data invalidation, per-destination exclusion, stale fingerprint rejection, deletion failure after success, changed backup after completion and URI/phase checkpoint recovery. These tests simulate durable checkpoints; they are not an actual purchased ActivityResult/OS process-kill claim. The first test pass exposed a test-provider discovery assumption in isDocumentUri; structural SAF tree/document validation now works independently of picker-discovery metadata.
- The final combined Pixel run passes all 23 selected tests: ten writer, two merge, five file-delivery and six picker recovery. Debug assembly/lint pass. All data/documents are in separate instrumentation storage; no purchase bypass ships. Real Play/provider matrix, recovery UI, low-storage/revoked-grant acceptance, full-day fallback, occurrence success/cursor receipts and WorkManager scheduling remain unfinished. No automatic schedule is enabled.

## October 27: durable occurrence coordination and cursor commit

- Added `ScheduledExportCoordinator` with a private AtomicFile state and process/filesystem locking. Before preparation, it captures a UUID, scheduled occurrence, local zone, execution timestamp, options/window, stable filename and destination tree. Retries reuse this plan even after midnight, changed foreground filename preferences or coordinator recreation. An unfinished captured write is resumed; the coordinator does not enumerate missed historical days.
- The coordinator resolves the destination, prepares a rewrite or delta, stages a merge, and starts/resumes the durable document writer. Only a completed verified delivery receipt can atomically update last success and append cursor and clear the pending run. A crash after delivery but before this commit uses the completed receipt to finish bookkeeping without reopening the destination for writing. Captured execution timestamps match iOS recordSuccess/cursor behavior, including an explicit run after a future stored cursor. Once-daily/GPX/KML rewrites do not advance an append cursor.
- Missing append day files rebuild the full captured day rather than creating an incomplete delta-only aggregate. A typed unmergeable-JSON result also falls back to a full-day rewrite, with a persisted notice. Read/write storage failures remain failures rather than being treated as JSON parse errors. Destination/payload guards and paid capabilities still apply to fallback. Scalar delta totals, CSV/Markdown merge semantics and per-format append policy retain the established iOS behavior.
- Duplicate simultaneous wakes serialize; once one succeeds, the same occurrence is no longer due. Canceling a pending run commits its canceled occurrence separately from success/cursor, preventing that same automatic wake from recreating it while allowing the next occurrence. Schedule/format edits require finishing or canceling a pending run; disabling remains available. Corrupt/future state fails closed until explicit reset. Clear-data now invalidates coordinator/writer generations, clears automatic configuration and private delivery receipts, and prevents late pending work while retaining external documents/backups.
- Eleven isolated Pixel coordinator tests pass for current-day/no-backlog behavior, concurrent wakes, append and once-daily policies, crash checkpoints before preparation/after delivery, captured retries across midnight/preferences, failed provider writes with unchanged success/cursor, malformed/missing day-file recovery, disable/cancel/reset, unverified enable denial, corrupt state, canceled-occurrence suppression, full-day GPX rewrites and a future cursor. An earlier combined run passed 22 coordinator/writer/merge tests; after the final timestamp/fallback refinements, all 11 coordinator tests, export JVM tests, debug assembly and lint pass. The 28 domain and 28 export JVM suites remain green; fixture provenance checks pass. These are synthetic checkpoint tests, not actual provider/OS process-kill or purchased Play acceptance.
- AppGraph owns the coordinator and Clear all journal data clears it. WorkManager trigger/retry scheduling, app-resume catch-up, scheduling/recovery UI, cold-worker purchase-readiness and physical unattended/provider acceptance remain unfinished. No automatic schedule has been enabled on the owner's Pixel.

## October 27: background scheduling, native controls and cold-worker billing

- Added unique one-time WorkManager requests toward the next daily/anchored interval occurrence, with exponential retry on failed runs. Existing retry identity/backoff is preserved when the app opens. A finishing worker schedules its successor under a distinct occurrence name instead of replacing/canceling itself. Future stale requests are canceled on timing/zone changes; disabling and clear-data cancel tagged export/reconciliation jobs. Boot, package replacement and time/zone receivers enqueue reconciliation only, with a bounded broadcast lifetime; they do not start recording or an export foreground service. Work requests contain no history, destination URI or credentials. Android remains free to delay execution; no exact-alarm permission is requested. [Work request and retry semantics](https://developer.android.com/develop/background-work/background-tasks/persistent/getting-started/define-work).
- MainActivity resume now checks the latest due occurrence through the same coordinator. The application-owned controller serializes settings/run/retry commands, coalesces foreground wakes, exposes loaded/busy/error/pending/next-run state and invalidates active delivery before cancel/disable/reset. The native Automatic exports screen offers enablement, system time picker, once daily/every 1–23 hours, format/data selection, rewrite/append, last success, planned/delayed run, captured pending-window retry/cancel and explicit corrupt-state reset. It uses the default export folder and saved filename template. Clear all journal data awaits controller/work cancellation before removing history.
- Four physical Pixel instrumentation tests exercise WorkManager's actual test scheduler and injected production workers with synthetic Room/documents: unique delayed execution, successor creation without self-cancellation, production worker retry result with preserved run/job identity, successful retry, zone-based replacement, disable cancellation, foreground catch-up and clear-data cancellation. Initial delays are advanced with TestDriver, so this proves integration rather than an unattended timing/OEM budget. The final combined worker/coordinator run passes all 15 tests. The build/lint pass; remote CI remains unrun.
- Paid entry points now load valid signed cache without a network request and renew expired access through Play/server verification when needed, including cold workers. A separate renewal mutex coalesces concurrent requests. Pending/refunded/unconfigured/unverifiable expired access stays locked; free features bypass renewal work. Three new JVM tests cover valid cold cache, one concurrent renewal, expired/offline denial, free access during blocked renewal, revocation and no store query in unconfigured builds. Billing now passes 11 tests; domain/export remain at 28 each. Real configured Play/Integrity/Publisher acceptance is still required.

The first replay's final off-screen folder expectation was corrected to the actual Settings root; another app was observed taking focus afterward. The polished replay passes. Dark-theme inspection found unreadable dark status-bar icons over the dark app background; WindowInsetsControllerCompat now follows the Compose theme through LocalActivity. The dark-to-System replay passes 24 actions/checks plus echo, captures an inspected screenshot with readable light status-bar icons, and restores System theme. Picking a result moves the draft pin, fills a blank name and replaces the address; an existing user-entered name is retained. Reverse metadata always uses the exact selected coordinate, even when the provider returns a nearby street centroid. Completed/canceled requests cannot restart just because the network preference changes.
- A reusable domain lookup service enforces the live geocoding preference for new and cached requests and cancels work on opt-out. It serializes requests, throttles native starts by 500 ms, applies a 15-second timeout and holds at most 64 in-memory cache entries (up to ten results each) for one day, keyed by query/coordinate/locale. Search rejects missing/nonfinite/out-of-range coordinates and blank metadata, bounds labels, limits results to ten, and deduplicates by the iOS name/rounded-coordinate identity rule. Errors and canceled requests are not cached. Clear journal data cancels these requests and clears their owned memory cache.
- The Android adapter uses API 33+ asynchronous Geocoder callbacks. Since that API cannot cancel an already issued native request, canceled results are discarded and at most four outstanding callbacks are allowed; a provider that never replies can exhaust those slots until callbacks arrive or the app process restarts. API 29–32 uses one daemon worker and one queue slot, with canceled queued tasks removed. Legacy Binder calls can continue after logical cancellation, but cannot create unbounded threads or queue growth. Regional availability/terms and API 29 behavior remain release gates, not proven by API 37 testing. System lookup is not a replacement for the still-unimplemented nearby-business catalog or map-region search bias.
- Ten new domain tests pass (39 total), covering malformed/duplicate results, signed half-coordinate rounding, canonical Unicode ID/cache equality, name fallback, Unicode query length, opt-out for cached/new/in-flight/throttled requests, stale provider completion, cancellation on owned-data clearing, cache expiry/LRU/locale/coordinate keys, timeout/retry and concurrent identical queries. Three isolated Pixel instrumentation tests verify native Address extraction, sparse/missing coordinates, localized metadata and polar/dateline bounds. These use only synthetic Address objects and no journal or network access; CI includes the suite. The test class is absent from the main APK.
- The installed Pixel's `place-search-public-query` replay passes 37 actions/checks plus its prerequisite echo using only the public query Reykjavik Iceland. It verifies result selection, draft name/address application and final discard. Its first replay stopped on a recorder-vs-runner combined-label mismatch; the semantic Select this place target fixed that without changing the result assertions. Light-mode screenshot inspection confirms readable native controls. The flow requires a working English system provider/network and is not a deterministic offline test or global coverage proof.
- The existing offline `saved-place-map-selection` replay still passes all 55 actions/checks plus echo with the search UI present.
- The public Eiffel Tower pin at 48.8584, 2.2945 also returned address metadata during live testing and retained both exact coordinates through applying to the unsaved editor. The actual provider supplied a floor-level label; the UI explicitly asks the user to review estimates. Its `pin-address-public-coordinate` replay initially failed (resolved by scoped scrolling in the subsequent visit-editor work): `scroll-to Find address for pin` loses the full-screen picker and fails after 25 attempts. A fresh five-step diagnostic reproduces this with the keyboard already closed. The first live status check also needed a fresh accessibility tree after scrolling; the repeated check passed. That checkpoint retained the coordinate/result assertions; the subsequent visit-editor work below resolves the replay without weakening them. Final debug assembly/lint and all eight native libraries' 16 KiB alignment pass. The final Pixel aggregate remains 13 points, zero visits/open sessions and idle recording; the saved-place library remains empty after discarding the public drafts. Nearby/current-place suggestions, manual-visit lookup integration, disabled-geocoding UI replay, dark/large-font/TalkBack and full device/provider cancellation acceptance remain active work.

## October 27: guarded visit editing and location selection

- The manual/edit visit form now shares the saved-place map/search/address picker and adds a searchable offline saved-place chooser. A first map selection retains typed details when lookup supplies no replacements; changing an existing coordinate clears the prior location's metadata unless a new result supplies it. Choosing a saved place copies its name/address/coordinates without editing that place. Device-geocoder selections use the existing `coreLocationGeocode` interchange category recognized by the current Swift importer; saved/direct-coordinate choices use `userEntered`. Editing the name/address/coordinates manually sets user-entered provenance.
- New past visits require a trimmed name, valid coordinate and a departure after arrival, with both times in the past. Past-visit creation does not offer Still here; existing open visits can remain open. New manual visits have a stable draft UUID, confirmed status and confirmation/update timestamps. Retry preserves the same candidate. Date controls remain in place and disabled during a save. Dirty cancel offers Keep editing/Discard; correction undo and visit deletion require confirmation. Confirm visit is separate from changing a place.
- UI writes now use shared `HistoryRepository.saveVisit/deleteVisit` commands, serialized with recording ingestion and committed in writer transactions. They compare the expected snapshot so recording/country-analysis edits or deletion cannot be overwritten by a stale editor. New identity collisions are rejected, exact retries are idempotent, and timestamps are normalized to Room's millisecond precision before comparison/persistence. Deleting or manually closing the detector's active visit clears its dwell state atomically, while unrelated visits preserve the active detector state. Routes and saved places remain untouched.
- Seven draft JVM tests pass (46 domain total) for first-selection fallback/provenance, creation/audit fields, validation, confirmation without inventing correction history, original/detected-value preservation, geography invalidation, notes whitespace, existing open visits and identity rejection. Six new isolated Pixel command tests pass with eleven existing repository tests (17 total). They cover stale saves/deletes, identity retries/collisions, no resurrection after deletion, active/unrelated dwell state, sub-millisecond timestamps and a real SQLite trigger proving the visit update rolls back when detector cleanup fails. No database schema change is needed; CI's existing data suite includes these cases.
- The saved `manual-visit-location-editing` replay passes 61 actions/checks plus its prerequisite echo on the Pixel. It checks the empty saved-place chooser, preserves a typed name through offline pin selection, keeps editing after dirty cancel, creates the synthetic 0/0 past visit, verifies it after restart, then confirms deletion. It requires an empty saved-place library and the named fixture absent, after 01:00 local time so the default one-hour visit belongs to today. Light-mode screenshot inspection confirms readable controls. Small-pixel idle warnings are not a release performance result. Current-place capture and save-as-reusable-place from manual visits remain unfinished. Positive saved-place selection, visit-specific network lookup, correction/confirmation controls, process-death drafts and dark/large-font/TalkBack remain additional acceptance work.
- The first complete replay exposed the same scrolling dismissal seen in reverse lookup. A five-step diagnostic reproduced it; temporary coordinate-free traces identified `DialogWrapper.onTouchEvent`, rather than Back navigation. The runner's full-screen scroll ended outside the dialog. Stable scroll-container accessibility IDs and `scroll-to.within` keep the gesture inside the actual editor viewport. The focused diagnostic and the original full visit replay both pass with that scope. Temporary logging was removed; Back/cancel behavior remains available. The picker also needs the page margin as its gesture origin so the embedded map does not consume the scroll. Its stable margin scope resolves that second constraint. The original `pin-address-public-coordinate` regression now passes through a standalone wrapper: 41 actions/checks plus echo, preserving exact public-pin coordinates before/after lookup and in the unsaved editor, then discarding the draft. The old scrolling QA gap is closed; temporary diagnostic flows were removed. Final debug assembly/lint and 46 domain/46 export JVM tests pass; all eight native libraries retain 16 KiB alignment. No temporary dismissal logging or isolated test/provider class ships in the main APK. The final installed build also passes the full 61-check visit replay. The original 13 journal points, zero visits/open sessions and idle recording are preserved after deleting the synthetic fixture; the saved-place library remains unchanged.

## October 27: reusable places from manual visits

- New manual visits have an optional Save as reusable location control and the iOS radius choices (50/100/150/250 m). Cancellation is checked during matching and before commit. No schema or dependency changes are required.

## October 27: indexed day and outing replay

- The Map and exact/inferred outing screens now open a full-screen native replay view. It shows the current timestamp, point index/count, elapsed/total duration and traveled/total distance, with autoplay, play/pause, a slider, start/end actions and Close/Back. Index rounding, clamping and 250 ms progression with at most 180 advancement ticks follow the inspected iOS calculator. Playing again at the end starts from the beginning. Scrubbing pauses playback. The prefix path and current-position marker update independently of recorded history; map tiles remain opt-in. Replay is free.
- Preparation pages the complete selected history in one reader snapshot into a transient private binary index, preserving equal-timestamp ordering. It computes cumulative Android ellipsoidal distance from every included original point. Frame seeks do not rescan history; each loads at most 2,500 prefix drawing points plus the exact cursor/statistics. Day ranges exclude outliers by default. Outings filter outliers and retain iOS's all-outlier fallback; inferred outings exclude exact-session windows. This is separate from the existing display sample and never changes stored point coordinates, IDs or outlier flags. Close, disposal and preparation cancellation remove the index; abandoned prior-process indexes are pruned at the next preparation. Preparation/storage errors remain retryable through reopening the view.
- Backgrounding pauses and releases the active index; returning prepares a fresh paused snapshot. Changes to points/sessions invalidate the open replay and ask the user to reopen it. Clear all journal data cancels and joins preparations, closes active archives, removes cached files and excludes new snapshots until database deletion finishes. Its isolated regression exposed a real cancellation issue: Room 2.8.4 supplies its database job to reader callbacks. Reattaching the replay callback to the original caller job fixes the stalled cancellation without changing the shared export snapshot helper. Temporary diagnostic logging was removed. The caller-lifetime fix also protects close/background cancellation during preparation. Other long-running snapshot clients still need their own cancellation audit.
- Four domain regressions cover nonfinite/clamped progress, empty/single routes, Swift half-step rounding, long-route tick pacing and rounded duration formatting (56 domain tests total). Seven isolated Pixel repository tests cover a 5,001-point index across equal-timestamp pages, unsampled cumulative distance with a 25-point drawing limit, outlier policies, exact/inferred boundaries, cancellation cleanup, concurrent seeks, a writer committing during a disk-backed WAL reader snapshot, and cache clear with an in-flight suspended preparation. The actual Swift calculator generator records source/harness hashes and produces 56 index/pacing cases plus three nonzero-distance snapshots, which match on the Pixel within 0.1 m. CI checks fixture provenance and the existing data selection includes all replay tests. No journal schema or dependency change was required.
- The recorded `route-replay-day-outing` flow passes 74 actions/checks plus its prerequisite echo on the Pixel: native CSV import of 12 public-coordinate points, day autoplay/end statistics, start/midpoint scrubbing with exact elapsed/distance assertions, play/pause, inferred-outing playback, Close, and confirmed deletion of the synthetic outing. Its echo requires that fixture's date to have no other points/visits, matching inference settings and the staged CSV; never clear real history to satisfy setup. The first full run passed all replay controls but stopped in the cleanup scroll. A separately recorded cleanup span now seeks Show on map before Delete through the safe page margin; the complete run then passed without recovery. Saved actions use semantic selectors and scoped scrolling, with no raw coordinates or gesture exceptions. Live inspection sometimes needed a Pixel-scoped accessibility reset; the runner independently verified the assertions.
- A 74.9-second 1080×2400/30 fps capture is saved as untracked local evidence at [replay QA recording](../.argent/recordings/screen-recording-2C061FDH200CJN-1793120071185.mp4). Sampled light-mode frames show the marker/prefix, midpoint/end statistics and native controls without clipping. This is not a release frame-time, complete motion, dark/XL or TalkBack verdict. Live-recording refresh parity (current changes require reopening), million-point preparation/seek latency and memory, storage-pressure/process-death cases, exact-outing UI and background/data-invalidation UI acceptance remain open. The final build passes the complete 74-action replay again after the caller-lifetime/cache-clear fix. Final debug assembly/lint, 56 domain/46 export tests and seven isolated replay tests pass; eight native libraries retain 16 KiB alignment, and no replay test class or temporary trace tag ships in the main APK. Final aggregate-only verification preserves 13 original points, zero visits/open sessions and idle recording; the replay cache contains zero files. The staged Downloads CSV was removed. Road-matched replay, photo markers and the broader map features still require implementation and QA.

## October 27: photo correlation and local metadata foundation

- Added Android-independent photo metadata/moment/source models, endpoint-retaining 500-photo display sampling, and the inspected iOS correlation priority: authorized photo GPS, nearest eligible route point within 15 minutes, then the earliest matching visit with 15-minute grace. Earlier route fixes win an equal-distance time tie. Open visits use the supplied reference time. Missing capture instants stay unresolved. Database resolution queries complete unsampled route history and applies the all-outlier fallback to the entire requested sync range, matching the Swift implementation; unrelated good fixes in that range can therefore suppress a nearby bad fix and allow a visit fallback.
- The generator compiles the actual extracted `resolvedPhotoMoments`, `nearestLocationPoint` and `visitContainingPhoto` functions with the current constants and metadata structs. A fixed reference date replaces the clock, and deterministic synthetic stubs supply route/visit data. Sixteen Swift-produced cases cover GPS/invalid-GPS priority, nearest/tie and exact 15-minute boundaries, all-outlier/full-range policies, visit grace, open-visit future bounds, overlapping visits and no match. All match the Android repository on the Pixel. Source/harness/model hashes are checked by CI; fixtures use only public synthetic coordinates.
- Room schema 5 adds `photo_assets` references, capture/GPS metadata, selected-reference preservation, availability and validation time; a composite photo capture-time/URI index supports ordered pages. Existing cached moments remain stored but cannot enter windows without a validated available reference. Atomic commands sync at most 500 inputs, remove stale unresolved moments, hide unavailable references, restore reselected references and remove metadata without touching the photo file or location journal. Window counts include every eligible moment while drawing samples retain at most 500. A caller-job wrapper protects the transaction lifetime; canceling a suspended progress callback promptly rolls back partial references/moments and releases the writer.
- Four new JVM photo cases bring the domain suite to 64; all 46 export regressions also pass. Six isolated photo repository tests plus recording/repository and schema 1/2/3/4 upgrade cases pass all 19 selected data tests on the Pixel. The tests cover the 16 Swift fixtures, hide/reselect/remove behavior, missing times/unlocated references, 2,500 equal-time moments across pages, triggered write rollback, and real caller cancellation. Migration fixtures preserve legacy cached photo rows and point/visit/country/correction data while keeping unvalidated photos hidden. Schema 5 and its migration are exported.
- Added an explicit-content-URI Android adapter with no scanning or permission requests. It validates image MIME type and URI shape, prefers the library capture instant, falls back to strictly parsed EXIF original time only with an explicit offset, reads available GPS, and decodes bounded orientation-correct thumbnails. It never uses file creation time or the phone’s present zone to invent a capture instant. AndroidX ExifInterface 1.4.2 is pinned after checking its official release documentation. Four isolated synthetic-file/provider tests pass for GPS/offset/subseconds, MediaStore timestamp precedence, missing time, portrait EXIF orientation, thumbnail size, malformed/file/network references and revoked/missing assets. The private synthetic provider/test classes are absent from the app APK. CI includes this source suite.
- Final debug assembly/lint, fixture provenance and eight native-library 16 KiB checks pass. The tested foundation is exposed through AppGraph; no photo job starts from construction. This is not an end-to-end photo feature: picker/grant and full/partial/denied permission handling, MediaStore scanning/observer lifecycle, automatic sync/cooldown, stale-work exclusion during clear-data, thumbnail failure/revocation UI, clusters/map pins, strips/pager and physical selected/full-access workflows remain unfinished. Native provider cancellation, large-library write/latency budgets and broader capture-time/format variants need acceptance. Whole-library permission eligibility and policy declarations remain release gates; no selected-only deviation has been approved. All tests use isolated synthetic databases/files and no owner photo assets.

## October 27: selected-photo controller and gallery

- Settings → Photos now opens the system photo picker or image document picker. The controller reads only explicitly selected URIs or saved references, retains grants after validating metadata, refreshes on foreground entry, and reports unavailable assets without deleting originals. The chronological gallery pages 50 references with stable capture-time/URI cursors; unknown capture times remain explicit. Thumbnails and the full-screen per-page pager use bounded image decoding. Previous/next controls, source labels and confirmed reference removal are implemented. Removal is guarded against repeated confirmation taps; counts use singular wording where appropriate.
- Photo jobs and image requests share a clear-data gate. Clear invalidates a persisted selection epoch, cancels and joins in-flight work, excludes new jobs through deletion, and releases only grants belonging to deleted photo references. Late picker results from before clear are rejected even after controller recreation. Cancellation and failed writes release newly retained uncommitted grants while preserving committed references and unrelated folder grants. Native provider I/O still needs cancellation/blocked-provider acceptance.

## October 27: photo map groups and outing strips

- Matched moments now feed main-map and outing-map photo layers. Android uses ellipsoidal native distance for UI clustering, retaining the existing 500-moment display bound and full count. Compact camera pins and thumbnail sprites include group counts. Photo-marker and image visibility persist through DataStore. Fit route includes visible photo coordinates. Foreground entry and Refresh validate only previously selected references; no library scan, broad permission request or automatic whole-library access is implied. Background/failed validation hides cached moments. Removed or revoked references invalidate open viewers and map groups. Full-count windows explain when display sampling applies.

Uploading first sprites before source publication, and republishing on a newly available key, fixes it without rebuilding geometry for ordinary thumbnail replacements. The regression also injects a tap at the native projected pin position and verifies selection, then verifies that disabling the layer removes its rendered feature.

## October 27: complete outing photo paging

- Resolved the sampled-outing gap identified above. Outing cards and their immersive viewer now read the complete chronological, half-open-range Room query; the map continues using its independent 500-photo sample. Existing `(capturedAt, uri)` indexes support the new query/rank lookup, with no schema migration. Equal timestamps retain deterministic URI ordering.
- Added Room Paging and Paging Compose (3.3.6) with 50-row initial/continuation loads, placeholders, a 150-row retention target, and independent strip/viewer caches. Lazy cards decode only composed thumbnails. Viewer navigation requests adjacent pages rather than materializing the complete photo set. Loading errors expose retry controls. Opening uses the selected URI's current rank and verifies the loaded URI before displaying it. Source invalidation dismisses the viewer; revoked/removed/cleared data cannot silently substitute another selection. A selection deleted between rank lookup and initial load closes instead of leaving a stuck loader. Foreground access/sync gates continue hiding photo surfaces and clearing selection while validation runs.
- Nine `PhotoRepositoryTest` tests pass on Pixel 7 API 37. New cases traverse all 1,200 equal-timestamp photos in 50-row pages, verify global ranks and direct distant loads, retain the independent 500-photo map limit, exclude out-of-range photos, and verify Room source invalidation/rank changes after unavailable/remove/clear operations.
- Final isolated native suite passes **8 tests** (`PhotoMapRenderingTest`, `PhotoStripInteractionTest`, `PhotoPagingInteractionTest`) in 14.925 seconds. Four new paging tests reach/select index 600 and reach the final card in a 1,001-photo source; open at the selected global rank and advance 50 times across a real page boundary; open photo 601 through an actual synthetic in-memory Room source and dismiss after revocation; and reject a selection deleted before first load. The first development test run exposed an off-screen test target and an incorrectly reused fake PagingSource; corrected test fixtures/targets pass. No AppGraph, owner database or real photo library is used by these native tests.
- `:app:assembleDebug`, `:app:assembleMapQa`, `:app:assembleMapQaAndroidTest` and `:app:lintDebug` pass on the final code. CI's isolated app-test class selection now includes the paging tests. The earlier 151-check picker/map/outing end-to-end video remains evidence of the earlier revision, and was not rerun for this change. Full permission/scanning/automatic-sync flows, cross-page Settings-gallery immersive navigation, release memory/latency budgets and full device accessibility/motion acceptance remain open. AndroidX's [paged-data guide](https://developer.android.com/topic/libraries/architecture/paging/v3-paged-data) informed the source, stable-key and placeholder integration.

## October 27: bounded saved-photo sync staging

- Replaced refresh's complete reference/metadata lists with 500-row reference enumeration and a disposable private `cacheDir/photo-sync` metadata spool. Each source reference is read once, capture-time bounds are accumulated as scalars, the sealed spool is verified with SHA-256 before any apply batches, and correlation applies at most 500 metadata rows per batch using the same full requested range. This preserves the iOS full-range all-outlier policy across batch boundaries; immediate per-page correlation would have changed results. No history schema, permission or original image file changes were needed.
- The spool preserves null capture times/GPS, exact Instant seconds/nanoseconds and Unicode references, limits individual UTF-8 URI records to 64 KiB, and rejects corrupted staging before correlation. Normal completion, errors and cancellation close/delete staging; the next sync or clear operation reaps abandoned owned stage files, excluding live spools in the application's single process and unrelated files. Cache staging is disposable rather than a resumable sync journal. Clear-data now determines owned photo grants from the retained-grant inventory rather than collecting the entire reference library.
- Fixed a real grant-return cancellation race: URI grant acquisition and registration in the bounded explicit-selection ownership set now execute together before a canceled caller resumes. Uncommitted newly acquired grants are released; committed reference grants and unrelated export-folder grants remain intact. This does not establish crash-safe reconciliation of grants acquired immediately before process death; that remains part of release/process-death acceptance.
- **18 isolated Pixel platform tests pass** (10.479 seconds): 11 controller tests, three spool tests and four real metadata/thumbnail source tests. New cases refresh 2,001 synthetic references with one metadata read each and full-range outlier fallback into a containing visit; cancel at grant return; reject an unavailable staging directory before reading/retaining anything; fail the second apply batch and retain only the completed first 500; stream 1,201 Unicode/null/nanosecond metadata records in 500/500/201 batches; preserve concurrent live spools during orphan cleanup; and reject checksum corruption before correlation. Existing revocation/reselection, rollback, clear/late-callback/image cancellation, paging and grant-isolation cases remain green. All fixture history/photos/grants are synthetic in the platform test package.
- The final app also passes the full recorded picker → map → outing → cold gallery → reference-removal regression: **151 actions/checks plus two echoes**, uninterrupted, in 2m49s. Three small-motion idle warnings occurred at steps 17/45/150; subsequent destination controls pass. No new video or release frame-rate verdict is claimed. Debug and mapQa assembly, platform-test assembly and debug lint pass. CI's platform class selection now includes `PhotoSyncSpoolTest`; remote CI has not been run.
- This removes the identified collection growth in saved-reference refresh, but quantitative release heap/latency/storage budgets, actual process-death/low-storage acceptance, full/partial/no-library permissions, broad enumeration and automatic sync remain unfinished. The earlier owner-data incident remains unresolved; this work uses only isolated QA packages and updates the main app with install-r.


## October 27: explicit library access and manual sync

- Added Android API-specific image permissions (legacy read through API32, images from API33, selected visual media from API34), live full/partial/none resolution, and explicit reselection through Settings → Photos → Library access and sync. Partial-access references rely on their current Android permission; picker-retained grants remain separately owned. Unknown totals use an honest processed count.

These tests query only a private synthetic provider, never the owner's MediaStore library. CI now selects the access tests.

The app-permission settings link is implemented but its return/grant-change matrix remains open.

## October 27: optional original GPS and picker location sharing

- Added a separate explicit ACCESS_MEDIA_LOCATION request/status for original library-photo GPS. The control requires full/partial library access; granting it revalidates known references, without starting broad enumeration. Canonical MediaStore image URIs request original bytes only when the permission is currently allowed. Unsupported, missing or newly denied originals fall back to the readable ordinary URI. GPS is suppressed for eligible library originals when access is denied/revoked, while capture times and route/visit fallbacks remain available. Reference identity stays the original base URI. Document/picker/proxy/video URIs are not converted into original MediaStore reads, preserving their explicit scope and picker metadata decisions.

A delegated native picker contract sets Android's documented REQUEST_LOCATION_METADATA_ACCESS extra only for native ACTION_PICK_IMAGES when opted in, removes it when off, leaves document/fallback intents unchanged, and preserves selection order/cancellation parsing. The string form supports the API36 build; Android documents the extra in 37.1 and supported pickers/user choice determine whether GPS is returned. Older or declining pickers may still return redacted images. The library-options body now scrolls so the additional controls remain reachable.

## October 27: foreground automatic photo sync

- Added a persisted, default-off Automatic photo sync control to Library access and sync. Enabling also enables photo markers, matching the inspected iOS preference behavior. It requires existing library access and does not request permission implicitly. The application-owned controller observes MediaStore only while the activity is resumed, access is allowed and the option is enabled. It debounces changes for 300 ms, conflates bursts, and allows one follow-up after an active scan. Pause, disable and clear cancel/join active work; stale observer callbacks and completions cannot restart it. Stop discards queued wakes while retaining a fresh observer for later changes. No background WorkManager photo scan is scheduled.
- Resume compares each concrete external volume's MediaStore version and generation before using the ten-minute monotonic full-access cooldown. Changed stamps, GPS/access capabilities, map ranges, explicit refresh and failed prior scans bypass it. Partial access always refreshes on resume because its subset may change without changing the access enum; API29 or unavailable stamps also refresh. Settings-load/save errors are surfaced; a failed disable save inhibits scans in the current process until settings can be saved. This follows Android's [generation/version guidance](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/provider/MediaStore#getGeneration(android.content.Context,java.lang.String)) and [resume-time partial-access guidance](https://developer.android.com/about/versions/14/changes/partial-photo-video-access).
- Automatic discovery uses indexed MediaStore capture-time bounds and the existing 500-row/spooled pipeline. The range spans map dates, the earliest/latest point, visit and session endpoints, and today, rounded to local civil days with DST support. An all-dates selection retains representable bounds. Metadata is checked again against the requested range; unknown/out-of-range discoveries are excluded. Correlation uses that complete requested range for the iOS all-outlier rule. Saved references are revalidated first, with no intermediate ready state before discovery finishes. Clear-data persists automatic sync off and joins it before deleting photo/history data; clear failures now expose retry feedback. No schema migration is required.
- **42 isolated Pixel platform tests pass** in 10.613 seconds: ten automatic-controller, seven access/enumeration/observer, fourteen photo-controller, three spool and eight metadata-source tests. Coverage includes default-off/no-access isolation, version/cooldown/capability changes, partial/unknown-generation resumes, burst coalescing, failures, pause/disable/stop/clear cancellation, stale callbacks and an actual in-memory Room/spool pipeline that cannot repopulate after clear. A real ContentObserver registers/notifies/unregisters against the private synthetic provider. A ranged-provider test checks half-open capture-time filtering; a ranged sync test verifies full-range outlier fallback. The first run exposed five test methods whose expression bodies returned values instead of JUnit void; explicit Unit declarations fix initialization without changing assertions.
- **11 photo repository tests pass** in 2.938 seconds, including journal endpoint range aggregation and all-dates correlation bounds. **69 domain tests pass**, including two automatic range tests with a 23-hour DST day and future/history/map union. **11 isolated app tests pass** in 20.856 seconds. Debug/mapQa/test assembly and debug lint pass; CI includes the automatic controller suite but remote CI remains unrun. Native tests use isolated test packages, synthetic history and explicit test-owned photo URIs.
- Physical full/partial automatic sync, original-GPS grants/revocation, supported-picker consent, cross-provider URI alias deduplication, actual process-death/storage/performance/OEM acceptance and Play permission review remain open. The owner library remains ungranted and unscanned; the earlier owner-data loss remains unresolved.

- The final build passes the fresh five-page onboarding replay (**18 actions/checks plus echo**, 18.1 seconds), library-denial replay (**25 plus echo**, 28.7 seconds), and complete selected-photo picker/map/outing/cold-gallery/reference-removal replay (**151 plus two echoes**, 2m51s). The long replay has minor-motion idle warnings at 45/73/150 followed by passing destination gates; no new video or release motion/frame-rate verdict is claimed.
