## October 27: provider-confirmed photo identity

- Added local identity hints while preserving every original access URI. MediaStore identity combines the queried concrete volume, store version, row ID and creation generation (API29 uses the store version and ID). This distinguishes identical-looking copies and avoids merging separate store generations. Supported media/external-storage document URIs resolve through Android's getMediaUri contract; provider canonicalization can establish other aliases. Identity lookup never substitutes the URI used for EXIF, GPS, thumbnails or grant ownership. It reads explicit-reference metadata, without scanning to find a lookalike. Failed/unsupported equivalence remains unknown. Filenames, times, GPS and image hashes never establish identity.
- Schema **7** adds nullable indexed identity to photo_assets plus a shared visible_photo_assets view. Existing rows retain their metadata/grants and unknown identity until successful validation. Every alias stays in the base table for bounded revalidation and independent revocation. Gallery/map/outing counts, paging and ranks share the same view. Representatives prefer readable access, a capture date, GPS, explicit selection, then URI order. A readable dated library alias can therefore surface an image whose selected alias lacks EXIF time. Source invalidation closes a viewer if the representative changes. All-unavailable groups remain visible once in Settings and absent from the map. Removing a group deletes its saved moments/references transactionally and releases only its owned selected-photo grants; originals and unrelated export/other-photo grants remain intact.
- The metadata spool carries bounded identity hints through its checksum-verified batches. The provider lookup supports MediaStore image and file URI forms, verifies image MIME type, and uses only the original granted document's official equivalence. Opaque picker/cloud identities without a supported equivalence remain separate; universal cross-provider deduplication is not claimed. Android documents [getMediaUri's limited provider support and permission boundary](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/provider/MediaStore#getMediaUri(android.content.Context,android.net.Uri)) and [provider canonicalization](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/content/ContentResolver#canonicalize(android.net.Uri)).
- Added a test-only activity and UI Automator dependency in the isolated platform instrumentation APK. It opens the real document picker at one uniquely named test-owned MediaStore image, selects that exact filename, retains the returned grant, verifies its identity matches the MediaStore URI, releases/verifies removal of that grant, and deletes the exact fixture row. No broad media permission, owner-library query or production activity is involved. Synthetic provider tests cover explicit canonicalization, unsupported/malformed/denied equivalence, concrete-volume aliases, changed store versions and unavailable version stamps.
- Development verification caught Room's exact view-SQL quotation requirement; the migration now creates the view with the same quoted name as generated schema validation. It also confirmed that owning a MediaStore file does not itself grant the equivalent document URI; the real picker test now obtains that grant normally. The test provider authority was corrected. Assertions were retained; no broad permission or shell grant bypass was added.
- Final native verification passes **28 data tests** in 12.686 seconds (13 photo repository plus 15 history repository tests, including migrations from versions 1–6), **46 platform photo tests** in 14.457 seconds, **11 mapQa UI/contract tests** in 20.903 seconds, and **69 domain tests**. Alias coverage traverses 600 unique photos represented by 1,200 aliases through bounded pages/ranks while retaining the independent 500-map sample; tests also cover revocation fallback, all-unavailable groups, stable selection, metadata preference, independent lookalikes, group deletion, grant isolation and identity spool round trips. Debug/mapQa/test assembly and lint pass; eight native libraries retain 16 KB alignment, and APK dex checks exclude the identity test activity/class/provider. CI selects PhotoIdentityTest; remote CI remains unrun.

## October 27: webhook request, storage and delivery foundation

- Inspected current iOS WebhookManager/WebhookSettingsView: JSON/CSV/Markdown/GeoJSON/GPX/KML/OwnTracks/Overland; none/query-key/Bearer/Basic/custom-header authentication; real-time/count/time/manual modes; defaults off, real-time, JSON, ten points/five minutes; count choices 5/10/25/50/100 and intervals 1/5/15/30/60 minutes. The Android protocol now represents those choices, validates URLs/headers/credentials without echoing secrets, percent-encodes query key/value components, preserves unrelated query parameters, supports Unicode Basic credentials, and adds a stable Idempotency-Key. Explicit custom Cookie authentication remains supported while automatic cookie persistence is disabled. Transport-owned headers cannot be overridden. HTTPS is the default; HTTP requires an explicit setting, and production platform cleartext policy is unchanged.
- Added authenticated AES-256-GCM settings storage using an Android Keystore key, randomized IV and package/path/version-bound associated data. The complete configuration is encrypted, including endpoint paths/query strings, auth keys, usernames and values. AtomicFile framing and size limits bound reads; version/schema/field validation rejects corruption. Existing corrupt or key-invalid settings require explicit clear/reset rather than an implicit overwrite or replacement key. Missing settings return disabled without creating a file/key. Clear deletes the key and encrypted document. Configuration/request/credential/result string representations mask endpoint/auth material; network exceptions and server response bodies are not exposed. This uses Android's [Keystore GCM contract](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/security/keystore/KeyGenParameterSpec).
- Added private, fsynced immutable payload files produced by the existing streaming renderers, with size/SHA-256 checks, safe UUID paths and restoration from captured metadata after process recreation. Retries retain the original body and identity instead of rereading mutable history. Canceled/failed preparation deletes staging. A paid delivery boundary checks webhook access before validation and immediately before POST; callers must supply a generation fence, also checked during chunked writes. The OkHttp 4.12.0 client disables automatic redirects, connection-failure retries, cookies, authenticators and disk cache; it keeps platform TLS verification, sets 30-second connect/read/write and 60-second total timeouts, closes responses, and cancels/joins callback completion with its coroutine. Existing runtime dependencies resolve Okio 3.6.0. Source references/license text were added to third-party notices and packaged assets; the full notices UI audit remains open. [OkHttp's call contract](https://github.com/square/okhttp/blob/parent-4.12.0/okhttp/src/main/kotlin/okhttp3/Call.kt) explicitly allows ambiguous server acceptance after a network failure, so receiver-supported idempotency remains necessary for at-least-once delivery.
- Retry decisions match the iOS three-attempt classes (network failure/408/429/server errors; one/two-second base delays), with bounded Retry-After delta/date support up to one day. Successful responses complete; redirects and ordinary permanent client errors stop. This computes decisions only; no queue dispatcher or worker is enabled yet.
- **55 export JVM tests pass**, including nine new protocol tests for all auth/MIME mappings, escaping/injection boundaries, URL validation, explicit HTTP opt-in, masking, settings choices and retry classes/dates/bounds. **23 isolated Pixel platform tests pass** in 2.984 seconds: five new real-Keystore cases, nine new delivery cases, and the existing seven photo-access/two export-settings regressions. Real encryption tests verify non-exportable keys, fresh IVs, ciphertext without plaintext endpoint/credentials, recreation, tampering/truncation/oversize framing, relocated ciphertext, lost-key rejection, preserved committed settings on validation failure and clear/reset. Loopback-only HTTP tests POST all eight formats/five auth modes, restore the same payload across retries, preserve body/key fingerprints, ignore response cookies, stop redirects, reject purchase denial/stale generations/corrupt payloads before a request, classify offline/401/408/429/500, cancel a stalled response, and stream a 20,000-point payload. No owner history, real endpoint, purchased bypass or external receiver is used by these tests.
- Debug assembly/lint pass. CI now selects both webhook suites; remote CI has not run. The isolated test APK alone opts into cleartext for its loopback receiver. The production merged manifest adds no cleartext allowance or test component. No Room schema migration is needed for this foundation.
- The durable outbox remains unconnected to these APIs. Transactional accepted-point capture, real-time/count/time/manual dispatch, persisted retry/acknowledgement state, WorkManager reconciliation, purchase-readiness, disable/clear/revocation ordering, test-connection and queue/settings UI, actual receiver duplicate semantics and field/process-death/TLS/provider acceptance still require implementation. AppGraph does not instantiate this delivery stack, no owner endpoint is configured, and no live webhook egress is enabled. This is progress toward the full workflow, not a completed webhook feature or phone release.
- Updated the owner APK using install-r after confirming recording idle. The existing billing/free-preview replay initially stopped at its first gate because the main install had not completed onboarding after the earlier data incident. Reused the already recorded five-page body as the app-independent `onboarding-pages` fragment, with an explicit fresh-page prerequisite; it passes all **17 actions/checks** in 16.5 seconds and requests no location/notification permissions. The unchanged billing/free-preview replay then passes **19 actions/checks** uninterrupted in 21.3 seconds. This was a setup prerequisite, not a weakened product assertion. ## October 27: durable webhook capture and queue state

- Schema **8** adds a nonsecret capture policy, point snapshots and typed batch records. Live recording reads the policy once inside its existing writer transaction and captures only newly accepted record identities while automatic capture is enabled. Missing/disabled/manual policy does not capture, imported/history-only writes do not enqueue, duplicate IDs are not re-enqueued after acknowledgement, and accuracy/time/deadline rejections remain unchanged. Route/queue/dwell/recording progress commit together. A failed queue insert rolls back the fix so it can be retried without reporting a saved point that has no queue entry. Endpoint/auth material remains solely in encrypted settings, outside Room. AppGraph still does not activate a webhook policy or dispatcher.
- Snapshots preserve the accepted point fields independently of later history edits. Pending outlier corrections update/remove only unclaimed snapshots; toggling outlier inclusion does not backfill historical data. Claimed snapshots remain immutable so retries retain their original request bytes. Claims require an active matching revision, honor count thresholds/capture cutoffs, and select at most 500 records with indexed chronological reads. A durable existing head is resumed before a new claim. Typed manual entries retain already-frozen file metadata and complete point/visit counts without duplicating the historical records into the live buffer.
- Payload ID/format/size/SHA-256 become immutable before an attempt can be reserved. Atomic attempt reservations assign a unique token and 120-second lease; concurrent repositories cannot reserve the same batch. Results must match both configuration revision and attempt token. Success cascades deletion of only that batch's snapshots, preserves journal history and advances last success. Failures retain membership/body identity and persisted retry deadlines or stopped status. Three exhausted attempts, including interrupted leases, stop without discarding data; explicit retry starts another bounded attempt cycle using the same body identity. Discard rejects an active lease; its controller must cancel delivery and release/invalidate the reservation before discarding. Shared domain retry limits keep the database and HTTP policy aligned.
- Configuration changes no longer silently discard pending records. The default transition rejects a change with queued work; an explicit retain transition rebinds snapshots/batches to the new revision while preserving payload identities and invalidating old attempts. Explicit discard/disable removes pending data. The future controller must authorize retention against endpoint/format changes and cancel/join active network calls under its process/file lock before changing this mirror. Clear-all already empties the new tables through Room, leaving capture absent/off. Encrypted-settings reconciliation, payload-file cleanup and actual dispatch cancellation still require that controller; these data APIs alone are not the full clear/disable workflow.
- **44 isolated Pixel data tests pass** in 18.205 seconds: 15 new queue cases, 16 history cases and 13 photo cases. They cover capture/route rollback, no historical/manual capture, stable IDs, bounded membership, immutable history snapshots, eight-way concurrent claims/reservations, retry/lease recovery and exhaustion, stale completion/disable rejection, explicit retention/discard choices, outlier changes, manual metadata, database close/reopen and clear. A named WAL database test pauses recording after inserting the route row but before inserting its queue snapshot: an independent reader sees neither uncommitted row, disable waits for the writer, and final state retains the history point with capture disabled and its queue removed. These are synthetic transaction/object-recreation tests, not a real OS process-kill or field recording verdict.
- Migration tests now cover versions **1–7 → 8**, including reconstruction of the version-7 photo view, with preserved visits/points/photo metadata and no enabled webhook policy. **69 domain and 55 export JVM tests pass**; the existing **14 platform encryption/HTTP tests** also pass in 2.432 seconds. Debug/data-test/platform-test assembly and debug lint pass. CI already runs the full data suite except its separately scoped million-point test, so it includes WebhookQueueTest and the new migration case. Remote CI remains unrun.
- After confirming the owner app idle, updated it using install-r and replayed the unchanged billing/free-preview smoke check: **19 actions/checks pass** in 20.0 seconds. A minor-motion warning after the initial map idle check is followed by passing Export identity and free-preview checks. No new video or visual/frame-rate acceptance is claimed. The owner confirmed no existing history export is available; the earlier 13-point loss remains documented with no available recovery source. Full webhook controller/work scheduling/UI, receiver and field acceptance and the rest of the Android implementation plan remain active work.
- Final queue-only verification also passes all 15 tests after adding direct counts across every revision to the disable/clear checks. The final owner diagnostics remain zero points/visits/open sessions with recording idle. The owner package was updated, never uninstalled or instrumented; no owner data reset, webhook configuration or external delivery was performed.


## October 27: webhook orchestration and recoverable settings changes

- Added `WebhookCoordinator` as the single owner of configuration and delivery. A shared per-directory process mutex and cancellable OS file lock serialize instances; configuration mutations fence/cancel/join active preparation and HTTP before changing policy. Old attempts cannot acknowledge a newer revision. The encrypted settings store is now internal to the platform module so application callers use the coordinator. No AppGraph instance, worker, owner endpoint or automatic dispatcher is enabled yet.
- Settings use an encrypted prepared/active journal: persist the intended configuration and queue transition, apply Room idempotently, then commit the encrypted active document. Recovery either finishes that exact change or stops on inconsistent settings/queue state before HTTP. Legacy version-1 encrypted documents still load. Fractional/unknown envelope versions, lost keys and changed ciphertext fail closed. A disable can abandon a prepared transition that cannot transfer existing work; reset clears the queue/settings even after key loss without deleting history. Format changes cannot retain already-frozen bytes; default configuration changes still require an explicit retain/discard decision when work is pending.
- Realtime sends the current accepted-point window. Count mode waits for its selected threshold then freezes the complete buffer, matching the iOS flush behavior. Time mode uses the configured repeating interval anchored to policy creation and waits until a due window; actual background scheduling is still unfinished and may be delayed by Android. Complete windows use SQL membership assignment and 500-row streaming reads rather than a 500-point total cap. An explicit flush marks existing point batches and freezes all currently eligible remaining points; the intent survives reopening and manual-mode changes. Newer batches remain behind older batches even when their creation clock repeats or moves backward.
- Manual history sends freeze one read snapshot into a private file before queueing its counts/fingerprint. Explicit manual work can run while older automatic work is paused in manual mode. Test Connection sends only a synthetic zero-coordinate point, reads no saved history, and does not enable the saved configuration. Connection tests and all real delivery use the paid capability gate. Automatic mode also checks current location access; loss disables capture and discards its queued work before any further delivery. The application-wide permission/revocation/clear controllers still need to invoke this owner.
- Each actual attempt restores/verifies the frozen body and persists its reservation before HTTP. Response-aware retries preserve bytes and idempotency key. An interrupted prior reservation is recovered only under the exclusive delivery lock; it waits one second and still counts toward the three-attempt limit. Success removes only acknowledged snapshots and then their private body. Missing/corrupt bodies stop for review rather than rebuilding different bytes. Orphan cleanup streams only owned UUID `.tmp`/`.body` files, preserves referenced bodies and unrelated files, and runs under the same lock. An HTTP response followed by process death can still cause replay; receiver idempotency is required to suppress duplicate effects.
- Schema **9** adds an index for body-reference lookup. **35 isolated Pixel data tests pass** in 7.340 seconds (18 queue and 17 history cases), including versions **1–8 → 9**. The version-8 fixture now preserves an actual capture policy, queued point, frozen body metadata, attempt token and lease through migration. Additional queue cases cover 1,201-point windows, a later cutoff, flush ordering, exclusive recovery to the attempt limit and stopped-body retention.
- **33 isolated Pixel platform tests pass** in the final 14.609-second run: 16 coordinator, eight encrypted-settings and nine HTTP/payload tests. They include real Keystore round trips and legacy envelopes; named database close/reopen at both settings-journal checkpoints and a persisted HTTP retry; same-body replay after a response-before-ack checkpoint; authentication repair; concurrent owners; cancellation/join on disable; cancellable OS-lock wait; manual/flush/connection behavior; permission/purchase gates; corrupt bodies, orphan cleanup and key-loss reset. Checkpoint interruption and object/database recreation are synthetic recovery evidence, not a real OS process-kill or field verdict. A mixed-module test-class filter initially produced class-loading failures; separate module-scoped invocations pass. CI now includes the coordinator tests; remote CI remains unrun.
- **69 domain and 55 export JVM tests pass**, as do debug assembly and debug lint. The main owner app remains installed at schema 8; no owner update, instrumentation, history reset, permission grant or webhook configuration was performed during this milestone. The owner confirmed there is no existing history export to restore the earlier 13 lost points. No available recovery source has been found. WorkManager/foreground orchestration, the complete webhook settings UI, production HTTP policy/receiver acceptance and the remaining phone/release plan are still active work.


## October 27: webhook background dispatch and application ownership

- Connected the coordinator to AppGraph through a dedicated WorkManager scheduler and application-owned controller. Accepted-point queue changes now schedule realtime, threshold, due-time and explicit manual/flush work; no history import or disabled/default policy starts a delivery. Workers receive only a canonical configuration UUID; plan tags/names contain an opaque SHA-256 identity, never a URL, credential or raw point identity. A stale worker cannot send a newer configuration. The owner installation has not been updated or configured during this work.
- Unique pending work preserves its identity and saved deadline across reconciliation. HTTP retry attempts/deadlines remain in Room; a worker schedules a distinct successor after a retryable response. Clock rollback can create a delayed successor without canceling its running predecessor. Reconciliation preserves running work so observing an acknowledgment cannot cancel the worker that just succeeded. Completed history is excluded from scheduler queries/observation; failure lookup is limited to the current plan. WorkManager execution requires a connected network and is deferrable; this is not an exact-minute delivery guarantee. See Android's [work request and constraint guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/background-work/background-tasks/persistent/getting-started/define-work).
- Infrastructure/access exceptions use at most three WorkManager runs with exponential backoff; these do not substitute for the three persisted HTTP attempts. Retained failed work stays paused across ordinary queue updates; foreground or explicit user reconciliation can resume it. Work records request seven-day minimum retention, so this pause is backed by retained WorkManager state rather than a permanent Room suspension flag. Controller state observes failures and exposes a paused/error state. HTTP-stopped bodies continue to require explicit retry or discard. Boot/time/time-zone/package-replacement broadcasts enqueue only reconciliation, with a non-exported receiver and no HTTP in the broadcast callback.
- The controller owns load/save/test/history/flush/retry/discard/disable/reset actions across screen disposal. Disable/reset invalidate older queued UI commands and can cancel a live request before waiting for another action's mutex. Saved outlier preferences must load before pending snapshots are filtered; changes then update only the unclaimed policy/data. Foreground and recording-stop permission checks disable automatic webhooks and clear their queued work after cancellation/join. Manual-mode connection tests remain available without GPS permission and are not canceled by an irrelevant location check.
- Clear-all now pauses and joins controller actions, cancels delivery under the coordinator's process/OS lock, erases encrypted settings/payloads, executes the existing journal/photo/replay clear callback under that same lock, and cancels tagged work. Old workers are revision-fenced afterward. This integration changes no original photos or external exports. The application still has no webhook settings screen, so no endpoint can be enabled through the current UI. Production purchase gates remain active; synthetic paid callbacks exist only in isolated native test fixtures.
- A combined **53 isolated Pixel platform tests pass** in 27.228 seconds: 17 coordinator, eight encrypted-settings, nine HTTP/payload, nine WorkManager, six controller and four existing scheduled-export cases. New coverage includes constrained/unique/delayed work, opaque metadata, retry successor/body identity, stale revisions, failed-work pause/resume across new fixes, stopped HTTP failure, clock rollback, queue-driven scheduling/status, delayed preference loading, foreground and in-flight permission revocation, manual tests without GPS, and clear during a stalled request. Two initial controller failures came from synthetic timestamps before recording start; the fixtures now use valid recording timestamps and pass. After limiting queries to active/current-plan work, the final **15 scheduler/controller cases pass** again in 12.900 seconds.
- Added an actual MainActivity/AppGraph test restricted to **`com.bontecou.whereday.mapqa`**. It verifies fresh startup is disabled with no policy/work, then clears one explicitly synthetic point through the graph's guarded callback and verifies foreground reconciliation cannot resume a webhook. The initial app-target run passes in 1.788 seconds, and the final active-query implementation passes again in 1.779 seconds. This is lifecycle/data integration evidence, not a full visible settings-screen, motion or field verdict. The application test target remains `mapQa`; the owner package is never instrumented or uninstalled. CI includes the new graph, scheduler and controller cases; remote CI remains unrun.
- Debug/mapQa assembly, debug lint and the unchanged 69-domain/55-export JVM suites pass. No schema beyond version 9 or new production cleartext allowance is introduced. The main owner installation remains at schema 8 with no new permissions or webhook endpoint; its earlier 13-point loss still has no recovery export. Settings UI and its on-device accessibility/motion/large-text checks, explicit production HTTP policy, real Play verification/revocation, receiver acceptance and Android background/OEM/process-kill/field release gates remain open under the full implementation goal.

- Final verification confirms debug assembly/lint pass, the merged webhook receiver is non-exported, and no production cleartext override is present. Disposable app-test packages were removed by test cleanup; only the owner Whereday package remains installed. No owner APK update or owner journal operation was performed.

## October 28: shared journal read and rename commands

- Added AppGraph-owned `JournalCommands` for inclusive date-range visit/recorded-movement reads, complete point/count/distance statistics, current open outing lookup, and visit/outing/current-outing renames. The native outing editor now enters through the same command owner. This is an internal seam only: no new exported Android component, history provider, authentication bypass or paid export path is introduced. Start/stop/runtime duration, parameterized export dispatch and the authenticated opt-in client surface remain to be integrated.
- Read ranges match the iOS read intents' event-start semantics rather than map overlap semantics: both explicit endpoints are included, visits use arrival and movements use start. Day helpers honor an explicit IANA zone and exclude the next midnight, including DST days. Sub-millisecond bounds round inward for the millisecond database. Keyset pages retain equal-timestamp entities, cap each batch at 500, and keep a single committed WAL snapshot while another writer can commit. Consumers can cancel; callback consumers must not start a write transaction within the read callback. Exact stored movements are returned; inferred outings have no mutable stored identity.
- Rename transactions fetch current fields under the repository writer lock, trim/reject blank names and preserve notes, times, coordinates, corrections/detected values and country metadata. Visit rename changes only its display name and updated time. Current-outing read/rename share one newest-open-session lookup; this represents persisted open-session state and does not assert that a foreground recording service is running. A stale visit editor rejects a save after an automation rename rather than overwriting it. Native editor name-reset/notes behavior remains separate from the nonblank automation rename contract. No schema change is required.
- **26 isolated Pixel data tests pass** in the final run (nine command tests and 17 existing history/migration tests). Coverage includes 1,201 equal-time entities across pages, inclusive boundaries, overlap exclusion, negative-epoch/sub-millisecond bounds, a 23-hour DST day, streaming statistics/outliers, provenance-preserving rename, missing/blank inputs, current-outing selection, stale-editor rejection, cancellation and a real independent WAL writer during a snapshot. Debug/app assembly and debug lint pass. Existing CI runs this data suite excluding only the million-point scaling test; remote CI remains unrun. All fixture databases belong to the isolated data-test package and are removed afterward; the owner app was not updated, instrumented or queried.
- The owner delegated automation-client selection; **Automate** is the integration target. Its official [Content query contract](https://llamalab.com/automate/doc/block/content_query.html) supports bounded result rows, and [App start](https://llamalab.com/automate/doc/block/activity_start.html) delegates result-waiting to App decision. The actual scoped/authenticated transport and installed-client walkthrough are still open; selecting a client does not establish integration acceptance.


## October 28: automation authorization and private result transport

- Added AppGraph-owned `AutomationAccess` with default-off, explicit per-scope approval bound to package, UID, first-install time and the current SHA-256 signer set. Installed identity comes from PackageManager. No caller extra/referrer is trusted. Approval changes freeze reviewed scopes/identity, cancel/join active work and fence older queued requests across instances. Versioned AtomicFile state fails closed on corruption/unknown fields or scopes. A failed settings write inhibits the current process and surfaces failure rather than claiming persistence. The public command Activity and consent UI are still absent; no client can enable this through the current UI.
- Added bounded strict JSON requests for summary/visit/outing/current-outing reads, three rename actions, recording start/stop and complete version-2 export selection. Duplicate keys at every depth, trailing input, unsupported versions, unknown fields, incomplete/invalid dates and malformed export filters are rejected. Command scopes are explicit; request string descriptions redact user parameters. This is request validation, not yet an executor or a paid-operation bypass.
- Added streamed, fsynced immutable private result files with byte count/SHA-256, current approval revision/scope/recipient identity and ten-minute access expiry starting after write completion. Opens reauthorize and verify content; reboot, clock rollback and either wall/elapsed deadline deny access. Failed/canceled writes clean their owned files. Expired private files are swept on subsequent creation, clear-all or cache eviction, not by an exact-minute deletion promise. Reapproval does not revive older results. Existing open descriptors/previously read bytes cannot be withdrawn.
- A merged **non-exported**, read-only URI-grant provider serves only an exact result UUID and file-name/size metadata, never history queries. Unsupported columns, selections, write modes and malformed URIs are rejected. An actual native test exposed Android's anonymous MIME lookup; type resolution now returns a generic type without checking existence, while protected metadata/contents still authenticate the caller. The future command response must supply the actual MIME type. No external URI grant is issued by the app yet. The provider's own-UID ContentResolver paths pass; foreign-UID grant/forwarding acceptance remains pending.
- **26 isolated Pixel platform tests pass** in 2.601 seconds: nine access, five protocol, nine result-store and three provider tests. They include actual test-package signer lookup, installation changes, default-off/scope checks, active/queued cancellation, immutable consent snapshots, failed-write inhibition/reset, strict requests, all eight export selection formats, payload corruption, dual-clock/boot expiry and private cleanup. **One actual MainActivity Settings clear-all test passes** in 3.403 seconds in mapQa: it revokes access, cancels a pending write and removes a completed synthetic result. Debug assembly and debug lint pass. CI includes all new tests; remote CI remains unrun.
- [AUTOMATION.md](AUTOMATION.md) records the implemented contract and its limits. Automate's official [App decision](https://llamalab.com/automate/doc/block/activity_start_result.html) supports waiting for Activity results. Android documents [verified calling-package identity](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/app/Activity#getCallingPackage()) and [anonymous provider MIME lookup](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/content/ContentProvider#getTypeAnonymous(android.net.Uri)). Automate is not installed on the Pixel at the last package check. Public Activity/executor, consent/scopes UI, current runtime/recording and paid export dispatch, launcher shortcuts, real foreign-UID/process-death and installed-client acceptance remain active work. No owner APK update, owner data operation or new GPS/media permission was performed. The earlier 13-point loss remains unrecovered with no export available.


## October 28: automation execution and paid result delivery

- Added `AutomationExecutor` and an AppGraph factory binding the shared read/rename commands, result owner and export engine. Read responses stream complete JSON files instead of Binder-sized arrays. Visit/outing entities expose only their intended name/address/time/identity fields, not route coordinates, notes or correction provenance. Summary counts/distance include all requested points and take current recording duration from an explicit runtime binding. Recording start/stop dispatch distinguishes accepted requests from reported actual activity; the real foreground-Activity/service binding is still pending. Native tests use a synthetic adapter and never start GPS.
- Exports stream a single ZIP containing every selected format and generated day/outing-relative path, with filenames/subfolders intact. The executor uses saved inferred-outing settings through the production graph, and the existing engine retains all filter/field/splitting behavior. A new internal prepared-reader verifies size/SHA-256 under the same delivery lock and lifetime gate as file sharing, honors cancellation and generation changes, and rechecks after its final authorization. Prepared bundles receive best-effort private cleanup after use.
- Export results now require a dedicated paid authorizer before writing, before publication and again on metadata/content opens. The production result store uses PurchaseController's file-export gate; a result store without that authorizer fails closed for exports. ## October 28: real recording command binding and registration readiness

- Added `RecordingCommands` and `AppGraph.automation(activity)` as the real runtime binding. Start requires an existing location grant and a focused, nonfinishing foreground Activity, without prompting implicitly. A ResultReceiver acknowledgment returns only after the service accepts fused registration; Stop returns only after recording state closes. Caller waits are bounded to 15 seconds. Statistics require actual runtime activity plus a requested open session and derive duration from that session, with backward-clock clamping. A persisted requested flag alone remains inactive.
- Fused registration now suspends until Google Play services accepts it, with a ten-second bound. Cancellation unregisters immediately and again on a late completion; stale/stopped callbacks cannot deliver points into a new source. The service marks runtime active only after registration succeeds. Commands serialize through the service, ordinary/acknowledged starts have ephemeral cancellation tickets, duplicate active starts preserve the source, old callback generations are fenced, and start IDs protect later requests from earlier stop cleanup. Pending starts are protected from accidental relaunch interruption reconciliation.
- Clear-all and deleting the current outing await Stop before deleting data. Main's stop button and stop deep link use the same acknowledgment path, with concise error feedback on failure. Queued startup is canceled/joined before Stop acknowledgment. No new external command Activity, permission grant to the owner, foreground-service type or background-location requirement was introduced.
- A native canceled-start case first reproduced an actual `ForegroundServiceDidNotStartInTimeException` in the isolated test process. Canceled requests are now rejected before `startForegroundService`; a legitimately dispatched foreground request promotes before handling late cancellation, and illegal promotion stops the service rather than retaining its outstanding foreground promise. The same regression then passes. Native fixture Activity focus is injected only for permission/foreground guards; no native test starts real GPS. See Android's [deadline troubleshooting](https://developer.android.com/develop/background-work/services/fgs/troubleshooting).
- **53 combined isolated platform tests pass** in 7.715 seconds, including the previous 40 automation/export cases and **13 recording/provider/notification cases**. After adding the final pending-start recovery guard, those 13 pass again in **1.031 seconds** and **five actual mapQa settings/lifecycle/edit regressions pass** in **12.426 seconds**. Coverage includes delayed provider acceptance, denied/failed/canceled registration, late old-source completion, stale callbacks, truthful runtime duration, actual stop acknowledgment, canceled ticket rejection, no implicit permission prompt and notification Stop. Debug/mapQa assembly and debug lint pass; CI includes the new recording cases, while remote CI remains unrun.
- Installed only the disposable mapQa APK, without pregrant flags. The live first-run walkthrough accepted its own precise/while-in-use and notification prompts, observed real provider startup and three temporary GPS points, retained recording across Home/resume, and stopped through the acknowledged UI path. A live bridge wait after Stop was stale/unmet; its raw take is preserved in ignored captures. A separately recorded idle Home/resume tail confirms the stopped state, and the polished complete fresh-install replay passes **42 actions/checks plus two echoes**, no failures/errors/skips, **37.6 seconds**. The saved path is `.argent/e2e/recording-provider-qa-20261028.yaml`, requires fresh isolated QA onboarding/permission state, and intentionally starts a temporary real GPS recording. It never targets the owner package or enables online maps/exports. No raw coordinates, weak role targets, stored device IDs or fixed waits remain.
- Both QA installations and their temporary GPS data/permissions were removed afterward. Pixel-scoped Argent helpers were stopped; package verification shows only the owner Whereday app remains. No owner update, instrumentation, permission change or journal operation occurred. No new full-motion video, screen-lock/OEM/battery/visit-precision/process-death or release claim is made. The earlier owner-data incident remains unrecovered. Public automation Activity/consent UI, cross-UID result/error paths, launcher shortcuts, installed Automate flow and the full remaining implementation plan stay active.

## October 28: support, process-local diagnostics and development notes

- Matched the current `IsoMe/Services/LogManager.swift` / `IsoMe/Views/LogViewerView.swift` retention model: a process-local 500-event buffer, newest-first viewer, explicit clipboard copy and confirmed clear. Android uses fixed enum events rather than accepting free-form strings. Entries contain UTC instants, severity and static status text; there is no coordinate/name/address/URL/credential/exception payload field, disk persistence, Logcat forwarding or automatic upload. Concurrent writers publish immutable bounded snapshots. Process-owned logging is available before AppGraph initialization, so an early service rejection does not initialize Room/billing/work merely to log it.

## October 28: opt-in analytics contract and consent/queue core

- Inspected the current iOS event/client/Cloudflare transport and shared Worker. The existing receiver was iOS-only, so implemented the Android wire contract locally rather than sending unsupported events to production. Typed enums cover the existing eleven onboarding/purchase/restore names and coarse fields, plus Android's notification page/request and individual SKU. App version/build are bounded numeric values; there is no general property map, user text, device model, advertising ID, coordinate, client timestamp, path or support-log input. A shared synthetic JSON fixture matches the actual Kotlin encoder and is accepted by the local receiver.
- Added `UsageAnalyticsCore` with default-off collection, lazy post-consent metadata/UUID creation, a persisted 50-event queue, immutable snapshots and stable retry receipts. Calls before loading/approval are dropped. Explicit event-boundary permits prevent an asynchronous callback captured before consent, under an earlier consent epoch or from another engine owner from becoming collectable later. Missing transport cannot be approved. Reapproval while already enabled preserves current consent/receipts; off/on creates a new consent identity without replaying old events.
- Consent changes inhibit new collection/dispatch immediately and fence queued old work. Successful opt-out writes a disabled state with no identity/queue before waiting for an active transport to finish cancelling, so a process death during draining cannot revive that queue. Transport cancellation must close the actual HTTP call when the adapter is implemented. Already received bytes cannot be withdrawn. Failed reads/writes fail closed in this process and surface a storage error; no durable revocation is claimed after a failed write. Reset to disabled is required before approving again after corruption.
- Added a private, fsynced AtomicFile store. Strict versioned decoding rejects duplicate/unknown fields, unsupported values, invalid IDs, disabled states containing events and oversized input. The existing strict JSON helper keeps its 32 KiB automation default; the analytics store explicitly permits at most 64 KiB. No schema/database migration or owner-data operation is required.
- Tests found an async transport exception escaping its intended retry conversion through normal coroutineScope cancellation. Switched the owned send to supervisorScope and verified retry behavior. Added late-read/queued-enable regressions to prove a later opt-out cannot be undone by restored or waiting old consent. Strict malformed/unknown enum inputs now consistently fail validation; test fixtures catch the parser's documented malformed-input exception types rather than treating those as valid state.
- **Nineteen JVM tests pass**: eleven analytics-core cases (**0.161 seconds**), four analytics protocol/shared-fixture cases (**0.013 seconds**) and four existing local-log cases (**0.062 seconds**). **Nine isolated Android native cases pass** in **0.902 seconds**, covering four new AtomicFile/default-off/crash-recovery/corrupt-reset tests and five existing automation strict-parser regressions. Native tests target only `com.bontecou.whereday.platform.test` and unique synthetic directories, removed after testing. No GPS, permission grant, app UI interaction or real telemetry was used.
- Extended the local Worker allowlists for Android, enforced platform-specific SKU/notification values, and bounded unknown/chunked request streams before text decoding. Oversized streams are canceled. **Thirteen receiver tests pass** under local Node **26.8.1**, including eight legacy cases and five Android/privacy/stream/shared-fixture cases; TypeScript typecheck passes. Consulted current Workers best practices and official `@cloudflare/workers-types` **5.20261028.1**; no new binding API/configuration or deployment was introduced. CI now includes the native store case and a Node24 receiver-contract job; remote CI is unrun.
- Debug/mapQa packaging and lint pass. **Application integration remains unfinished:** AppGraph does not own/invoke this core yet; there is no opt-in UI, event producer, HTTPS adapter, retry scheduler or configured Android endpoint. The installed Android app still collects/sends no usage analytics. The receiver patch is local, undeployed and must not be assumed live. Server retention, Android disclosures, consent/revocation device matrix and the full remaining implementation plan remain active work. The owner's Pixel stays reserved for the other task; only the dedicated emulator was used for isolated library tests.

- The final event API requires an explicit opaque consent permit; it has no implicit fresh-permit default for a delayed collector to accidentally use. Rebuilt and reran the nineteen JVM and nine isolated native checks after tightening that signature. App UI, event hooks and network/background delivery remain the next integration work.

- Finished isolated test cleanup: no emulator-scoped Argent service was still running, and the dedicated emulator was shut down. The Pixel and other task’s emulator/helper were left alone. No analytics consent file, identifier, event or telemetry request was created in the owner or mapQa application; tests used only the library-test app and in-process fake receiver.


## October 28: analytics app ownership, explicit consent and delivery integration

- AppGraph now owns the analytics controller, AtomicFile state, configured transport and background scheduler. The controller captures consent synchronously before dispatching event work; changing consent inhibits collection/cancels in-flight delivery immediately. Controls and clear operations are application-owned, with revision fences and serialized writes; overlapping clears keep approval inhibited until every clear completes. Actual journal clear invokes analytics revocation first. This does not resolve the earlier owner-history loss.

Permission and billing/restore outcome hooks remain open.
- Build availability defaults **false**. A configured build uses a fixed HTTPS endpoint and optional extractable abuse-control key; no runtime destination switch or enabled production fallback exists. The Android receiver patch remains undeployed. HTTP has no redirect following or automatic connection retry, bounded deadlines/receipt reads, strict acknowledgments, and cancellation that closes its owned call. A local consent-to-HTTP test verifies zero pre-consent requests/metadata reads, exact retry bytes/receipt, acknowledgement and post-revocation inhibition.
- Background jobs contain no consent identifier or event input. Unique constrained one-time work drains at most ten events; periodic 15-minute recovery covers a new event racing a finishing unique job. Both use exponential backoff and only their own analytics tag is cancelled. Native tests verify default-off scheduling, unique connectivity-constrained jobs, empty worker input, bounded drain and cancellation after revocation.

## October 28: consent-fenced permission and purchase event hooks

- Added opaque controller action permits captured synchronously before asynchronous system/store actions. Null, foreign, revoked and old-epoch permits cannot evaluate a completion event factory. `UsageFunnel` keeps only reviewed enum metadata and an original permit, suppresses duplicate completions, and orders completion persistence after the start event. Pre-consent requests and callbacks arriving after off/on cannot be reassigned to new consent. Unknown configured product strings are omitted, never uploaded as arbitrary text.
- MainActivity now instruments only onboarding-originated foreground-location and API33+ notification requests/results. Android's ambiguous pre-request state stays `unknown`; notification approval is a distinct `authorized` value valid only for an Android notification request, never a location/background grant. Both Kotlin validation and the undeployed receiver enforce the distinction, with a second shared encoder/receiver fixture. Activity/process recreation intentionally drops outstanding attempt metadata; it never captures new consent to reconstruct a lost callback. Foreground and background recording behavior remains separate from onboarding events.
- Wired Export and webhook purchase surfaces, direct click-boundary purchase/restore starts, and store results. Only explicit Restore clicks create restore events; automatic refresh/resume does not. BillingSession now returns typed verified/pending/not-found/unavailable/failed results while preserving entitlement behavior. A cached valid offline grant remains usable but its failed refresh is not counted as fresh verification. Play-sheet checkout remains occupied until its result callback instead of reopening on Activity resume; result handling takes its pending analytics attempt before reopening checkout. Duplicate callback completion is suppressed. Real Play checkout, pending-payment resumption and account/refund behavior still require configured-store acceptance.
- Welcome-start is observed only while Welcome is currently visible under consent; earlier pages are not reconstructed. Completion truthfully records Android's current later-start behavior. The iOS ready-page immediate-start choice is still absent on Android, so that choice/intent hook remains a parity task rather than reporting an action that did not occur. No new visible UI was introduced by these event hooks.

- **11 native cases pass** on the dedicated API35 emulator: 2 actual PurchaseController tests in `com.bontecou.whereday.platform.test`, 7 existing analytics/settings/webhook navigation cases and 2 new actual-consent integration cases in disposable `com.bontecou.whereday.mapqa`. ## October 28: historical Markdown portability

- Inspected the current iOS ImportService legacy readers and ExportService escaping. Android previously handled only ISO Markdown tables; its file detection also treated section-only legacy files as CSV. Added one shared content detector used by the actual import picker, retaining JSON/BOM and quoted/unquoted CSV precedence before Markdown-looking content inside notes.
- Added historical iOS `##` full-date headings, `###` visit sections with Arrived/Departed/Address/Coordinates and blockquote notes, local-clock visit/point tables, and positional point rows without headers. Modern ISO tables continue to import. Historical timestamps use explicit locale/zone arguments, defaulting in the UI to the device context as in the iOS reader. Tested US/Puerto Rico and French/Paris contexts; no assumption that every export is US English or UTC. ISO instants remain independent of device context.
- Invalid date headings reset context instead of reusing yesterday. Malformed section timestamps/coordinates are row errors; a bad departure cannot silently produce an ongoing visit. DST gaps reject the affected row; overlaps use the first valid offset. Missing optional cells and open stays are accepted. Column counts, duplicate raw headers and duplicate coordinate aliases are validated before constructing records. Escaped delimiters, paired backslashes, empty cells and line breaks are handled together, so a literal backslash before a separator does not hide the separator.
- Round-trip tests exposed an existing Android writer defect: literal backslashes were not escaped before pipes. Corrected list and streaming history tables and both outing-table paths to follow the current iOS backslash convention. Historical Android bytes containing unescaped paired backslashes are ambiguous; no lossless recovery claim is made for those malformed old outputs. Stable legacy identities are derived from the resulting instant and coordinate, so the same context reimports idempotently.
- **65 export/import JVM cases pass**, including **10 new legacy/content-detection cases**, plus **2 native Android persistence cases** in `com.bontecou.whereday.platform.test` on the dedicated API35 AVD. Native tests use only an in-memory synthetic database: shared detection/parse → atomic upsert twice → SQLite read → streaming modern Markdown → reimport preserves counts, identities, text/backslashes, coordinates and times. French/local-time and DST behavior also pass on Android. Packaging and lint pass. Added the native case to existing CI; remote CI is unrun.
- New cases include complete/open visits, date changes, quoted CSV precedence, BOMs, optional values/units, malformed sections, bad row widths, duplicate aliases, positional tables, non-US locale, DST gap/overlap, escaping and stable JSON conversion. They are synthetic examples based on inspected reader syntax, not falsely labelled iOS-generated golden exports. Early local iterations exposed a fixture literal typo and nullable writer-chain compile errors; corrected those before the final green run. First writer round-trip failure is retained as evidence of the backslash defect. Final evidence and source hashes are saved under ignored `captures/legacy-markdown-20261028/`.
- This integrates the new parser into the existing import path without changing visible UI. It does not complete history import preview/cancellation, every locale/legacy variant, iOS-generated golden fixtures, actual system-picker/provider and physical iOS↔Android round trips, release-scale budgets or the rest of the plan. No app was launched, no permission was granted, no real history was imported and no owner package was changed. Pixel and the other task’s emulator/helper remain untouched. Visit reconciliation remains open because merged correction/original provenance needs a complete preservation policy; no destructive merge was introduced as a shortcut.

- Final cleanup: no dedicated-emulator Argent helper was running; shut down only emulator-5556 cleanly after preserving native/XML results. No recorder/video is active, and the full goal remains active.


## October 28: review, cancel and commit history imports

- Replaced immediate file-selection upserts with an application-owned review flow wired into the real Export picker. The selected file is read/decoded/parsed without history writes. Review shows format/counts/date bounds, original device locale/zone context for legacy Markdown, existing-ID matches, repeated source rows and up to ten bounded row errors. Valid rows can be imported explicitly; Discard and Back leave history unchanged. Conflicting source values with one identity fail instead of silently choosing the last row. Fixed singular/plural count copy after the first walkthrough.
- Default keep-existing preserves local records. Explicit replace requires another confirmation and an exact row snapshot recheck inside the atomic database write. Changed matches reject the whole replacement before writing. Added bounded 200-ID lookup batches without a schema migration. Point replacement retains session membership, and a newly closed detector-owned visit resets its dwell state. Matching remains ID-based; cross-format semantic reconciliation and complete correction provenance preservation are still open.
- Added UTF-8 rejection, retained bounded file reads and cancellation checkpoints in JSON reader/record iteration, CSV scanning and Markdown lines/cells. Unsupported timestamp ranges fail before preview/database inspection. The in-memory review never persists its source URI or auto-replays after process death. Application ownership retains it across ordinary screen/Activity recreation. Imports and their contents are not analytics events.
- Reading cancellation closes/cancels owned handles asynchronously and immediately fences/detaches stale work; a provider that ignores cancellation cannot publish a late review or hold journal clear hostage. Resources controlled by that provider may remain until it responds. A cancel click arriving just after parsing discards the review too. Writes use a per-operation cooperative cancellation flag rather than canceling across SQLite commit: a pre-decision cancel throws and rolls back, while an already committed result is reported honestly. Journal clear blocks new imports through the actual clear callback, drains an approved write and discards old state before reopening imports. A recreated screen cannot revive an old import.
- **66 export/import JVM cases pass**, including the new cancellation checkpoint case across all supported parsers, plus **11 controller cases** for review-only selection, discard/stale identity, blocked input close, early cancellation, pre/post-commit cancellation, repeated/conflicting IDs, clear exclusion, uncooperative provider fencing, input size, UTF-8 and timestamp limits. **Four isolated native database cases pass** for no-write review/keep behavior, session/dwell preservation, stale replacement rejection and mid-write rollback. **Five native UI cases pass** for default policy/no auto-import, separate replacement approval/cancel, honest pending cancellation, dark/1.6 text-scale reachability and resetting replacement to keep-existing after recreation. Count-copy refinements were followed by compiled/linted resource checks and the final live replay. These **86 targeted cases**, packaging and lint pass; new classes are included in CI, but remote CI is unrun.

## October 28: Swift history-import compatibility and reverse JSON/CSV acceptance

- Compiled the complete current iOS `ImportService.swift` with its real format enum and a Foundation-only synthetic harness. Fifteen checked-in cases now compare every represented visit/point field against actual Swift results: correction/original/detected metadata, open visits/nulls, Unicode/quotes/multiline text, offsets, Unix fallback and historical English Markdown in Puerto Rico time. The generator records source/harness/input/self hashes; CI checks them and now runs for changes to the iOS importer. This does not substitute a Kotlin reimplementation for the reference parser.
- Fixed Android JSON import rejecting otherwise valid records merely because a future unknown field contained an object/array. Unknown fields are ignored; known scalar fields still reject structured values with a row error. JSON null remains missing, while literal `null`, `Ongoing`, `Still here` and `—` in names/addresses/notes survive. CSV literal text also survives; Markdown-specific placeholder handling remains in its own reader. Legacy Markdown display labels normalize only the source/status columns, preserving `Automatic`/`Corrected` provenance rather than falling back to imported/unconfirmed.
- Point timestamp resolution now uses a valid ISO instant or the iOS-compatible Unix fallback when ISO is absent/invalid. The same resolved instant determines deterministic legacy identity and the point value. Decimal Unix seconds preserve parser nanoseconds, including negative fractions; nonfinite/out-of-range values fail instead of saturating a `Long` millisecond value. Extreme scientific exponents are rejected or reduced below the nanosecond threshold without expanding enormous powers of ten. Room persistence remains millisecond-based.
- Added six validation regressions covering known structured fields versus valid neighbors, nested future fields, fallback identity, fractional/extreme Unix values, ISO precedence and CSV literal placeholders. Added a build-only producer of four real Android-rendered files (JSON/CSV × visits/points), with assertions against original models for all representable fields. The actual complete Swift importer accepted all four files and matched every represented value, including multiline/Unicode text, open visits, outliers and JSON provenance. Payload and both platform source hashes are recorded in `export/build/history-interop/swift-acceptance.json`.
- **88 export/import JVM cases and 11 import-controller cases pass (99 targeted)**. Final mapQa assembly/lint pass. The checked-in Swift fixture provenance check passes; all four reverse-direction Swift checks pass after the final test/model assertions. Linux CI verifies Kotlin/provenance and uploads generated files; macOS Swift execution currently remains a documented local command. An initial fixture pass exposed nine real compatibility failures plus two harness assumptions: the conversion check needed an explicit pre-epoch range, and the current Swift CSV parser needs LF record separators in this case. Corrected those harness inputs without weakening Android's RFC4180/CRLF tests; no claim of universal iOS CSV acceptance is made.
- Evidence is retained under ignored `captures/history-interop-20261028/`: failing baseline, passing JVM/build logs, XML reports, generated Android files, actual Swift acceptance and source/APK hashes. No device was booted or interacted with, no runtime permission was granted and no user data/network service was used. The Pixel, owner package and other task's emulator/helper remain untouched. No iOS production file was changed.
- Full eight-format renderer goldens, Android Markdown accepted by iOS, combined imports, UUID/session/country/photo limitations, complete locale/provider/physical round trips, semantic reconciliation, streaming imports beyond the temporary 25 MB cap and release budgets remain open. This milestone advances the full active plan without claiming phone parity or release readiness.

## October 28: Markdown and combined-file acceptance through the iOS app entry point

- Reproduced Android Markdown failing in the current Swift importer (`No visits found in Markdown file`) while JSON/CSV passed. Preserved the working-tree branding edits already present in that file.
- Swift visit tables now accept absolute ISO arrival/departure instants without a local date heading, including offset/cross-midnight times, while retaining historical date/clock input. The new negative cases prove invalid departure, duplicate alias and stale date-heading rejection.
- The Android build-only producer now emits **150 files**, covering JSON/CSV/Markdown, list/streaming renderers, six visit field choices, all sixteen point-field combinations, combined records and combined files with either subject empty. Each producer checks representable fields against original models. The complete actual Swift source is compiled and **all 150 pass through public importFile**, comparing every represented field and checking the absent subject stays absent for subject-specific exports. This supersedes the earlier four/six-file typed-entry evidence. The Android format is unchanged, retaining ISO timestamps and correct escaping.

CI runs the expanded Kotlin contracts, checks current Swift source/harness/input/generator hashes and saves the producer output. Actual Swift execution remains a documented local macOS check. ## October 28: incremental import reader and constrained-heap scale foundation

- Replaced whole JSON-document DOM and CSV row-list parsing with a shared `HistoryImporter.scan(Reader, format, ..., onVisit, onPoint)` path. The existing text/list API now collects from the same implementation, so production import decoding uses this reader. JSON reads only known scalar fields and skips unknown structured metadata; CSV emits RFC4180 logical rows across arbitrary reader chunks; Markdown emits records from the current table/legacy section. None of the scanner implementations retains earlier records. Large individual values/sections still consume memory proportional to that record; a per-record release budget has not been accepted.
- Checkpoints run during input reads, unknown metadata skipping and per-record processing. Sink errors/cancellation escape directly instead of being converted to malformed-row messages, including visit/point tables and legacy-section EOF flush. The caller owns its reader. Emissions are provisional until successful EOF validation: malformed/trailing JSON and duplicate subject arrays fail the whole scan. Future disk staging must discard those provisional emissions on failure. Known duplicate JSON fields and duplicate CSV columns reject their record rather than silently taking the last value. Standard strict JSON is now required.
- Retain at most ten diagnostics of 200 characters while counting all rejected rows separately in `ImportResult.rejected`/`ImportScan.rejected`. Import review uses the exact total rather than the diagnostic-list length. A controller regression verifies 2,000 rejected rows, one valid point, ten bounded messages and no write before approval. This eliminates an error-list growth path without underreporting rejected input.
- Seven new streaming-reader regressions cover all twenty-nine actual Swift fixtures at reader chunk sizes 1/7/8192, exact/bounded diagnostics, visit/point/legacy sink failures, malformed tails, unknown-metadata cancellation, duplicate fields/columns and reader ownership. Existing identity/metadata/CSV escaping/locale/time-zone contracts remain green. The full current Android producer output also passes all **150 public Swift import checks**, with both platforms' decoder/renderer source hashes retained.
- Added a dedicated **96 MiB JVM scale gate** that lazily generates and consumes **one million records per format**, verifies each timestamp and complete counts, and verifies the first emission occurs before EOF. JSON processes 67,000,012 characters, CSV 28,000,029 and Markdown 36,000,041, all with heap maximum 100,663,296 bytes and no complete input/history collection in source or sink. This is a structural parser memory check, not a device or whole-app memory/latency budget. The ordinary suite excludes this class; CI explicitly runs `:export:streamingImportBudgetTest` and uploads its report.
- **109 ordinary export/import cases + one constrained-heap scale case + 12 import-controller cases pass (122 targeted)**. Final integrated mapQa assembly/lint, current fixture provenance and the public Swift acceptance pass. Evidence, logs, XML, acceptance files and source/APK hashes are saved under ignored `captures/stream-import-reader-20261028/`. No app/device was launched, no runtime permission was granted and no actual journal was touched. Pixel, owner package and the other task's emulator/helper remain untouched.
- This is the required parser foundation, not completion of large-file importing. The picker still retains bounded source bytes/text, parsed collections, ID maps and complete matching baselines. **The 25 MB guard remains in place.** Next work must introduce private disk-backed source/review staging, indexed deduplication and baseline storage, bounded database batches within one approved atomic commit, clean cancellation/clear/orphan handling and native large-history/crash/resource acceptance before lifting that guard. Existing physical/locale/provider/semantic and broader phone/release gates remain open; the full goal stays active.

## October 28: disk-backed review, bounded commits and million-record native imports

- Replaced the production controller's whole-file bytes/text, record lists, identity maps and complete match snapshots with private disposable workspaces. It streams source bytes to disk, detects format through a repeatable reader, validates UTF-8 and emits records into a separate SQLite scratch index. The index holds at most 200 pending records, detects repeated/conflicting identities across flushed batches, and pages at most 200 records. Successful parsing deletes the raw source before matching. Review keeps only counts/range/context and ten bounded diagnostics in memory. **The 25 MB total-file guard is removed for JSON, CSV and Markdown.** Disk availability, individual-record/cursor limits and device budgets remain constraints. No history schema migration was needed.
- Matching runs against one history snapshot and stores complete-row SHA-256 fingerprints, including session/correction/country metadata, in the stage. A checksum seals identities, values and baselines. Approval verifies the stage, processes bounded lookups/writes within one Room transaction, rechecks replacement fingerprints, preserves current point session membership, and releases closed detector visit ownership. A stale later batch rolls back earlier writes. Keep-existing respects current matches and post-review edits. A final stage verification/token check precedes the commit decision; post-decision cancellation still reports actual committed counts. The legacy small-list target helpers remain available for existing contract tests; AppGraph and the controller require the staged target path.
- Application ownership preserves review across ordinary UI recreation. Discard, cancellation and completion retire owned workspaces off the UI thread. Clear drains an approved transaction, joins local cleanup and strictly reaps inactive staging before the real journal-clear callback. External provider cancellation remains separate, so an uncooperative read cannot hold local cleanup/clear or publish a late review. Factory reconstruction best-effort reaps abandoned directories, protects active reviews across factory instances and retries cleanup strictly on create/clear; a damaged cache does not disable free recording. Process death drops review authority and never auto-replays an import. Staging stays outside the FileProvider export root and is not analytics data.
- **Seven final native regressions pass** for all-format no-write review/default keep, replacement/session/dwell behavior, stale later-batch rollback, cancellation after writes begin, corrupt sealed-stage rejection, identity conflicts after buffer flush, startup/orphan/live-review isolation, invalid UTF-8/bad EOF and cleanup. The first run exposed a fixture assumption: exports omit UUIDs, so source-identity cases now explicitly supply supported IDs, while legacy matching uses deterministic IDs. A JVM metadata case verifies full private payload fidelity and fingerprint sensitivity, including nanoseconds, correction originals and country/session fields. The twelve controller lifecycle cases still pass using a small in-memory stage double; production always uses the disk factory.
- **Three final native scale cases pass** on the dedicated API35 AVD with a 201,326,592-byte Java heap limit. Each format reviews and imports one million points from a source larger than 25 MB, verifies no writes before approval, then reimports all one million matches with zero duplicate writes and preserves an intervening local accuracy/session edit. Each case clears the journal and all owned staging. Inputs and expected records are generated lazily; no complete history collection exists in the source/sink. The full ten-case native run passes in 13m12s. Measured review / full import+reimport+clear times and sampled Java usage are:

  | Format | Source bytes | Review | Full case | Sampled Java usage maximum |
  | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
  | JSON | 67,000,012 | 81,719 ms | 245,703 ms | 9,010,416 B |
  | CSV | 28,000,029 | 76,242 ms | 250,184 ms | 8,936,688 B |
  | Markdown | 36,000,041 | 90,160 ms | 290,806 ms | 194,968,128 B |

  Samples include uncollected allocations and exclude native SQLite/PSS; they are not retained-memory or release/device budgets. The initial single JSON review/commit/clear also passed before the expanded matrix. An interrupted test's delayed UTP cleanup removed the disposable APK during an immediate restart; after identifying package removal in the log, verified that no UTP child remained and reran the whole matrix successfully. The test's first-point query was corrected to use the Room-supported millisecond range rather than Instant.MAX. No owner package was instrumented or uninstalled.

Final mapQa assembly/lint and fixture provenance pass; all **150 public Swift file checks** pass. A fresh onboarding replay passes eighteen checks without recording permissions. The unchanged real DocumentsUI import replay passes **94 checks/actions plus echo in 49.9s**, covering discard, approval, keep-existing reimport and the actual Settings clear wrapper. ## October 28: bounded import records and storage-failure rollback

- Added retained-text bounds of 256 Ki UTF-16 code units per logical record, including format-specific field/metadata overhead, and 256 columns. Known JSON scalars are guarded before string materialization; unknown metadata still skips incrementally. CSV budgets span quoted multiline rows; Markdown budgets cover tables and entire legacy sections. Oversized input aborts review with an actionable error, rather than silently omitting a record. The total-file cap remains removed.
- Encoded scratch records, pending payloads and paged payloads are limited to 1 MiB, alongside the existing 200-record bound. Matching measures existing journal text in SQLite before loading Java records, splitting groups above 512 KiB; a single record may contain at most 1 MiB of text. The single-record allowance preserves reimport of valid large Unicode records. Oversized existing text fails review without modifying that record. No history schema migration was required.
- Strict UTF-8 validation now happens during capture, rejecting malformed bytes without staging the rest of a large binary file. Scratch open/write/flush/close failures become explicit storage errors, and the underlying sink closes even when the encoder fails during close. Native SQLite-full tests cap only disposable test databases: scratch exhaustion cannot produce approval, and journal exhaustion rolls back the approved transaction while preserving a pre-existing sentinel. Room rollback may wrap SQLite failures, so write errors are mapped at the transaction boundary without exposing raw database errors.

## October 28: chronological visit merges and stale-open reconciliation

- Ported current iOS `LocationManager` duplicate rules: 100 m coordinate threshold, inclusive fifteen-minute arrival tolerance or thirty-minute interval gap. Android uses its platform ellipsoidal distance for actual database decisions. The chronological target retains identity, coordinates and correction/country provenance; missing represented names/addresses fill from the source, distinct nonempty notes combine, either open input keeps the result open and inferred-departure flags combine. Equal-arrival order is deterministic by ID; iOS sorting leaves that tie unspecified. This does not invent a separate correction-priority policy for a later source visit.
- New detector arrivals close preceding open visits and merge into an existing matching stop inside the same point/state transaction. Dwell ownership transfers to the survivor; subsequent departure updates that identity. Startup repairs multiple open visits by retaining the latest arrival and closing earlier ones at the next strictly later arrival, then merges duplicates chronologically. Paged candidate queries use conservative latitude/time prefilters before exact distance checks; the implementation does not collect the full visit history. One Room transaction covers closures, merged values, deletion and detector remapping, with cancellation checkpoints. Failed startup repair emits fixed local diagnostics/message instead of crashing the Activity or exposing an exception.
- The new macOS fixture generator executes the actual Swift method bodies/constants with synthetic model adapters, not rewritten reference algorithms. Fourteen deterministic cases cover gap/distance/arrival behavior, open/future visits, identity exclusion, notes/metadata, inferred departures and date-line coordinates. Source/model/harness/generator hashes and the native asset copy are checked by CI. Android domain tests compare represented merge results; native tests verify all fourteen distance decisions with the real Android geodesic calculation. This evidence does not cover every iOS-only model field or SwiftData persistence.
- **100 targeted tests pass:** 73 domain cases, four local activity-log cases and 23 isolated native data cases (six reconciliation plus seventeen existing recording/repository cases). Native evidence includes 505 merges crossing page boundaries with correction/country/notes retained, repeated repair idempotence, strict-next-arrival closure, stale detector release, automatic-arrival identity transfer and real subsequent departure, date-line versus remote/separated stops, and a trigger-induced failure during a later merge. That failure restores all original visits and detector ownership, proving earlier closures/writes/deletions roll back. Final mapQa assembly/lint and fixture provenance pass.
