## October 28: foreground map location and camera follow

- Matched the main iOS map's user-location/follow capability with explicit Android controls. Follow defaults off and requests foreground coarse/fine access only after the user's action and explanation. The actual current position is separate from stored route samples, date filters, the recording service and analytics. Approximate fixes remain usable and cap the camera zoom; accuracy is displayed in metric or imperial units. Monotonic fix age rejects stale/future positions without depending on the wall clock. Invalid coordinates/accuracy are ignored; a position expires after thirty seconds without a usable update and the UI explains waiting/settings failures.
- Camera following and visible location are separate states: Fit route or a native map gesture releases camera tracking while the marker remains available; Follow recenters the same accepted fix, and Hide removes the marker and cancels collection. Updates use a separate balanced-power fused callback with bounded conflated delivery, only while the map is resumed and its window focused. Covering dialogs, backgrounding and leaving the composition cancel collection and clear its ephemeral fix. Returning resumes the current composition's explicit choice; recreation/process death defaults off. Online tiles are still a separate off-by-default option, and no map-only sample reaches the journal.
- **90 targeted tests pass:** 76 domain tests, eight native location-source cases and six native map UI cases. Tests cover freshness/invalid/future/coarse fixes and polar zoom; actual Android non-batched request settings; denied/failed registration and late canceled callbacks; real MapLibre camera/marker rendering; Fit/re-follow of the same fix; panning; explicit Hide; background/resume and dialog cleanup; a scrollable 1.8× font permission explanation; and the existing late-photo-sprite regression. The first source assertion assumed the provider would retain a zero max-delay field; Play services normalizes it to the interval, so the final assertion checks its actual non-batched flag. No production behavior was relaxed.
- Final mapQa assembly/lint pass. Two intermediate native runs collided with the live Argent helper's UiAutomation registration after the walkthrough; stopping only `emulator-5556`'s Argent services allowed the complete unchanged native matrix to pass. This was an automation-service ownership conflict, not a map feature failure. Its tap selectors are semantic, with no retained raw coordinate/gesture exceptions. The standard permission explanation was visually inspected; the temporary capture was removed when its scoped helper stopped, so it is not retained evidence.

## October 28: authenticated purchase notifications and terminal revocation fences

- Added the local `/v1/rtdn` Worker route, disabled by default with empty audience/sender/subscription settings. It verifies Google RS256 OIDC signatures through pinned `jose` 6.2.13 and a fixed, bounded, timeout/redirect-constrained Google JWKS endpoint. Issuer, exact scalar audience, configured numeric subject/email, verified-email flag, issue/expiry/age and algorithm checks precede payload processing. Public key metadata is cacheable; bearer tokens, proofs, bodies and credential-bearing upstream URLs are not logged or retained. Existing outbound tracing stays disabled. Google/Cloudflare/library primary references and current Workers types were reviewed for this implementation.
- Wrapped envelopes require the configured subscription/package/version and one supported event, with bounded JSON, strict UTF-8 decoded data, timestamps and fields. Purchased/canceled events re-query the exact Publisher product/token; unsettled/pending or unavailable results retry. Full one-time void events create a terminal hash-only fence, including tokens not yet granted. Notifications never grant a purchase, issue a client lease or acknowledge a Play purchase. Other products/subscriptions/partial-quantity refunds and pending review events are outside the single-quantity lifetime entitlement policy; the README records the remaining review/operations work.
- Migration `0002_revocations.sql` adds permanent token-hash tombstones and bounded-retention notification receipts, preserving legacy revoked grants. Revocation updates its tombstone/grant atomically; grants cannot reactivate a terminal or older revoked row. Verification checks revocation after acknowledgment/signing and returns revoked for tested concurrent notifications before that final check. Existing leases already issued still obey their bounded cache/refresh policy; no immediate remote-recall claim is made.
- A durable claim/receipt controls delivery: only completed duplicates acknowledge immediately; in-progress duplicates retry, expired owners may be replaced, and stale owners cannot complete/delete a newer claim. Receipt failure after revocation leaves the fence intact for a safe retry. Cleanup expires receipt hashes after 35 days while retaining terminal revocations. No raw purchase token, order, sender JWT or incoming message body is persisted.
- **Sixteen local Worker tests pass**, including the original five verification contracts, synthetic signed sender/tampering checks, real-D1 concurrency/crash/expiry/privacy cases, unseen/known refunds, stale delivery and migration preservation. Races revoke before grant, during acknowledgment and during signing; none returns an active lease in those tested windows. An HTTP-level test uses intercepted Google JWKS, authenticates the actual route and verifies 204 only after durable revocation. Final generated binding types, TypeScript check and local Wrangler bundle dry-run pass. Existing CI includes the tests, but remote CI remains unrun. No Android binary or device was changed in this milestone.
- Production Play/Cloud identities, keys, topic/subscription permissions, deployment, real deliveries and dead-letter/refund operations remain required. The cleanup cron still does not query Play: a resumable bounded Voided Purchases pull reconciliation is the next required purchase backend implementation. Customer cache terms, real sandbox/refund/account-switch acceptance, rotation/rollback/retention review and the broader phone/release plan remain open. The reserved Pixel and other task's emulator/helper were untouched; the complete goal stays active.

- Final evidence is retained under ignored `captures/purchase-notifications-20261028/`: passing type/test/dry-run logs, exact dependency/reference versions and hashes of service sources, migrations, tests, configuration and documentation. Every local D1 proxy was disposed; no device, production service or recurring cloud job was started.


## October 28: resumable Voided Purchases pull recovery

- Added the local Publisher voided-purchase gateway and an hourly maintenance entry point, separately disabled by default. Provider requests use the fixed API host, OAuth, bounded JSON, redirect rejection and deadlines. The public Google discovery schema confirms flat `maxResults`/`token` parameters. Requests select one-time in-app purchases with quantity-based partial refunds excluded, matching the approved single-quantity lifetime policy. No real Google purchase API request, service deployment or recurring cloud job was started.
- Migration 0003 adds one scoped active window, a reclaimable/renewed three-minute owner lease, completion/error metadata, restart count and latched coverage-gap flag. At most four fifty-record pages execute per invocation. Terminal hash-only revocations apply in twenty-five-record transactional chunks before the corresponding next-page/completion checkpoint. Failures can replay an already applied prefix safely; a stale worker may add valid terminal fences but cannot move/release a newer owner's checkpoint. Completion does not create or restore paid access.
- The provider continuation cursor is AES-GCM encrypted under a separate server-only key, with fresh nonces and authenticated package/window context. UTF-8 plaintext and stored ciphertext have explicit byte/length bounds. State constraints reject detached cursor/window metadata. Reconstruction resumes without an in-memory full history. Wrong keys, corruption or moved ciphertext preserve progress and fail closed; rotation/drain/recovery requirements are documented.
- Bootstrap requests the provider's full default available window with a one-minute end delay; completed windows overlap by one day and immediate duplicates throttle. Old actual void timestamps are not locally filtered because Google applies its query filter to discovery time. A still-valid old provider snapshot is preserved even after newer coverage may have aged out. Authoritative cursor rejection allows one bounded default-window restart per invocation and latches uncertainty. Interrupted first-page bounds are recalculated without falsely preserving a lost bootstrap interval; existing proven overlap is retained when possible. Busy/disabled executions report unassessed coverage as null. Gaps remain visible rather than being declared repaired by a successful available-window scan.
- **Thirty local Worker tests pass**, including the existing sixteen notification/verification cases and fourteen scan/transport/recovery cases. Evidence covers 210 records across reconstructed runs, known/unseen revocations, privacy, malformed pages and loops, partial chunk failure, failure after revocation before checkpoint, concurrent/expired owners, wrong/moved keys, provider expiry, old valid snapshots, overlap/outages, failed initial pages, UTF-8 cursor bounds, schema constraints and the real scheduled handler through mocked Google transport. Final generated bindings, TypeScript check and local Wrangler dry-run pass. CI includes the tests automatically; remote CI remains unrun.
- The service README now includes all three migrations, encrypted-cursor key setup, hourly cadence, privacy-safe health SQL, fixed failure/gap events, supported key/rollback recovery and remaining acceptance. Real financial permissions, Play refunds/chargebacks, cloud scheduling/RTDN delivery, quota/backlog budgets, alert provisioning, operator recovery, cache terms and the broader phone/release plan remain open. No Android binary or device changed; the reserved Pixel and other task's emulator/helper were untouched. The complete goal stays active.

## October 28: local review milestones and gated Play review integration

Corrupt/oversized/future state fails closed; journal clear removes eligibility but preserves an already consumed attempt.
- Added a lifecycle/focus-aware Play Review 2.0.2 bridge. Preparation has a ten-second deadline; cancellation, clear generations and concurrent callers fence stale/duplicate launches. The attempt is durably reserved before SDK launch, so a process death or focus loss in that interval may consume it without showing a card. Google controls card visibility and neither API completion nor this state proves a rating was submitted. The default-off build flag and real Google Play installer check prevent automatic production calls from ordinary sideloaded QA builds.
- Verified manual SAF/default-folder completion hooks run only after read-back verification. Preview, staging, failed writes, scheduled output and share-chooser launch do not count. Android generic sharing cannot reliably establish recipient completion, unlike the iOS completed-share callback; this platform limitation remains documented. An observer failure cannot delete or misreport a successfully delivered file. Support's manual rating action opens the production listing directly with a web fallback and remains disabled with explanatory text in unconfigured builds.

## October 28: shared surface-distance metric and nonzero statistics references

- Replaced the fixed-radius domain sphere with one deterministic double-precision surface metric, using WGS84 local curvatures in the measured Core Location local branch and a bounded twenty-iteration inverse series for long arcs. Routed replay, saved-place repository matching and photo-map clustering through it, removing three independent Android float adapters. History/session/notification aggregation, quality/dwell and domain photo paths already use Coordinate.distanceTo. No raw point, timestamp, persisted outlier flag or schema was migrated. The existing conservative 6,300 km candidate-envelope radius remains safe for local saved-place matching and was not narrowed.
- Added an eighteenth generator that compiles the actual LocationPoint distance method and RecordingSession route sum, invoking Core Location on **macOS 27.2, build 26B123**. It produces **176 references**, with fresh-process pair isolation and preserved call order inside eight 100-leg routes. **169 conditioned comparisons** cover meridians/parallels, local/world coordinates, dateline, branch boundaries and route totals. Pair tolerance is 0.01 m plus 1e-9 of distance; route tolerance is 0.25 m plus that allowance. Seven exact/near-antipodal references retain their measured values but are only tested for finite bounds, with equivalence explicitly unresolved. These host references are not physical iOS runtime acceptance.
- The initial domain run exposed a precision bug in modulo-based longitude wrapping: adding a large constant erased tiny longitude differences and incorrectly merged a zero-radius photo group. Normalizing only when crossing ±180° fixes it, and a nextDown-coordinate regression preserves that distinction. Ordered Core Location totals also differed slightly from isolated-pair calculations; measured call-order behavior informed the separate aggregate tolerance. Curvature caching is a plausible explanation, not a documented API claim; Android does not copy undocumented mutable cache state. An opposite-pole reference was correctly classified with the other singular cases rather than reported as conditioned parity.
- Expanded actual Swift outing summary fixtures from six to ten with nonzero ordinary, outlier-bridge, all-outlier and dateline routes. Distance/speed JSON comparisons use half the CSV decimal step (0.005 m / 0.0005 m/s); all remaining fields stay strict, and every single/pair CSV remains byte-exact. The original provisional micron comparison was too strong for observed Core Location call-context variation. A native saved-place test intentionally asserted the former sphere/adapter disagreement; it now verifies their shared decision and the existing eighteen Swift duplicate plans still pass. The equatorial 0.00135° case is below 150 m in both paths, instead of being excluded by the old sphere.
- Added a native Room/reference route test: eight real persisted routes produce full-history totals matching their Core Location references, a two-point map sample retains complete distance/counts, replay agrees with the full summary, and all original records remain unchanged. **1,312 scoped automated tests pass:** 253 domain JVM cases, 934 export JVM cases, 108 isolated data cases, fourteen isolated outing/localized-file cases, two actual mapQa MapLibre photo-rendering cases and one separate million-point native scale case. All eighteen source/native-asset checks pass. The complete public Swift importer still accepts 150 Android history compatibility files.
- The million-point synthetic disk history test retains count, first/last endpoints and the 2,500-point display bound with the new metric; its measured window read is **1,827 ms**. No physical latency/CPU/battery budget is accepted from this result. Final mapQa assembly/lint pass. CI includes the new provenance check; the native data test is included by the existing full data suite. Remote CI remains unrun.
- The final disposable APK passes the unchanged fresh onboarding replay: eighteen actions/checks plus echo in 12.6s, no warnings/failures/errors/skips, with no runtime permission request or recording. Native tests supply the nonempty route/statistics and rendered-map evidence. ## October 28: complete disk-backed photo-place directory

- Added a complete validated, alias-deduplicated metadata-page reader to PhotoRepository. ## October 28: photo-directory ownership and clear-data integration

- AppGraph now owns a PhotoPlaceRepository for its private cache root. First preparation reaps abandoned prior-process directories; later preparations preserve live peers. Registrations are removed on close. Directory counts, marker/place/photo queries and cleanup run on IO; an active state fences retained Ready references and closed reads fail explicitly.
- Preparation has a dedicated child operation and a generation check. Clear-data sets its exclusion gate, cancels and joins old preparation jobs, closes every published directory, removes cached metadata and holds the gate through the journal-deletion callback. A failed deletion releases the gate for recovery without reviving old snapshots. Ownership outside the return context closes a completed directory if cancellation rejects delivery to the caller.
- The repository's observation API takes an explicit caller eligibility flow, replaces directories after either photo table changes, and closes the prior directory before emitting Unavailable when eligibility is removed. Collector cancellation closes its live directory. Settings clear-all now nests this owner inside the existing photo-library gate and outside replay/history deletion. Current map screens have not activated the directory yet, so actual foreground/access eligibility and map/directory/viewer presentation remain the next integration work.
- Six isolated native ownership tests cover abandoned-cache reaping, preservation of another live directory, idempotent close/read rejection, pending-preparation cancellation, exclusion throughout deletion, metadata/access replacement, observation cleanup, clear/rebuild behavior, deletion failure recovery and a deterministic queued-dispatcher cancellation at the completed result handoff. No synthetic photo file, permission prompt or owner history is used in that UI test.

## October 29: preserve the next stop during departure hysteresis

- Fixed a detector timing gap: after leaving an open visit, the first two minutes at a new stop used to be discarded when departure was confirmed. The next stop therefore needed roughly seven minutes of samples to meet the five-minute dwell threshold, and its arrival was backdated only to the departure-confirmation sample. A persisted, separate next-place anchor/time now retains spatially consistent evidence while departure is pending. Continued movement resets that candidate; returning to the old place discards it. Poor, outlier and out-of-order samples remain ignored, and a sampling gap still discards unsupported presence.
- A delayed batch may now confirm the old departure and new arrival in one transition. The existing repository commits both visits, saved-place stamping, route points, recording cursor and detector ownership in its single transaction. Native failure injection proves that a failed new-arrival save rolls all of these back; retry succeeds without duplicate records. Database reopen checks cover both pending departure and the promoted next-stop candidate.
- Room schema **10** adds only nullable next-anchor/next-candidate-time fields to the persisted detector state. Migration preserves the old anchor/times/owner/departure evidence and all journal rows. Older states have no new-place coordinates to recover, so their new candidate starts with the first supporting sample after upgrade. No historical arrivals are rewritten. Tests now migrate each schema origin 1 through 9 to the current schema while preserving manual correction/country metadata, automatic detector ownership, points, photo references and existing webhook data.
- The new focused regression suite was first run against the old implementation: **four of six cases failed**. After the fix, **262 domain JVM tests and 127 isolated native data tests pass** (389 unique cases); the native suite excludes the separately scoped million-point HistoryScalingTest. The six detector cases cover adjacent stops, one-batch departure/arrival, continued movement, brief return, rejected samples and long gaps. Three native handoff cases and the schema-nine migration origin are included in those totals. Build/QA APK assembly and lint pass; existing CI runs the full domain and non-scaling data suites, including these cases. Evidence, red/green XML, native/build logs and source/schema/APK hashes are under ignored `captures/visit-handoff-20261029/`.

## October 29: provider-neutral nearby-business search rules

- Inspected the actual iOS NearbyPlaceSearchService and current Android SystemPlaceLookup. Android's existing provider resolves addresses and cannot supply a business catalog. Provider selection remains pending an owner reply; no account/key, paid service, production request or provisioning was created. Geoapify is a candidate because its [Places documentation](https://apidocs.geoapify.com/docs/places/) supports category/radius POI queries and stored results. Google's [Places SDK policies](https://developers.google.com/maps/documentation/places/android-sdk/policies) require separate consideration of content storage and map/attribution terms; the public Overpass [usage guidance](https://dev.overpass-api.de/overpass-doc/en/preface/commons.html) does not support using its shared public instance as a general consumer-app backend. This is research for the pending choice, not provider/legal/release acceptance.
- Added provider-neutral business, all-place and text-query requests to the shared lookup service. Nearby normalization matches the inspected iOS algorithm: trimmed nonempty business names, reserved-placeholder rejection, first eligible case/canonically-equivalent name winning before sorting, the 20% radius allowance, measured-distance ordering and localized case-insensitive name ties. Invalid coordinates and nonfinite/negative distances are rejected. Request radius caps at the actual reference SDK's 2 km maximum; default radius/limit remain 1 km/eight results. The full bounded page is cached independently of the caller's display limit.
- Ported fallback order: business-filtered search, then all POIs if no normalized results, then restaurant/coffee/bar/hotel/shop queries if still empty. Failures propagate rather than pretending empty/unconfigured results are valid businesses. The current native address provider explicitly reports nearby search unconfigured. The new entry shares the existing default-off network policy, cancellable request ownership, global request serialization/throttle, timeout, language/coordinate/radius-aware bounded cache and Settings clear-cache path. Forward/reverse/nearby cache keys are separate. Cancellation is checked before and after each provider call, including between fallback stages, and late canceled results are never cached.
- Added a generator that extracts and executes the actual Swift suggestion model, makeSuggestions/suggestionID bodies and address formatter with real synthetic MapKit items and Core Location distances. **Thirteen reference cases** cover empty/placeholder data, trim/address handling, name deduplication order, distance sorting, exact-distance name ties, radius slack, canonical Unicode, negative rounding, dateline/polar coordinates and a small radius. CI now checks their source provenance. Core Location reports an approximately two-nanometre asymmetry for the symmetric-coordinate ID fixture; Android's shared metric reports a tie and orders by name. Tests explicitly retain this difference: algorithm order is compared using the same reference measurements, production membership is exact, production distances are within 2 mm in these cases, and production ordering matches the other twelve cases. This does not claim bit-identical distance or locale behavior everywhere.
- **273 domain tests pass**, including eleven new nearby tests exercising the thirteen reference cases, invalid records/arguments, radius clamp, cross-operation cache isolation, display-limit independence, locale/coordinate isolation, fallback stopping/order, fresh/cached opt-out, cancellation during fallback, clear, timeout/failure/retry, concurrent callers and an unconfigured provider. Current QA APK assembly and lint pass. Evidence, Swift/build logs, XML and source/fixture/APK hashes are under ignored `captures/nearby-places-20261029/`. No new native/device/UI test or live provider verdict is claimed.
- This completes shared search rules only. A selected/configured provider, its category mapping and attribution/privacy acceptance, nearby/current-business UI, correction distance/provenance integration and real provider/device acceptance remain required. The reserved Pixel, owner package and other task's emulator/helper were untouched; no device was used this turn. The earlier photo replay failure, passive capture, field/OEM/battery and all remaining phone/Wear/release gates stay open. The full goal remains active.

## October 29: date-line-safe route display and camera fitting

- Replaced ordinary min/max-longitude fit bounds with the smallest containing longitude arc. Both Fit route and Fit current recording use it, including exact full-route endpoints already supplied by their owners. Equivalent ±180° positions collapse to a single camera target. Camera latitude is clamped to the Web Mercator projection limit; original coordinates remain unchanged. MapLibre's unwrapped longitude bounds are supported by its [documented bounds constructor](https://maplibre.org/maplibre-native/android/api/-map-libre%20-native%20-android/org.maplibre.android.geometry/-lat-lng-bounds/-companion/from.html).
- Native screenshot inspection exposed a second bug after fitting was corrected: raw crossing LineStrings went around the long side of the globe. Added shared display-only antimeridian cuts to ordinary/replay and active-session line geometry. Each existing time-separated segment is cut independently, with a projected intersection at ±180°; no temporal gaps are joined, and no synthetic fixes enter history, statistics or exports. This follows the [GeoJSON antimeridian cutting recommendation](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7946#section-3.1.9) for presentation geometry. Numerical endpoint intersections preserve the exact existing projected endpoint instead of introducing duplicate near-zero vertices.
- **289 domain tests and 13 native map cases pass**, with final QA assembly and lint. Twelve new domain cases cover empty/ordinary/singular/equivalent-boundary input, tie behavior, worldwide and 2,500-point extent membership, polar clamps, crossings in both directions, exact-boundary continuity and finite projected intersections. Two additional native cases verify the 2,500-point date-line camera, visible endpoints and rendered route midpoint, plus active-session date-line/single-position/polar camera behavior. Existing photo-map and follow cases remain green. Data schema and persistence code are unchanged; the prior 134-case data result is retained separately rather than rerun or recounted.
- The first polar assertions incorrectly required an exact camera center at the projection limit and then ignored SDK viewport rounding. Diagnostics measured the SDK's constrained center and a 2.125 px south-edge rounding difference at 2.625 px/dp; final checks require a finite nearby camera and projected visibility within one dp. The earlier native failures and stale-frame polar diagnostic are retained as diagnostics, not visual acceptance. Final before/after date-line native screenshots show the route changing from incorrect outside-edge bands to the expected crossing between its endpoint flags. These are synthetic component harness captures, with test Activity chrome overlap; they do not certify full product typography, accessibility, physical rendering or release performance. Final XML, logs, hashes and screenshots are under ignored `captures/active-recording-map-20261029/`, with final reports in `camera-final/`.
- This supersedes the previous entry's open date-line/polar camera checks only for these bounded emulator cases. World-scale native fitting, real large-history performance, provider/physical/lifecycle matrices, localization/accessibility, remaining photo-flow repair and all broader phone/Wear/release gates remain open. The reserved Pixel, owner package and other task's emulator/helper were untouched. The full goal remains active.

## October 29: visit-editor saved-state and background process-death recovery

- Fixed parent navigation state that dismissed an unsaved visit editor during Activity recreation even though its text fields used rememberSaveable. ## October 29: Geoapify nearby adapter and opt-out-safe HTTP boundary

- Implemented GeoapifyNearbyPlaces and a PlaceLookupProvider wrapper. AppGraph now selects the wrapper only when the explicit build flag and key are configured; defaults preserve the system provider and unavailable nearby behavior. System address search/reverse delegates are unchanged. Keys are omitted from disabled APKs, and enabled configuration validates bounded/control-free input. No account, key, paid plan or production endpoint was provisioned. Current-place UI binding, disclosure/attribution and live configured acceptance remain active work.
- The nearby client uses the pinned documented Places endpoint, circle/proximity/50-result/language parameters, mapped business/broader POI families and category-aware restaurant/coffee/bar/hotel/shop fallbacks. Arbitrary nearby name filters are URL-encoded. Returned named features require numeric finite valid coordinates; source addresses/names are bounded. The existing service retains its two-kilometre cap, first-eligible-name deduplication, distance/collation order, shared-limit memory cache and opt-out semantics. No history writes or route uploads were added.
- Added bounded cancellable HTTP with ten-second timeouts, one-MiB declared/chunked response limits, strict UTF-8/JSON, duplicate-key/depth/collection bounds, no redirects and sanitized exception chains. A targeted test exposed OkHttp 4.12's automatic follow-up for 503 Retry-After: 0 despite connection retries being disabled. A network interceptor removes that 503 follow-up instruction while preserving the error status; the caller owns any retry. The failing probe is preserved and the final no-follow-up test passes.
- **All 13 new JVM transport/service tests pass**, covering exact request context, language/name encoding, fallback categories, system delegation/default-off configuration, invalid records/input, error redaction and status/redirect/request counts, malformed/duplicate/excess-feature/chunked/invalid-UTF8 responses, cancellation, disabled/in-flight opt-out, shared cache limits and the seven-step empty-result fallback chain. All **11 existing nearby-domain cases** also pass; unchanged up-to-date results are not extra executions. An initial assertion expecting no exception cause was corrected to require every recovered cause-chain message to remain sanitized, because coroutine stack-trace recovery wraps exceptions. No network/privacy assertion was relaxed.
- QA assembly/lint pass. The existing CI platform unit-test task discovers the new test class automatically; remote CI is unrun. Logs, regression probe, final XML and source/APK hashes are under ignored captures/geoapify-adapter-20261029/. Only localhost synthetic servers were used. ## October 29: current-location business suggestion flow and saved-place race

- Connected the approved provider to Find current location. Opening the dialog requests neither GPS nor a place lookup. Its configured-provider disclosure explains coordinate/radius/language/category sharing before Request location. A usable requested fix prefers the nearest local saved place within that place's radius; otherwise it requests **one business within 500 m**, matching the inspected iOS current-place lookup. Unknown/unloaded saved-place state blocks the network decision. Default keyless/unconfigured builds retain the GPS/local-saved-place path without a business request.
- Business selection uses the same draft command as the nearby-list workflow: explicit selection replaces draft name/address/coordinates and retains provider distance; only Save writes a visit. The raw GPS action preserves typed metadata. Network opt-out, invalid/expired/replaced fixes and backgrounding cancel or invalidate business work and selections. The location dialog clears its unselected fix on backgrounding and asks for a new request on return. GPS requests use generation guards and an explicit cancellation check, preventing a late canceled source from replacing a newer pending request or resetting its loading state.
- A new native race test exposed a real stale-presentation problem: a saved place added before recomposition could be ignored by a queued business callback. Rechecking rememberUpdatedState alone did not close that gap. Added an authoritative local saved-place reader before lookup, before presenting a response and before business selection; selection also rechecks network preference, fix, request generation and lifecycle. The original same-frame race assertion now passes with the production fix. Existing local-saved-place actions also track the latest published fix/library snapshot, with a new held-action removal regression.
- Localized the entire current-location dialog and new business states with **25 reviewed resources**, bringing the Spanish pack to **458 externalized resources**. Coordinates/accuracy use the UI locale; business distance follows app units. Added disclosure, local-library loading, provider loading/empty/error/retry, interruption and attribution states. Large-text inspection prompted shorter footer labels, Use coordinates and Cancel, making the raw-fix choice distinct from Use this business.
- Initial native run: **22 of 23 passed**, with the stale saved-place callback failure retained. After the production reader fix and additional saved-place/poor-GPS assertions, **all 24 final native cases pass at actual system font scale 1.8**: ten current-business cases, four location/editor/repository flows, six saved-place cases and four existing nearby-selection/restoration cases. The unchanged race assertion covers the previously failing same-frame update. The whole flow verifies explicit request, exact origin/500 m context, no write before selection/Save, stored coordinates/provider distance/current-visit state, local saved-place priority with zero business calls, localized source errors/poor accuracy and late noncooperative GPS cancellation. Component cases cover unknown library state, invalid fixes, opt-out, saved-place arrival during lookup, expiry, replacement, background/retry and redacted empty/error states.

## October 29: review audit lifecycle and atomic confirmed-place memory

- Compared the current iOS correction, undo and rememberConfirmedPlace paths. Three new regressions failed against Android's prior behavior: repeated corrections replaced the first confirmation time, undo erased the original audit anchor, and the generic manual-correction command retained an old provider distance. Corrections now preserve an existing confirmedAt while advancing updatedAt and invalidate stale provider distance; a deliberately selected provider can then supply its new distance. Undo restores the original place and clears active confirmation/provider provenance while retaining original and detected audit fields. Null original names/addresses stay null through later edits. A later confirmation after undo starts a new confirmation epoch.
- The editor now offers Undo only for corrected records with an original anchor, matching the inspected iOS UI. A retained audit anchor on an unconfirmed/confirmed record no longer causes an inappropriate Undo action. Notes/time-only edits retain their existing audit behavior. Existing coordinate-based country-cache invalidation policy remains unchanged.
- Added confirmed-place memory for explicit review of an existing visit: confirmation and actual place correction insert a 150 m saved place only when no matching named place exists. Matching uses the existing native case-folding/distance rules and the larger of managed/default radii. Existing managed coordinates, address, identity and radius remain untouched. Place creation shares the visit transaction; failures/cancellation roll back together and exact retries do not duplicate either record. Fresh library state is read inside the transaction. New manual visits still use the separate explicit reusable-place choice; notes-only saves and undo do not create or delete library entries.
- The editor sends an explicit remember intent through map, timeline and linked-outing save adapters. Its visible explanation describes this side effect. One reviewed English/Spanish resource brings the Spanish pack to **459 externalized resources**. No stored model fields, Room schema or migration changed.
- All **304 domain tests** pass, including the three previously failing audit cases and four confirmed-memory policy cases. All **18 scoped Room instrumentation cases** pass: six new transaction cases plus existing editing/reusable-place checks. Coverage includes a real SQLite place-insert failure rolling back the existing visit update, stale edits creating no place, exact retries, preservation of a freshly managed pin/radius, independent notes/undo behavior and rejection of invalid remember intents.
- All **22 scoped editor/restoration native cases** pass, including three new full review lifecycles, existing provider selections, localization, dirty-cancel guards and saved-state restoration. The **seven targeted review/provider cases also pass at actual system font scale 1.8**; these repeats are not extra unique coverage. Tests verify repeated corrections and restored drafts retain the first confirmation and original fields, managed memory survives a later address correction, undo confirmation is required, the retained original survives reconfirmation and later correction, inappropriate Undo disappears, and notes-only saves create no place. Inspected the large Spanish post-undo editor: review explanation and Confirm visit wrap/read correctly; Cancel/Save remain reachable. The isolated QA host's launcher button is visible behind the dialog; this is component/flow evidence, not full-shell/system-inset acceptance.
- Lint, five localization converter cases, resource verification and workflow YAML parsing pass. All **935 regular export cases** pass after the model change; all **twelve generated Android backups** again pass the actual SwiftData commit/deduplication acceptance check. CI explicitly includes the new editor class; its existing unfiltered data suite discovers the new transaction class. ## October 29: pin selection and reverse-address lifecycle

- Reworked reverse-address requests around an explicit cancellable job/generation. Before request and result application, the picker reads the current network preference and requires the same pin/generation and active started lifecycle. Pin movement, explicit cancellation, opening search, backgrounding, dismissal and disposal invalidate/cancel pending work. A late non-cooperative response cannot attach an old address to the new pin. User filenames and CSV/JSON/protocol data remain unchanged. Broader Export choices/filters, purchase messages and other workflows still contain English; this is not complete phone localization or accessibility acceptance.
- Picker and settings controllers expose stable notice/failure identities, retaining computed English diagnostic accessors for non-UI callers. Delivery catches no longer display arbitrary exception/provider text. Existing typed purchase denial remains distinct from preparation or recovery failure. Changed-destination, lost-destination and changed-staging failures have typed identities; failed picker receipts persist their optional notice alongside the existing version-1 fields. Older receipts without that field and unknown future identities fall back safely. Success/cancellation identities cannot make a failed persisted receipt display success. No database/history format or paid-capability policy changed.
- Specialized preparation is guarded before coroutine launch against duplicate/held actions and stops on background or disposal. The application-owned picker controller remains responsible for a received result; an already prepared file can complete after its UI is disposed. Controller operations expose joinable jobs and bounded shutdown for deterministic QA cleanup. Resume still preserves changed destination bytes, rechecks purchase access and commits completion only after verified delivery. Cancel still prevents late callbacks from writing. No product entitlement bypass is added: production uses graph.exports and its existing purchase gate; only androidTest fixtures inject authorization.
- All **32 scoped app cases pass together at actual system font scale 1.8**, covering eleven new delivery cases, thirteen Country Days cases and eight outing cases. New tests exercise the real file engine/controller with synthetic private history and app-private FileProvider destinations: disclosure without preparation, held double action/cancel, real purchase denial without staging, sanitized exception/URI failures, pending cancellation and late callback rejection, verified bytes and duplicate callbacks, completion after UI disposal, background/disposal cancellation, locale plurals and destination-button width. The captured Spanish sheet was inspected: title/disclosure/buttons wrap without clipping; lower controls remain reachable by scrolling. This is component evidence, not a physical purchased system-picker/share or TalkBack/RTL/dark-mode verdict.
- All **23 focused native platform cases pass**, including recovery, ordinary/country file delivery and export settings. The expanded recovery test verifies typed changed-destination state after controller recreation. A new compatibility case reads actual legacy/unknown/forged-success failure receipts and proves generic failure presentation without leaking their raw error. Existing document-provider cases continue to prove partial-prefix recovery, unchanged verified bytes, changed-destination preservation, purchase rechecks, canceled late-result rejection and manifest containment.
- The initial app run rejected the new class because its setup method returned a state object; it now explicitly returns Unit. That run also had one country locale-restoration timeout and one country cancellation-wait timeout. Both country cases pass unchanged in the targeted rerun and the final 32-case large-text run; their transient causes were not established. The original failures are retained. The corrected normal-text run passes all eleven delivery cases plus those two unchanged cases. No existing assertion or locale/cancellation deadline was relaxed.

## October 29: Settings overview, command ownership and guarded journal clearing

Confirmations are tied to both their own token and the reviewed clear attempt. Cancel/background/disposal, reused callbacks and a newer clear attempt cannot submit an old approval. Returning from Support and recreating a child preserve the overview position. Existing support checks now also require the Support button to remain displayed after Back. The existing automation-clear integration now waits for the new attempt’s terminal clear state, rather than treating prompt disappearance as completion; all its revocation/cache/log/recording assertions are retained.

## October 29: automation consent localization, restored choices and publication fences

- Added 51 English/Spanish resources for all seven scope descriptions, install/reload controls, access status, review/revoke/discard confirmations, protocol guidance and feedback. Externalized coverage is now **1,049 resources**. Scope enums, package/action literals, request/result schemas, authorization checks, result expiry and signed purchase gates remain unchanged. Controller feedback uses typed identities while retaining legacy English diagnostic accessors.
- Saved choices are display-only and bound to package/UID/installation time/signing hashes plus saved approval and reset revisions. Restoration preserves choices but never restores a consent ticket or grants access. Changed installation or actual saved approval resets the draft. Immutable single-use confirmation tickets reject changed choices, stale consent epochs, canceled/replaced dialogs, background and disposal. Review/revoke bodies scroll independently of their actions. Access failures display unknown state rather than presenting cached approval as verified; retry requires explicit review.
- Accepted application-owned consent writes immediately publish busy state and advance the consent revision. Older reloads cannot publish over that fence. Clear/revoke cancels and joins earlier actions, advances a separate reset revision even without an existing approval, and retains the existing authorization/result cleanup. Deterministic controller coverage holds an older resolver read until a newer consent is accepted, verifies no revision rollback, checks changed-installation denial and confirms explicit reset/reload remains available. Installed identity is still revalidated by AutomationAccess before authorization is written.

## October 30: durable journal-clear recovery and admission fencing

- Added a private no-backup consent receipt around the existing protected clear pipeline. A canonical request UUID, phase and corruption checksum are read with a 256-byte limit; atomic writes explicitly sync and verify readback. Completion leaves a tombstone so a later launch cannot repeat deletion against new history. Unknown versions, malformed data, unexpected directories and uncommitted newer requests fail closed. A staged file alongside an older completion remains a persistent fence across repeated reads. The checksum is corruption detection, not remote authentication. Existing pipeline ordering and import exclusion remain intact.
- Pending receipts resume the already-approved pipeline on application graph initialization. Failed recovery keeps a global journal barrier; known pending work permits explicit retry, while an unreadable receipt requires a fresh disclosure and approval. There is no success until cleanup and completion persistence finish. MainActivity foreground/reconciliation/review hooks, recording starts, automation entry/result reads and real default analytics/export/webhook workers observe admission. Stop remains available to avoid deadlocking cleanup. Permission callbacks and private service start intents carry the journal generation and reject stale starts after deletion.
- Normal journal UI is absent while blocked. Saved UI state is scoped to the durable journal generation, previous drafts are discarded after clear, map/timeline selection resets and unobserved journal windows expire immediately. Returning to a tab reloads its window; ordinary same-generation recreation preserves current drafts. A deliberately unchanged-key saved-state implementation failed the new private-draft restoration regression before the generation fix. The initial AtomicFile test also caught replacement of an unexpected directory, corrected with explicit file-type guards. No Room schema or public automation protocol changed.
- Seven new recovery JVM cases cover startup, failures, canceled ownership, retries, persistence ordering and generation retention. All **91 platform JVM cases** and app/platform lint pass. Ten native receipt cases cover actual AtomicFile persistence, staged writes, corruption/bounds/file types, tombstones and request identity. Real default worker admission and existing recording command/notification regressions bring the scoped platform aggregate to **25 passing native cases**. A separate cold-graph case proves actual Room/approval/runtime cleanup and preservation of a later point.
- `scripts/verify_clear_recovery.py` verifies the explicitly selected emulator AVD and refuses a preinstalled disposable test package. It runs a real accepted clear held at the automation-revocation join, force-stops only that test package, then verifies recovery and a completed-receipt launch in three distinct OS processes. The final proof passed with PIDs **8064, 8131 and 8185**, including preservation of post-completion history. CI includes this isolated proof and archives its report; workflow YAML parses, but remote CI is unrun. This proves one synthetic checkpoint on API 35, not every pipeline checkpoint, abrupt power loss, reboot, real GPS or OEM/physical acceptance.

## October 30: signed bundle workflow and release artifact verification

- Added a manually dispatched, default-branch-only Android signed-bundle workflow with environment-scoped signing inputs, release lint/domain/export/billing/platform checks, version inputs and verified-artifact upload. It performs no Play upload, device install or rollout. GitHub environment `android-release` still needs owner-configured required reviewers, branch restrictions and production secrets; naming it in YAML does not create those protections. No remote workflow, environment provisioning, account/product setup or production key access occurred.
- Gradle accepts explicit version-code/name properties while preserving existing development defaults. The actual release bundle built at synthetic version **0.1.0-qa (7)**, release lint passed with **zero errors and 107 warnings**, and all **91 release-platform JVM cases passed**. A separate real Gradle invocation rejects version code 0 before packaging. Existing warning cleanup, current SDK/library policy review and runtime release acceptance remain separate work.
- Signing uses jarsigner environment password references; bundletool's APK-generation interface instead requires private single-line password files. Those files have mode 0600 inside a private temporary directory and are removed after generation or failure; no password value appears in command arguments. A Java JAR verifier reads every payload entry and requires one unexpired signer matching the independently configured upload-certificate SHA-256. It rejects debug certificates even if their fingerprint was supplied, unsigned archives, mismatched certificates, tampered payloads, newly appended unsigned payloads including META-INF files and duplicate entries. Signature machinery is excluded from payload checks. All **13 artifact-guard tests pass**, including compiled numeric/symbolic location flags and private/exclusive password-file handling. Both Android workflows pass actionlint.
- The full pipeline succeeded against the real AAB using a fresh two-day disposable QA upload key: **1,625 payload entries verified**, Google bundletool validation passed, package/version/API/backup/debug-component and recording-service declaration checks passed, and all **eight 64-bit native libraries passed ELF/ZIP 16 KB alignment** in a generated universal APK. An actual wrong-certificate attempt promoted no artifact; the CI clean-source guard also rejected the intentionally dirty local checkout. The accepted local report records that dirty state explicitly. It is QA build evidence, not a production-signed or field-accepted release candidate.
- Artifacts include the signed bundle, decoded base manifest, native-alignment report, source/version/certificate/tool provenance and SHA256SUMS; every emitted checksum was read back and verified. The current release build remains unminified, so no R8 mapping is invented. The verifier preserves a mapping from the signed bundle's metadata when present. Google bundletool 1.18.3 is pinned to the SHA-256 published with its upstream release. Initial local failures exposed Python 3.9 checksum API compatibility, numeric compiled manifest flags and bundletool's password interface; those were corrected, with failure evidence retained. A Python cache-write sandbox restriction was handled by directing compilation cache to task-owned temporary storage.
- Accepted artifacts, native/unit/lint logs/XML, intermediate failures, final source snapshots and hashes are under ignored captures/release-build-20261030/. Temporary QA private keys are gone, no production key was created or retained, and no device was accessed. The Pixel, GitSync emulator and all owner journals remain untouched. The workflow currently uses default integration configuration; provider/purchase/analytics production setup and acceptance, protected-environment/remote-CI validation, complete accessibility/notice/field/performance/OEM gates, Play submission and deferred Wear OS remain open. The full implementation goal stays active.

## October 30: road-display domain port and actual Swift references

- Audited the remaining Map/replay parity gap and ported the actual iOS road-gap rules into the Android-independent domain. The builder selects up to 40 eligible gaps by descending distance, processes selected gaps chronologically and tries two speed-ordered transport modes, allowing up to 80 provider attempts. It preserves the 120–25,000 m, positive/two-hour time, 55 m/s and distance-scaled accuracy gates; rejects implausible reported route distances; stitches endpoints and removes near duplicates; coalesces raw failures; and clips raw segments while withholding unfinished snapped segments during replay. Provider failure falls back, while coroutine cancellation propagates and cannot become a second request or a published fallback.
- Added deterministic source identity, lazy disabled-source evaluation, a frozen source snapshot and copied returned geometry. Accuracy participates in the token because the eligibility predicate consumes it. Inputs above the existing 2,500-point map boundary are rejected before fingerprinting/provider calls. No repository mutation, request transport, permission, configuration or production/UI call site was introduced.
- Generated **17 actual Swift reference scenarios**, running the current MapView helper method bodies with a synthetic replacement of only the directions request, including actual plausibility/transport/stitching helpers. Selection, raw coalescing, coordinate output and every replay index compare successfully. The 40-largest missing-response scenario proves 80 attempts rather than incorrectly claiming a 40-request network cap. The fixture also records actual MapKit candidate distances; Android's existing surface metric matches the decisions/geometry on all reference inputs, while numeric equality is not claimed. Additional injected-metric tests exercise inclusive/exclusive thresholds. Real geographic threshold edges, polar behavior, provider differences and live acceptance remain open.
- Final **320 domain JVM tests pass**, including **nine road-domain cases** covering both fixture metric modes, exact rule boundaries, implausible results, thrown provider errors, source/geometry mutation isolation, late non-cooperative cancellation, source identity/laziness and the 2,500-point boundary. Fixture provenance and actionlint pass; CI now checks the reference and includes MapView changes in its trigger paths. No remote CI or device tests ran. The first Swift harness exceeded the compiler's type-checking limit on a large tuple expression; splitting fixture construction fixed the harness without changing the actual helper logic. No real Apple or Geoapify directions request was made.
- Synthetic distance probes confirmed that MapKit's map-point distance is distinct from a simple Mercator scale multiplication and the shared Android surface metric, including high-latitude clamping. Those measurements are retained for later regional/threshold review instead of silently equating metrics. They do not change recorded coordinates, statistics, detector or export distances.
- The host filled before the initial new source file could be created. Removed only this task's earlier duplicate QA-signed AAB, reproducible unsigned AAB and generated release native intermediates; the final accepted signed bundle, reports and hashes are retained. The earlier duplicate artifact's historical checksum remains in its evidence manifest; that duplicate is intentionally no longer retained. Other tasks, shared caches, devices and journals were untouched.
- Evidence, Swift fixture/compiler logs, distance probes, domain XML/logs and source hashes are under ignored captures/road-display-20261030/. Routing-provider choice is pending separately from the approved Geoapify business-search integration. The routing adapter, explicit consent/opt-out and lifecycle/clear ownership, actual map/outing/replay integration, provider configuration and on-device/field/release acceptance remain unfinished. ## October 30: road request admission, bounded cache and maintenance ownership

- Added the provider-independent RoadDisplayService needed to connect road rules to visible maps. Configuration, explicit enablement, network policy, foreground visibility and journal readiness all default off and must be admitted together. Policy changes synchronously replace ownership, cancel only owned child workers and discard the memory cache. Results are bound to both their access token and source fingerprint; a retained result must pass isCurrent before rendering. This is an API contract and JVM evidence, not proof that Android lifecycle/settings or AppGraph currently calls it.
- Serializes requests and applies 250 ms attempt spacing across different route inputs, 10-second attempt timeouts and a 60-second total preparation timeout including queue wait. Only an attempt timeout can become alternate-transport fallback; total/caller cancellation propagates. Typed warnings cover unavailable/failed providers, attempt timeout, unusable geometry and geometry limits, without storing or presenting provider exception text. Cooperative cancellation is explicit: the future HTTP adapter must cancel its actual call and bound decoding. No real provider request, API key, account or network configuration was used.
- Caps accepted geometry at 20,000 coordinates per response and 50,000 cumulatively per preparation. Oversized/exhausted geometry stops further provider requests and leaves raw fallback for remaining gaps. The in-memory LRU is limited by both four entries and 50,000 coordinates, validates cache-hit age/clock rollback, and supports explicit refresh. Oversized results are returned without cache retention. No on-disk route cache was added; provider cache terms and real rendering/latency/memory budgets remain open.
- A new regression failed against the initial implementation: an access update during clear could reopen admission while a non-cooperative old provider was still being joined. A counted maintenance fence now rejects those updates and remains active across overlapping/canceled maintenance callers; clear requires fresh admission after completion. The unchanged regression passes.
- A second regression failed after that fix: canceling the original caller removed its worker from the registry before a non-cooperative provider actually completed, so a later clear returned early. Registration now lasts until worker completion rather than caller await cleanup. The unchanged regression passes. Clear also rejects maintenance invoked from inside provider work, preventing self-join deadlock even across dispatcher changes. Red sources/logs/XML are preserved separately from green evidence.
- Final **338 domain JVM tests pass with zero failures/errors/skips**, including **18 new road-service cases** covering all admission gates, idempotent access versus policy changes, source/cache identity, mutation isolation, TTL/rollback/refresh, coordinate/entry LRU limits, rapid opt-out/re-enable, queued journal generations, actual worker joining after caller cancellation, overlapping/canceled clear callers, self-join rejection, alternate/total deadlines, geometry limits, typed failures and serial spacing. The 17 actual Swift road-rule references still pass provenance checking. No remote CI or device/visual QA ran because this remains a domain-only service with no production caller.
- Evidence, both actual regressions, final source and native-independent JVM logs/XML/hashes are under ignored captures/road-service-20261030/. Routing-provider choice remains pending separately from Geoapify place search. Next integration work is the bounded HTTP adapter, explicit consent/localized UI, direct lifecycle/settings/journal-barrier wiring, source-bound MapLibre/outing/replay rendering and device acceptance. The Pixel, GitSync emulator, owned QA AVD and all journals were untouched in this turn. The full implementation goal stays active.

## October 30: application-owned road lifecycle, policy revisions and journal clear

- Added RoadDisplayController to the application graph with separate activity-owned sessions and a shared provider serialization/pacing gate. MainActivity resume admits its own session, pause/stop invalidates it and destroy closes it. Closing an older activity cannot disable a newer activity's session. Closed sessions stay registered until their actual workers finish, allowing application clear to join work even after its caller exits. Production construction supplies no routing provider; no HTTP adapter, key, account or coordinate sharing was enabled.
- Added persisted road-display consent, default off, and a storage-owned revision. Both road consent and network-geocoding changes synchronously invalidate the graph coordinator before their atomic DataStore write commits. Unrelated edits preserve the revision, and copied/forged revisions are ignored. Captured opaque service permits and activity epochs prevent held requests from obtaining new admission after a policy transition. Every actual provider dispatch and response rereads persisted policy, including changes a Flow observer has not yet seen. Policy-write invalidation is conservative on failure; a future consent UI must surface/recover failed writes rather than treating an unchanged snapshot as approval.
- Journal clearing now fences and joins all owned road workers before continuing journal erasure, then resets road consent off. Failed clear/reset remains unavailable across unrelated settings/journal emissions until successful maintenance. A real graph test held a synthetic non-cooperative provider worker, proved records and consent remained while clear waited, then verified cancellation, consent reset and erasure after release. ## October 30: explicit road consent, reliable revocation and DataStore race fix

- Added Settings → Road display with English/Spanish disclosure, a default-off consent switch, provider identity/privacy link, separate network-policy explanation, cancel/allow review, storage/stale feedback and an off/reset action. It explains selected segment endpoints/travel modes, estimated map-only geometry and that previously received coordinates cannot be recalled. Production graph construction still supplies no routing adapter/disclosure, so enabling is unavailable; an old enabled preference can still be revoked. The internal graph accepts reviewed disclosure together with a future adapter, and rejects disclosure without an adapter. Synthetic tests inject both; no live provider, account, key or coordinate request was enabled.
- RoadConsentSession belongs to one Settings screen. Reviews are single-use; lifecycle epoch, journal generation, current network policy, configured provider and preference revision are rechecked inside the atomic edit. Cancel, pause/close, clear/replacement generations and changed policy cannot approve stale reviews, including a non-cooperative queued updater. Enabling retries require a new review. Revocation synchronously invalidates routing before its DataStore write queues, then survives background/close so leaving Settings cannot undo an off request. Save failures retain conservative blocking and show persisted state with a localized recovery action. No raw exception text or URI is displayed as feedback.
- The actual graph/DataStore check found a real observer race: consent committed, but a sole routing observer stayed on the prior false/empty-revision value and remained blocked. It failed twice; a diagnostic second subscriber changed timing and made it pass. Removed that probe rather than relying on it. Upgraded DataStore **1.1.7 → 1.3.0**, whose upstream collector/write dropped-update fix matches this behavior ([official release notes](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/datastore#1.3.0-alpha03)). The original single-observer graph scenario then passed in **three fresh platform.test processes**. It deliberately holds another DataStore edit, proves route invalidation before the queued off write can finish, closes the consent session, releases the writer and verifies persisted revocation and independent network policy. Application revision/generation/dispatch guards remain intact.
- Generated a committed **369-byte** synthetic preference file using the actual old **1.1.7 PreferencesFileSerializer**, in an isolated Java process with pinned legacy artifact hashes. A fresh data.test process reads it under 1.3.0, preserves existing settings/privacy choices, performs an unrelated edit without losing its revision, then deliberately changes network policy and verifies the new revision without enabling road consent. Generator Java/Python and artifact/binary provenance are committed; CI checks provenance and runs this test alone, excluding it from the general data run so no already-initialized store is replaced. This does not replace Room migration or release acceptance.
- Final updated-runtime evidence: **109 platform debug JVM tests pass**, including **nine new consent cases**; **29 unique native cases pass** with zero failures/errors/skips. Native coverage comprises **21 consent/settings UI cases**, **one graph consent case** (three fresh-process passes count once), **four preference cases**, **one road-worker clear case**, **one cold pending-clear recovery case** and **one old-file compatibility case**. Six consent UI cases pass again at actual system font scale **1.8**. Repeats and earlier weaker/failed runs are not additional unique cases. Lint passes with zero errors/105 warnings, workflow actionlint and seven resource-converter tests pass, fixture provenance passes, and all **eight** final APK native libraries retain ELF/ZIP **16 KB** alignment. Resolved runtime dependencies are recorded in the normal-run log. CI includes consent UI once plus standalone fresh graph/upgrade cases; remote CI is unrun.

Initial UI navigation failure was a fixture trying to find an uncomposed lazy-list item; it now scrolls the list to that item. The screen now owns its Material surface and captures configuration-local dialog strings before dialog subcomposition. - Both final runtime exports pass, the regular debug build passes with all 86 tasks up to date, and workflow actionlint passes. Debug/release component/artifact inventories are identical. All preserved POM and notice hashes/lengths verify. Final selection: **162 external components**, **116 distinct artifacts**, **46 metadata-only components**, **97 embedded notice entries** and **23 distinct embedded byte payloads**. Source-cache absolute paths are confined to ignored local input manifests, not portable collected reports.

## October 31: Visits only persistence and balanced-power source foundation

- Recorded the owner's approval for default-off Visits only with balanced-power updates and an ongoing notification in the implementation plan. - Added transactional start/stop/interruption and generation-bound visit ingestion. Visit observations create no route points, exact sessions or webhook points. They share saved-place stamping, duplicate reconciliation and the persisted detector with recording. Starting/stopping/deleting/interruption of a route preserves detector ownership while monitoring is requested; monitoring Stop preserves an active route's detector. Its final Stop closes at the last supporting detector sample rather than later wall time/outlier time. Route batches write observation state once per transaction. Stale leases, replayed timestamps, pre-start/old/future/poor fixes and oversized batches cannot partially mutate history. Clear-table erasure invalidates existing leases; service cancellation/join still needs integration.

Recording retains its high-accuracy request configuration. Both policies retain provider-ready acknowledgement and late-registration/cancellation guards. ## October 31: interruption endpoints and explicit recording restart

- Reproduced four persistence regressions with a fixed clock: service failure used later wall time for route/visit ends; relaunch recovery treated a raw outlier timestamp as continued visit presence; interrupting Visits only before a new no-fix route changed the visit end; and a failed no-fix start invented route duration. The first three reproduced twice (18 cases/three failures), then the no-fix regression produced four failures among 19 cases. An actual service test separately proved explicit Start reused an abandoned requested/open session when runtime was absent.
- Shared transaction-local interruption endpoint selection now ends a route at its last durable fix, falling back to the session start if it has no fixes. Visits use the persisted detector's last supporting observation. Shared detector ownership remains until the final mode stops, so interruption order no longer extends presence. Explicit ordinary Stop keeps its requested wall-clock boundary. No detector thresholds or schema changed. The cause was endpoint selection, not altered event instants or acceptance of the tested outlier by the detector.
- RecordingService now reconciles abandoned persisted state before creating an explicitly restarted session. Its existing active-runtime branch still makes repeated Start idempotent. ## October 31: native renderer startup race and input readiness

## October 31: real process recovery and Android 15 force-stop classification

- A new host-driven real-service test exposed a user-visible bug: after force-stop, Visits only reported `visits.restarted` instead of an interruption. The receiver assumed every `BOOT_COMPLETED` meant a phone reboot. Android 15 also sends that broadcast when an app leaves the stopped state, as documented in [Android's stopped-state behavior](https://developer.android.com/about/versions/15/behavior-changes-all#stopped-state). The original failing native output is retained. The corrected force-stop proof records an unchanged boot count and the interrupted message.
- Schema **12** adds nullable private start boot counts to recording and Visits only state, with a preserving 11→12 migration. Each real service stamps the current boot count on explicit start; idempotent starts retain their owner's evidence. Missing/inaccessible/legacy evidence produces a generic interruption. Only changed known boot counts justify the existing phone-restarted wording. MainActivity and the boot receiver use shared recovery, protect acknowledged runtimes/pending starts, and bind database writes to the observed session or monitoring generation. A stale recovery decision cannot close its replacement. No location service is automatically started by recovery; history IDs, public import/export contracts and foreground permission requirements remain unchanged.
- Added `scripts/verify_location_process_recovery.py` and the isolated `LocationProcessRecoveryTest`. The harness verifies embedded APK/test-target identities, refuses physical devices and existing QA installations, requires fresh evidence output, checks the named AVD before termination, and touches only its own installed mapQa packages. A synced ready proof follows acknowledged real foreground-service startup. SIGKILL targets that exact QA PID through its own UID; force-stop targets mapQa; reboot targets only the selected emulator. Boot counts distinguish the termination modes, and success requires a new-process native recovery result and verified proof. The seed instrumentation's intentional process-crashed output is expected evidence, not itself a passing test.
- **All nine combinations of route-only/Visits-only/both and SIGKILL/force-stop/actual OS reboot have passing proofs against one identical main APK.** They assert inactive runtime before user restart, interrupted/reboot status, closed old sessions at durable evidence, preserved point counts and a known historical point/closed visit, fresh route identity/monitoring lease, and clean acknowledged Stop. Ready-phase reconciliation also proves active acknowledged modes survive a delayed recovery call. This is API 35 emulator evidence, not physical GPS/visit accuracy, low-memory killing, OEM behavior or a release power/performance budget.
- Reboot exposed a fixture assumption: instrumentation-granted location permission was absent in some restarted processes. The initial combined-reboot run passed interruption recovery but failed at explicit restart. The final fixture checks that denied restart leaves both modes off, then explicitly grants permission before restart, recording whether reapproval was necessary. An initial route-only reboot run timed out on an unlabeled 20-second condition. Two consecutive repeats with named diagnostic checkpoints passed without increasing timeouts or relaxing assertions. Its exact original timing cause remains unproven; bounded boot-delivery/full startup-timing acceptance remains open. These intermediate failures and the checkpoint runs are preserved.

## October 31: map preset parity, live browsing scope and Vulkan initial-surface acknowledgment

- Three new tests against the actual app `MapSelection` reproduced missing first partial days in 7/30-day views and inclusion of future records in Today/All. Compiling the current Swift `MapDatePreset` produces **540** membership ranges across nine zones. Android now matches all five built-ins, including DST gaps/overlaps, half-hour DST, skipped dates, partial-day date-line reversals and pre-1970 references. Database bounds retain millisecond membership while translating closed Swift ranges to half-open windows. All's lower bound matches Foundation's distant-past reference. No history rows or export/import contracts changed.
- Kept failed arithmetic/reference probes: naive Unix-double conversion erased Yesterday's `nextDown` boundary; the final generator compares actual `Date` values at database millisecond instants. Java GregorianCalendar subtraction differed for Apia; an intermediate offset normalization differed for Havana and Kwajalein. The final offset/day calculation matches every executed fixture. Source checks cover the selected enum/generator only; the frozen baseline and other workflow gates are unchanged.
- The model's clock boundary resolves again on real Room invalidation, preserving new fixes as a rolling window moves. Its existing 30-second cadence also admits newly current rows without a database write. Selection/retry identity keeps those updates from showing Loading or resetting map controls. Custom/outing bounds stay fixed; automatic-photo All scope stays all-history and does not receive forced scans on every map clock tick. Three real-Room model cases cover edge membership, future exclusion, new fixes, unchanged loading count, periodic refresh and fixed custom/outing behavior.
- A production photo-directory test reproduced dismissal when the map clock advanced. The map now retains the opening photo range during browsing, including validation and directory ownership. Close applies the newest range; explicit filter changes and eligibility/source loss still dismiss. - The first native integration group passed its range/map cases, then stalled launching a real MainActivity. Scoped JVM stacks put Main in MapLibre `onWindowResize` and the render thread waiting in `guardedRun`. Both referred to the same renderer with `hasSurface=true`, `waitingForSurface=false`, `graphicsSurfaceCreated=false` and `requestRender=true`. The [pinned Vulkan loop](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/maplibre/maplibre-native/android-v13.4.1/platform/android/MapLibreAndroid/src/vulkan/java/org/maplibre/android/maps/renderer/surfaceview/MapLibreVulkanSurfaceView.java) and the actual published AAR bytecode confirm a first surface can arrive before the thread acknowledges its initial empty-surface state, leaving resize waiting forever. This is separate from the earlier actor-query crash.
- Holding the real Vulkan-thread entry for **2.5 seconds** deterministically reproduced the same deadlock/flags. The shared view now establishes the SDK's empty-surface acknowledgment before attachment, while a real graphics surface is absent. Both journal and picker real-input/resume cases pass under the identical hold. The original actor-creation hold with early-input suppression also still passes. This app-side workaround is specific to the pinned 13.4.1 Vulkan startup path; native binaries/dependencies are unchanged and upgrades must re-evaluate it. Arbitrary reparenting, other SDK failures and physical GPU/OEM behavior remain unverified. The positively diagnosed stalled QA processes were force-stopped only after preserving evidence and detaching/resuming each debugger; original Gradle handles then terminated. No global process/device restart was used.

## October 31: precise visit timestamp editing and local-time resolution

- Three native regressions reproduced actual old-control defects: the default calendar rejected 1850; unchanged date Apply changed `2026-11-01T06:30:45.125Z` to `05:30:45.125Z` during New York's repeated hour; unchanged time Apply additionally discarded precision, returning `05:30:00Z`. The new resolver enumerates the selected zone's actual valid offsets instead of silently normalizing a local timestamp. It retains the original valid offset and seconds/fractions, rejects gaps/skipped dates/storage overflow, and exposes both repeated-hour occurrences with UTC offsets. The durable history contract remains millisecond storage; no schema or public import/export format changed.
- Opening snapshots the original instant, phone zone and edit part. Pending entry/occurrence survives saved-state restoration; a changed default zone does not reinterpret the dialog. Cancel discards edits, replacement/disabled owner properties dismiss it, and Apply checks the latest composed owner. Native controls expose contextual date/time labels, selectable occurrence groups and polite validation feedback. Calendar entry supports 1–9999; strict ISO entry covers year zero/BCE/expanded years within storage bounds. Numeric hour/minute fields follow 12/24-hour conventions, validate complete input and cannot apply or switch modes through forced disabled semantic actions. Changing a date or hours/minutes retains the other clock components and existing fractional precision.

The manual alternative uses strict ISO entry rather than the SDK's numeric-date parser. Initial captures and weaker probes remain separately labeled; they are not final visual evidence.
- A separate real-dial test reproduced a 4:30 edit reverting to 1:30 when live density changed to 1.8. Switching to validated large-text fields now transfers the edited dial value. Its exact callback retains the intended hour, seconds and milliseconds. This test uses a controlled composition-density transition; it is distinct from the full actual system-font run.
