## October 31: Check In/Check Out automation and writer cancellation

- Added current-source manual-visit commands: `checkIn`, `checkOut`, `savedPlaces` and `openManualVisits`. Saved places resolve at commit; explicit coordinates and saved destinations do not request GPS. Current-location check-in requires the command Activity to remain visible and already hold location access, with one cancellable 12-second request, at most 30-second age and 100 m accuracy. No permission prompt, recording start or paid tracking gate is introduced. Android retains its approved free-tracking policy rather than the newer iOS tracking entitlement policy.
- Separate `places` and `manualVisits` scopes default off for existing approvals. English/Spanish controls disclose their data and mutation access. All nine selections survive saved-state restoration without restoring consent. The existing identity-bound review, expiry, exact-recipient read-only grants and revocation boundary apply to the new commands. Checkout requires an explicitly selected or unique open manual visit; ambiguous, closed, missing and overlapping cases return bounded typed errors. Results omit coordinates and notes.
- The repository checks original instants before millisecond conversion and selects/validates/writes in one transaction. The actual selected Swift manual-overlap predicate generated 18 reference cases, including adjacent/equal/zero-length/open intervals and `Date.distantFuture`. Native Room comparisons and concurrent-repository admission pass. The native visit editor still needs its own overlap review/explicit-save workflow; this command increment does not certify that separate UI.
- A queued canceled check-in regression reproduced one committed visit after caller cancellation. Room's pooled writer callback had substituted its database Job. The shared writer seam now retains the caller Job with the confined transaction context, matching the reader seam. Native checks prove queued/active and nested cancellation rollback, while explicit NonCancellable cleanup can still commit. A canceled pool waiter may remain queued until the held writer is released; no immediate pool-wait cancellation guarantee is claimed.
- Current evidence: **192 regular data cases plus the separately run fresh-process upgrade case**, all passing. The upgrade fixture explicitly requires its own process; an initial combined invocation failed its fixture precondition and is retained as diagnostic evidence. **44 app cases** pass for cross-UID transport, English/Spanish scopes and review, clear-data and settings graph/barrier integration. The new foreign-UID flow creates two explicitly overlapping visits, rejects ambiguous checkout, selects an ID, finishes the unique remainder, rejects repeated checkout, and resolves a saved place without starting recording. **22 additional consent/localization cases** pass with the system font at 1.8× (one existing controlled-density case explicitly uses Compose 1.6×). The final **317-case platform group** also passes with no failures, errors or skips.
- CI now verifies manual-overlap fixture provenance and includes the foreground-location tests. Changes to the current iOS manual-store/intent sources trigger Android CI. All 1,218 localized resources and seven converter tests pass. Source, native XML, red/green cancellation evidence and diagnostic runs are retained under ignored `captures/manual-automation-20261031/`. Only Whereday's API 35 QA AVD and disposable test/client packages were used; the reserved Pixel and GitSync AVD were not operated.
- Actual Automate flow acceptance, native-editor overlap reconciliation, complete current-source parity, physical/OEM/battery/accessibility and all production purchase/provider/analytics/signing/store/Wear gates remain open. This entry does not mark a plan phase or the full goal complete.

Real document-provider identity tests now select only their own synthetic images and exercise MediaDocumentsProvider and ExternalStorageProvider with each provider's actual grant; they no longer ask reverse conversion to borrow an unrelated provider grant. The synthetic paging provider now owns per-test counters and idempotent cursor disposal; a new case proves late/repeated closes cannot charge a newer probe. ## November 1: native manual-visit overlap review and checkout

- The native editor now rejects a new manual visit or changed manual interval that overlaps stored history until the user explicitly chooses Save Anyway. The transaction excludes the edited visit itself, permits adjacent boundaries and uses the already compiled current Swift overlap references. Notes/place-only edits can retain an existing intentional overlap; detected/imported time edits retain their prior contract. Explicit admission still enforces the stale-visit and saved-place snapshots. Reusable-place and visit writes remain atomic. Original future times are rejected before storage rounding, and an existing visit's source cannot be changed through this editor boundary.
- A review owns an immutable candidate and draft/baseline/reuse key. Cancellation, replacement, same-frame edits, backgrounding, disposal and saved-state restoration cannot reuse an old confirmation. Double delivery saves once. A current-location draft must still hold a usable fix at confirmation. Separate Check Out closes the stored open manual visit at now, clears its inferred-departure flag and preserves stored metadata; the form explains that unsaved draft edits are not included. The dialog closes after success, following Android's existing editor navigation. No route start/stop, lookup, purchase or provider request is introduced.
- New persistence regressions reproduced silent overlapping creation and time edits before the correction. The full regular persistence group now passes **201 cases**, including nine new overlap cases and all 18 selected Swift predicate comparisons. One existing reusable-place fixture deliberately keeps duplicate intervals and now explicitly admits that overlap; its preservation/retry assertions remain intact. A malformed new fixture initially failed its domain constructor before reaching the repository and was corrected to exercise the sub-millisecond future boundary.
- The complete original **34-case native editor group** passes after temporary diagnostics were removed. One correction/restoration fixture intermittently timed out after a native Save tap. Isolated, class, paired, five-cycle and 12-case probes passed; delivered callbacks passed all live guards and repository commits. The fixture now synchronizes the actual Android dialog/keyboard before Save, following the existing timestamp integration fixture, while retaining the original five-second dismissal and metadata assertions. The original complete group passes with product guards unchanged. The exact cause of the earlier intermittent tap is not certified by these probes; diagnostics remain separate from accepted results.
- Initial emulator evidence includes an interrupted instrumentation process and rendering stalls; a fresh AVD also had a Settings-provider ANR before Whereday was installed, then recovered to a 54 ms Settings read. A separately named, task-owned `whereday-visit-overlap-api35-20261031` AVD was created from the installed API 35 image. The runner verified APK and target identities, exact selected cases, and removal of both disposable app packages. Final standalone GPS, upgrade and linked-command suites subsequently passed as recorded below.

It is now explicit in the phone plan and requirement table; this editor increment does not implement it or close complete source parity, physical/OEM/battery/accessibility, production integration, signing/store or Wear OS gates. The full goal remains active.

Native alignment/provenance and exact notice-package checks pass; legal review and production signing remain open.

## November 1: user-reviewed calendar event drafts

- Added Add event to calendar from timeline visit/outing rows, the saved-visit editor and exact/inferred outing details. The Android event-insert intent carries only title, exact start/end instants and optional location; open intervals capture the click time, and ends are clamped at the start. Locations prefer a trimmed nonblank visit address, then name, then six-decimal POSIX coordinates. Outings use the complete route's first point, including an outlier, independently of filtered map samples; unnamed outings preserve their chronological sequence title. No notes, audit data, attendees or journal identifiers are shared. The external editor owns user review/save/cancel; Whereday neither requests calendar permissions nor writes calendar rows or journal history. Unsaved visit drafts have no calendar action; saved-visit editing explicitly explains that unsaved edits are excluded. Android event location is text, with no separate EventKit structured geographic field.
- Native tests intercept the actual Android intent handoff before an external app launches. The unchanged regression drove a keyed button lifetime correction; the failed original run remains diagnostic evidence. - The unchanged source-replacement regression passes after the keyed lifetime fix. ## November 1: Google Timeline import integration

- Added offline Google Location History, Semantic Timeline and Device Timeline JSON through the existing free, cancellable history picker and private review workspace, including top-level device arrays. ## November 1: joint existing/source import admission

- The next current-source review exposed a Google data-selection bug beyond the earlier parser/duplicate predicate checks. With one existing visit and three source candidates that match nontransitively, source-only staging dropped the second candidate and committed the third. The actual native assertion reproduced `18.00144` instead of `18.00072`; the original failing log, APK/source hashes and source snapshots are preserved. The unchanged regression passes after the correction. No owner history was used.
- Replaced destructive source-only pruning with a private advisory admission index. Preview first checks each chronological candidate against the journal snapshot, then compares unmatched rows with source rows already admitted to that preview. The complete candidate stream remains sealed. Commit traverses it chronologically against fresh history inside its write transaction; candidates rejected by preview can therefore be admitted after a later journal change. Existing notes, corrections, IDs and session links remain protected. Google commit Kept counts now include duplicates encountered in the retained source stream, matching the tested fresh-admission outcomes.
- Chronology metadata is included in the review checksum, validated against decoded record times and checked for completeness. A real private-index mutation after review is rejected before any history write. Selection checks cancellation while skipping unselected pages. Private admission writes run in synchronous bounded SQLite batches, outside suspended journal queries; no scratch transaction is held across a coroutine suspension. Whereday's existing identity/replace path, journal schema and product/provider policies remain unchanged.
- Added a full-source macOS SwiftData harness covering seven actual preview/commit scenarios: Google Device/Semantic visits, raw points, Whereday JSON visits/points and late journal inserts for Google visits/points. It verifies read-only previews, new/duplicate counts, the exact admitted location and preservation of existing values. Only ExportFormat is extracted from its UIKit-dependent file. An initial harness compile caught a missing try on fetchCount; the corrected harness passes all seven. Two Whereday scenarios document the remaining Android semantic-import gap and are not counted as Android parity acceptance. The macOS CI job now executes this harness and preserves its source-hashed report; remote CI has not run.
- Final scoped native acceptance passes **33 persistence cases**, **29 import/link UI cases** and **one Spanish review repeat at actual font scale 1.8 with dark appearance**: **63 executions across 62 unique cases**, zero failures/errors/skips. The original focused red/green pair is retained separately and excluded from final totals. ## November 1: bounded country-geocoder requests

- Source review found that Country Days' legacy Geocoder executor used an unbounded queue. Coroutine cancellation did not remove its queued native work, and modern callbacks had no unresolved-request cap. Extracting the existing transport behavior reproduced three real regressions: canceled queued work still reached the provider (three calls instead of two), excess legacy work waited instead of rejecting, and canceled modern requests allowed more native starts. The original source, failed XML and log are retained.
- Replaced that transport with one legacy worker/one queued slot, removable canceled FutureTasks and prompt admission failure. Running Binder calls are not interrupted. Modern country lookups admit at most four unresolved callbacks; canceled/timed-out callers keep their slot until actual completion/failure. Atomic completion prevents duplicate/late callbacks from releasing another request's capacity. Failures are sanitized without provider details. SystemPlaceLookup's existing independent bounds, country results/cache version, time-zone lookup, storage schema and product/provider configuration are unchanged.
- Six shared provider-free regressions pass on the JVM and isolated Android API 35 runtime, with zero failures/errors/skips. Two initial privacy fixture assertions assumed a null cause; coroutine stack recovery adds a sanitized IOException wrapper. The corrected checks examine the bounded cause chain for sanitized messages/types, retaining the privacy requirement. No production assertion or timeout was relaxed. Shared test sources avoid separate drifting host/device copies; CI selects the Android class, while remote CI remains unrun.
- Current host suites pass **1,477 regular cases**: 366 domain, 943 export, 138 platform and 30 app. Debug/QA/release APKs, native test APKs and unsigned AAB build. Full debug/release lint retains all detectors and reports zero errors, 106 warnings and four informational findings per variant. Native page/provenance and APK/AAB notice checks pass with the same eight 64-bit aligned libraries, sixteen files/four owners, and 116 software/97 embedded/38 packaged notice files; legal review remains incomplete. The earlier 93-case import/shared-inspection acceptance belongs to its recorded build; those unchanged source paths were not rerun in this transport-only increment.
- Actual API 29–32 framework/provider behavior, modern service-death recovery and physical/OS/OEM testing remain open. These tests make no real geocoding request and do not establish geographic accuracy or release performance. Source/build/native proofs and original failures are under ignored `captures/geocoder-bounds-20261101/`. Cleanup verifies no disposable QA packages or test media and original 1.0/null/null font/clock/stylus values. The owned emulator exited zero after graceful shutdown; only its exact private ADB server was stopped and both PIDs were observed absent; the Pixel and other AVDs/shared ADB remain untouched. Full current-source/adaptive layout, production integration, field/accessibility/legal/signing/store and deferred Wear OS gates keep the complete plan active.

## November 1: recorded GPS-point inspection — implementation and scoped QA

- Implemented read-only point selection in the shared native journal map. Actual 48 dp taps choose the nearest projected point with stable ties; the same tap toggles selection, and true blank-map taps dismiss it. Photo and visit hits retain precedence. A native highlight distinguishes selected ordinary/outlier points. The optional text chooser is limited to currently displayed bounded map data and blocks incoming visit links while open.
- A selectable-text panel shows phone-local timestamp with seconds, five-decimal coordinates, accuracy, optional altitude/speed and outlier status. Metric/imperial formatting follows the unit preference. The saved state contains only an ID; visible data supplies fresh metadata. Removal/hiding, explicit date-selection scope changes and saved-visit focus clear it. This adds no journal writes, location request or network access. The current Swift marker, tooltip and selection fragments were source-reviewed and hashed; no executed SwiftUI, exact pixel/motion or viewport-sampler equivalence is claimed.

## November 1: fold-aware primary/companion layout — first implementation stage

- The real shell now pairs Map and Timeline at expanded available width or usable separating folds, retaining the selected primary tab. WindowManager 1.5.1 supplies window-local fold bounds; the layout translates them to its actual origin. A 24 dp minimum exclusion band avoids separating/occluding book/tabletop folds, including zero-width creases; insufficient space chooses one safe region. Leading/top primary order respects RTL. The entire primary Scaffold and companion chrome are measured inside safe rectangles. Export/Settings remain independent. Pane controls wait for the first known report instead of briefly drawing full-width across an unknown hinge.
- A single movable composition per screen preserves live owners during primary/companion swaps, with the existing keyed saveable-state holder retaining compactly removed content. Native tests verify the same actual MapView survives role swaps. Paired visit selection calls the existing day/pin focus and shared editor; Show on map updates the companion without leaving Timeline's outing navigation. ## November 2: inline visit inspector and approved launch scope

- The owner explicitly includes the adaptive workspace at launch. Compact-first deferral is no longer an open product choice; the implementation plan and acceptance gate reflect that requirement.
- One shared visit editor now renders inline in the Timeline region of paired layouts and in its existing compact dialog. Covered Timeline content leaves composition. Editor fields use a saveable holder inside movable content: the initial native regression reproduced loss of pending invalid ISO date entry during handoff, and the fix preserves that nested draft through handoff and saved-state restoration.
- Same-visit selection preserves the opening baseline for stale-record rejection. A different map visit queues until the current editor closes and then reads a fresh Room snapshot. External visit links wait behind the same owner. Both manual creation controls are disabled during inspection. A settled screenshot regression reproduced 297 enabled-primary-color pixels in a disabled label despite correct native input guards; explicit semantic disabled text colors correct that painting issue.
- Final current QA artifacts pass **61 unique native cases plus 11 expanded-layout repeats (72 executions)**, including eleven inspector cases, workspace shell/frame, shared visit inspection, editor restoration, visit links/activity and map focus. The expanded run uses actual 2200×1600/density-200 display overrides, then restores physical 1080×2400/density 420. Inspector handoff itself is driven by test density/state restoration; this does not establish real activity/window transition acceptance. Earlier passes and original failures are retained separately under ignored `captures/inline-inspector-20261101/` and excluded from that final total.

## November 2: visit dialogs constrained to safe panes

Dialogs translate those coordinates into their own native window and intersect system/keyboard insets; full-page pickers use the available pane, while reviews stay bounded and centered. Standard compact dialogs retain their original Material presentation.
- Root visit-link presentations now live in a stable modal slot carrying primary-region bounds. No modal controls are exposed before posture/origin is known. The host retains the native modal/scrim and Back policy; outside taps dismiss without activating the underlying pane. Saveable dialog bodies preserve nested state across compact/pane changes. ## November 2: remaining modal callers, root states and framework clocks

- Migrated remaining alert/calendar/full-page dialog callers for map/date/point/follow, photo/replay/outing, saved places/country stays, settings/clear/privacy/support, import/export, automation and webhooks. Added pane menus and bottom-aligned specialized export delivery sheets. The final source inventory records **60** owned call sites and no direct application-owned alert/dialog/calendar/menu/sheet/native-clock windows outside the shared host. OS permission, picker, chooser, Play and other external activities remain system-owned UI. No dependency or schema change was introduced.
- Loading, onboarding and clear recovery now use a safe single-region frame. Root permission reviews have a separate empty, untagged modal frame; it does not duplicate the journal's semantic pane targets. Launcher confirmations moved into the stable primary modal slot before visit-link handling. Their existing approval/admission/generation/lifecycle guards remain the action owners.
- Both scheduling and export time-of-day controls now use the same Android framework TimePicker widget inside a shared owned window, retaining draft hour/minute across presentation changes. Scheduling preserves its single-use/current-settings/foreground fences and closes a pending clock on Stop. Export time entry rejects changed/disabled/background/disposed owners, closes stale drafts, and uses the latest callback so unrelated filter changes are preserved. Its ordinary native overnight-range test and scheduling's framework-widget test target the new explicit confirmation controls instead of a removed native-dialog button ID.
- Ten new actual caller cases cover map layers/point chooser, tabletop range/invalid ISO, replay close/scrub without history mutation, synthetic unavailable-photo pager, saved-place dirty review, root permission/launcher/onboarding, choice selection and export sheet close without preparation. Three actual-density clock cases cover explicit hour/minute apply, cancel/background-stale approval and changed-time/unrelated-filter fences. An eight-case intermediate pass followed correction of three fixture errors (unsupported scroll-to targets and assertion before replay's committed close callback); no production behavior was altered to make those fixtures pass. Thirteen final-artifact wide smoke cases then passed. Failures and intermediate evidence remain separately retained.
- Expanded CI acceptance is authored in `scripts/test_expanded_workspace_ci.sh`: it refuses non-GitHub execution, physical/multiple devices, non-emulators or preexisting display overrides; targets the sole CI emulator, restores display settings on exit and preserves its results. Workflow/actionlint and shell syntax checks pass; remote CI remains unrun.
- The broad migration run passed **374 executions / 347 unique cases** (90 core, 76 map/photo/replay, 178 settings/export and 30 expanded). **That is the pre-clock-correction APK**, and does not prove final clock acceptance. Subsequent review caught two actual defects: invalid native numeric input committed one settings update, and the landscape dial was clipped (native measured width 280 px, visible width 209 px). Both failures reproduce in native tests; raw original evidence and eleven pre-correction captures remain separate. An earlier blank-input fixture assumption was corrected because the framework accepts its existing hints; the actual regression uses invalid 99-hour/71-minute input and waits for native mode animation before editing.
- The final clock host calls the public framework validator before committing, clears focus and reads the native selected values only after successful validation. Invalid input keeps the editor open without consuming approval. It inflates the framework's vertical clock layout in a theme-preserving, widget-only portrait configuration; the Activity/window/folding layout is unchanged. See Android's [TimePicker validation API](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/widget/TimePicker#validateInput()) and [context configuration override contract](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/view/ContextThemeWrapper#applyOverrideConfiguration(android.content.res.Configuration)). Dial, keyboard, AM/PM/24-hour controls remain native.
- **Current final APKs pass 62 focused native cases**: all 13 scheduling and 17 export-options cases in compact presentation, then 32 actual-density expanded cases (ten actual callers, five framework-clock cases, six generic dialog cases and eleven inspector cases). These include the two reproduced failures, successful correction after invalid entry, stale/background approval rejection and unrelated-filter preservation. This is the post-correction scope; the other broad groups were not repeated after the clock-only fix. Display overrides were restored after both red and accepted runs.
- All twelve final synthetic light-mode 2200×1600 captures were exported, hashed and visually reviewed: map layers, tabletop range calendar, replay, unavailable-photo pager, saved-place discard, root permission/launcher/onboarding, choice menu, export delivery sheet, full clock dial and native invalid-time error. - Final debug/QA/release APK, QA test APK and release AAB assembly and **41 app host tests** pass. Debug/release lint report zero errors, 106 warnings and four informational findings each. Localization verifies **1,263** resources; seven converter tests, actionlint and CI shell syntax pass. Current package/notices/native verification and cleanup are recorded below. ## November 2: complete-source viewport GPS dots and selection ownership

- Added a streaming viewport sampler that matches the current Swift `MapPointMarkerSampler`: one first-in-route point per 48-unit projected grid cell, at most 160 markers, selected-point precedence, stable route order and date-line wrapping. Android converts native screen pixels to dp. Unknown/invalid camera or layout bounds produce no dots. The unchanged Swift enum executes in a MapKit harness against **27 cases**; Kotlin matches every output. The harness adapts only the point model fields used by the enum and preserves actor isolation. Source/harness/generator/input provenance is checked in CI; the frozen iOS baseline is unchanged.
- Interactive markers now have a complete source independent of the route's uniform display sample. Main and exact/inferred outing maps read indexed 500-point pages under one reader snapshot. Outlier selection/all-outlier fallback and exact-session exclusions precede cell admission. The selected eligible record supplies current metadata even when absent from the route sample or outside the current viewport. Replay reads the complete archived prefix, extends a bounded marker cache during forward playback, rebuilds after reverse seeks/viewport/selection changes and clears it on close. Paths, endpoints, statistics and stored/exported coordinates retain their prior sources.
- Marker loads are scoped to source, viewport, selection, replay position, visibility, lifecycle and retry generation. Obsolete/canceled emissions cannot publish into a replacement owner. Backgrounding/hiding clears rendered markers; errors expose localized feedback and explicit retry instead of quietly drawing the route sample as complete markers. Added three English/Spanish strings; localization verifies **1,266 resources**, with all seven converter tests passing. No dependency, permission or schema change was introduced.
- Native probes reproduced two real selection defects: an excluded outlier returned after a rapid filter round trip while its excluded read remained pending, and the native map listener wrote to the old saved-state owner after a scope replacement. A transient ID/outlier eligibility hint now dismisses an excluded inspection without waiting for IO and never supplies displayed/saved metadata; the ordinary-selection control retains its selected point. Native point/photo/visit callbacks use the latest lambda for the current owner. An initial function-reference handoff still failed the owner test; changing it to the updated lambda closes the defect. The unchanged three-case probe fails two cases before correction and passes all three afterward. Original failures, intermediate results and compiled callback evidence remain separate diagnostics.
- Test-fixture diagnostics are not product failures: two first database assertions shared the default persistent test database; explicit in-memory databases isolate them. A native camera initially put both test points outside the viewport (the unique point projected to negative screen coordinates); controlled geographic fit corrects its premise. Native source-query geometry was incorrectly treated as an exact uploaded MultiLineString vertex count; acceptance now separately verifies the unchanged 1,600-point route presentation and visible native path. A text assertion needed substring matching for the localized accuracy label. Three older chooser tests now wait for a rendered viewport and visible chooser; restoration and stale-data assertions remain intact. The expanded shell test also requires its explicit `expectedWorkspaceExpanded=true` argument. Failed/intermediate runs are excluded from final acceptance counts.
- The million-point synthetic disk case passes: the new reader finds the non-uniformly-sampled point and selected record, returns two markers for this two-cell fixture, retains one million records and leaves the 2,500-point route sample/statistics unchanged. Its marker read takes **2,483 ms** on the owned API 35 ARM64 debug emulator. The separate JVM million-point stream checks the grid cap with many occupied cells. Neither measurement establishes physical/release CPU, heap, frame-time, battery or latency budgets.
- Final debug/QA/test and unsigned release APK/AAB builds pass, as do **369 domain + 47 app host tests**. Debug/release lint each reports zero errors, 106 warnings and four informational findings. CI includes the new UI cases and provenance check; a separate synthetic disk-marker scale job keeps that million-point fixture out of the normal database selection. Remote CI remains unexecuted for this revision. Native packaging/legal checks and final device scope are recorded with the evidence below; full source/adaptive, production integrations and release acceptance remain incomplete.
- Final current-artifact native acceptance passes **136 executions across 113 unique cases**, with zero failures/errors/skips: nine new source/rendering cases, 91 affected compact map/photo/replay/inspection/navigation cases, 23 actual-density expanded cases, twelve repository/replay cases and the one-million-point disk case. All three final application groups match the same current QA APK pair; both library groups match the current isolated data-test APK. Original failures, fixture probes and earlier broad runs are excluded from this count.
- All four final synthetic light-mode captures were exported, hashed and visually reviewed: bounded dots over the dense route and the fresh ±42 m unsampled-point inspector in compact and 2200×1600/density-200 windows. Controls and metadata fit; the path remains visible. The component harness omits root system-bar styling, leaving low-contrast status icons in these images. They are component fixtures, not whole-app release assets or complete motion/theme/font/RTL/TalkBack/physical GPU acceptance. Earlier seven diagnostic/pre-correction captures remain separate evidence.
- Final packaged notices verify 118 software entries, 99 embedded entries and 38 exact files in both QA APK and release AAB. Eight 64-bit QA native libraries pass 16 KB alignment; release native provenance matches 16 files/four owners. Legal acceptance remains incomplete. Cleanup verifies eleven exported/hash-checked synthetic capture URIs removed, app/data/platform QA packages absent, empty test media, original display/font/clock/stylus/IME settings restored and both exact owned emulator/private-ADB PIDs absent after graceful shutdown. The emulator session exits zero. Pixel, other AVDs/shared ADB and owner history were untouched.
- Evidence is under ignored `captures/point-marker-sampling-20261102/`, including final/probe/failed native results, source snapshots, executed reference fixtures, source/artifact hashes, verification summary and checksums. No phase or overall goal is complete. Remaining whole-source/adaptive/actual-window/motion/accessibility, configured provider/Play/analytics, real calendar/Automate, physical/OEM/battery/photo/performance, legal/signing/remote-CI/store and post-phone Wear requirements remain open. ## November 2: export date-review restoration and current Apply ownership

- Real MainActivity baselines reproduced loss of an Export ISO review after configuration recreation and a held Apply writing while stopped. ## November 2: map photo-directory ownership and bounded restoration

- Source review found memory-only directory requests, held ranges, selected places/photos and cursor trails. Map browsers now save a logical opening with selection epoch, held range, place/area scope, bounded directory/collection cursor pairs and nested current-photo URI. Drafts live above transient metadata loading and do not retain a directory, bitmap, native marker, PagingSource or cache handle. Data is reacquired before use; current directory metadata supplies counts and place details. A pending opening has a shared safe-pane loading window with Back/Close and expired loading-control guards. Closing/unrestorable openings release the held range. Configuration recreation retains logical data; ordinary stops/disposal dismiss the opening.
- Saved numeric IDs are bound to a streaming digest of the complete input and generated photo-to-place membership, place counts/centroids/times/representatives and area mapping, plus preparation parameters/range. This catches reused IDs and changed clustering output even for identical inputs; it identifies directory metadata/mapping rather than original image bytes. The photo-selection epoch separately invalidates older reset generations. Native marker/thumbnail owner fences remain intact.
- Pagination saves two page/cursor pairs instead of an unbounded trail. Previous boundaries use scoped keyset queries, and restored cursors must have the expected rank/boundary in the new directory before content is shown. Query results are bound to their cursor, repeated stale Next boundaries do not advance again, and backward IO admits one operation with a current-state check. Nested viewers resolve their saved URI inside the matching place and return to the retained collection page. The generic Context configuration-change helper was renamed for use by photo and existing clock owners; clock behavior is unchanged. No journal schema, permission, dependency, photo-correlation or provider/purchase policy changed.
- Final acceptance passes **114 native executions across 112 unique cases**, zero failures/errors/skips: seventeen directory backend cases, twenty-three actual MainActivity recreation cases, the two directory Activity cases repeated at actual 1.8× system text, forty-nine photo/picker/paging/browser/map/localization/restoration/removal cases, twenty-one expanded pane-dialog cases and two real keyboard cases. Six proof groups match the final QA app/test/data-test APK hashes. New component cases rebuild equal directories, reject a changed photo with a reused numeric place ID, retain collection page/current URI, and preserve held range/page through live-clock advance and restoration without resurrecting a failed opening. Backend cases verify identity stability/reuse rejection and scoped previous/restored boundaries.
- Real Activity cases use fifty-two task-owned cached JPEGs with capture-time/GPS metadata: one distinct-place directory retains page 2 and place/back navigation through both size directions; one collection retains photo 52 of 52, then returns to page 2 and can navigate backward. Existing visits, points and outing records remain unchanged. The fixture now spaces capture instants within the synthetic outing, including millisecond EXIF precision, and can generate either shared or distinct photo coordinates. These are isolated synthetic references, not real-library permission or original-photo acceptance.
- All **78 app host tests** pass, including six new opening/epoch/nested-draft/bounded-cursor/range/corruption checks. Debug/QA/release APKs, QA/data test APKs and unsigned AAB build. Full debug/release lint retains zero errors, 107 warnings and four informational findings. Three selected Swift photo fixture provenance checks, 1,266 localized resources, seven converter cases, three isolated-runner cases, shell syntax and actionlint pass. CI selects PhotoDirectoryRestorationTest and the existing isolated resize class includes its two new Activity cases. Remote CI and unrelated complete module suites remain unrun.
